===PAGE 6===
 Introduction
5
What Does 
This Book 
Contain?
Pages, pictures and print, clearly, but in terms of its 
organisation, the chapters are as follows:
Introduction
Which you are currently reading. The introduction provides 
an overview of Judge Dredd’s universe for beginners and 
old hands alike. 
Chapter One – Academy of Law
Creating a Judge character – everything needed to graduate from 
the toughest academy on earth and hit the streets with purpose.
Chapter Two – New Skills and 
Special Techniques
New skills and Special Techniques speciﬁ c to Judge Dredd.
Chapter Three – The Justice 
Department
The structure and workings of the Justice Department, 
including details on the separate divisions and game rules 
pertinent to sections such as Psi.
Chapter Four – I Am the Law!
Applying the law in Mega-City One – crimes, sentences and 
punishments.
Chapter Five – Equipment
Equipment – both speciﬁ c to Judges and to Mega-City One 
citizens.
Chapter Six – Megways and 
SkedWays
Vehicles of Mega-City One, plus information on the complex 
road systems running through the city.
Chapter Seven – Mega-City One 
Guided Tour
A deep-dive into the city, including details on Sectors, City 
Blocks, landmarks and a system for creating your own 
areas within Mega-City One. This chapter also provides an 
overview of the Cursed Earth and Black Atlantic.
Chapter Eight – Thought 
Crimes
Psi Division, psykers, new psionic talents and powers, and a 
range of psionic equipment.
Chapter Nine – Crazies, 
Fatties, Muties and Creeps
A guide to the different character types and crazes found 
in Mega-City One, along with game statistics and rules for 
bringing these into Judge Dredd campaigns.
Chapter Ten – Robots
Rules for creating robots and droids in the Judge Dredd 
universe, along with sample Mega-City One mechanical 
men.
Chapter Eleven – MAC’s 
Databanks
Resources for Referees – a glossary of Mega-City One 
terms and jargon; a timeline for the Judge Dredd saga and 
advice on running a Judge Dredd campaign.
Chapter Twelve – Sector 13
A detailed look at a single Sector – Sector 13 – complete with 
Sector House personalities, sector history, perps, gangs and 
plot seeds. 
The vast sprawl of Mega-City One means that crime is endemic 
and handling it a constant strain on resources. During the 21
st 
Century, as the Mega Cities developed, it became apparent 
that the old division between the state, law enforcement and 
the judicial process was simply no longer expedient or efﬁ cient: 
new ways required new means of keeping crime under control 
and punishing those that commit it.
The solution was the Judge system. Highly trained individuals 
with the power to apprehend offenders and sentence them on 
the spot, thereby massively reducing the reliance on a system 
of courts, lawyers and appeals. Concentrating such power 
into the hands of a few brought new concerns, principally 
regarding civil liberties and free speech but, generally, it was 
recognised that only justice or liberty could prevail; and, in 
the teem and madness of the Mega Cities, justice had to be 
the foremost concern. In those cities where a Judge system 
exists, the Justice Departments are the state, the judiciary, 
the legislature and the police, all wrapped into one.
And foremost amongst these Judges is Joe Dredd.

===PAGE 7===
 Introduction
6
Who Is
Judge Dredd?
6
  Introduction

===PAGE 8===
 Introduction
7
Dredd is the hardest, sternest, most effective, most dedicated 
Judge patrolling Mega-City One. His absolute passion for 
the application of justice makes him crime’s most formidable 
opponent and he embodies the unique skills and training 
all Judges possess in pursuit of justice. Dredd is, himself, 
a clone; a replication of Chief Judge Fargo, the father of the 
Judge system. He (along with his clone brother Rico) had his 
growth artiﬁ cially accelerated so that, in 2066, he emerged 
with an apparent physiological age of 5, complete with all the 
appropriate knowledge for his age computer encoded into 
his brain during the cloning process. The name Dredd was 
chosen by the genetic scientist who created him, Morton 
Judd, in order to instil fear and respect in the population. In 
every sense of the word Dredd was born to be a Judge; it is 
in his DNA and his reason for being. He was created to be 
the best and he has lived-up to that promise.
And this summation of justice in Mega-City One perfectly 
encapsulates what the Judges are about. In a city of some 
400 million people, covering much of the eastern seaboard 
of North America, order, discipline and control have to be 
enforced, ruthlessly, if civilisation is to progress. Individual 
freedoms are limited and punishment for transgressing the 
law is dealt swiftly and without much in the way of mercy.
But the Judges are not merciless. The pace of life in Mega-
City One creates a condition known as Future Shock and 
those who succumb to it are known as Futsies. The Judges 
recognise, and deal compassionately with, the strains 
the city imposes. The Judges are the fabric of Mega-City 
One, holding it together, preventing society from complete, 
devastating meltdown. Judge Dredd is the epitome of that 
fabric, woven from its cloth. He is The Law.
Who Are You?
Characters in Judge Dredd are Judges. Chapter One takes 
you through character creation and, at the end of it, you will 
have a Judge, either straight from the Academy of Law or 
a seasoned lawman who has served years on the streets. 
Later supplements for Judge Dredd will deal with playing 
citizens, mutants, aliens and, naturally enough, criminals 
(known as perps – short for perpetrators); but the focus of 
this book is four-square on the Judges.
It will be the task of you and your fellow Judges to follow in the 
footsteps of Judge Dredd, patrolling the streets, preventing 
and judging crime, handling disorder and becoming involved 
in the bizarre and unpredictable events that regularly af ﬂ ict 
a city the size of a country. You will ﬁ nd your characters 
dealing with mundane crimes such as littering, petty theft, 
mugging, vandalism and ‘slowing’ and the more bizarre 
and unique crimes such as organ-legging, umpty-bagging, 
boinging®, tapping and simping. They will encounter aliens 
– both from other planets and other dimensions – come into 
contact with powerful psions (and perhaps even be psions), 
face the undead, the walking dead and the unkillable. 
Anything and everything is possible in Mega-City One – and 
the Judges are the thin blue, black and red line that stands 
between these dangers and the normal citizens. It is one 
hell of a job.
Mega-City One
The urban sprawl that covers much of the eastern seaboard 
of North America, Mega-City One is the ﬁ rst – and largest – 
of the mega cities (Mega-City Two is located on the western 
Dredd’s dedication to the law is unswerving; to quote the man himself:
‘Where do I stand? I’ll tell you where I stand. i stand four-
square for justice. i stand for discipline, good order and the rigid 
application of the law - and grud help any limp-wristed liberals who 
say different. the people - they know where i stand. they need rules 
to live by - i provide them. they break the rules, i break them. that’s 
the way it works. the people like it that way. they need to know 
where they stand. rights? sure. i’m all for rights. but not at the 
expense of order. that’s why i like to see that statue of judgement 
standing there, towering over liberty. Kind of a symbol.’


===PAGE 9===
 Introduction
8
seaboard, spanning old California and beyond; Texas City 
covers the entire state of old Texas and, whilst not a mega 
city on the scale of the others, has a corrupt, Maﬁ a-run Judge 
system – until Dredd intervenes).
Mega-City One evolved during the 21
st Century, becoming 
an urban conurbation stretching from Boston to Washington 
in order to cope with the escalating population crisis facing 
the United States. Mega-City One was one of three major 
areas to survive the nuclear war of 2070, thanks to an 
experimental laser missile defence system built just before 
the war. Quickly spreading north and south, Mega-City One 
swelled to hold most of the population of the East Coast, 
reaching 800 million by the end of the 21
st Century. The 
Apocalypse War of 2104 effectively halved the population, 
devastating large tracts of the city; between then and 2131 
(the default year for Judge Dredd campaigns), the city has 
undergone considerable rebuilding and growth but has not 
yet reached the population peak of the pre-Apocalypse 
War era. Nuclear strikes during the Apocalypse War left a 
small part of the city, the North West Hab Zone, separated 
from the rest of the metropolis by a stretch of radioactive 
wasteland called Nuke Alley; North West Hab Zone is 
connected to the main city by a bridge but with a megway 
tunnel under construction.
Mega-City One is divided into 305 Sectors. Sectors 1 to 
300 constitute the main city, radiating out from the island 
of Manhattan. Sectors 301 to 305 form the North-West 
Hab Zone; Sector 301 is referred to by both Judges and 
citizens alike as ‘The Pit’ due to its incredibly high crime rate 
– excessive even by Mega-City One standards.
Beyond Mega-City One, inland is the radiation-blasted 
wasteland known as the Cursed Earth. Separated from 
the city by huge, weapon-guarded walls, the Cursed Earth 
is home to mutants, renegades, aliens and rejects who 
cannot – or will not – live within the mega cities. Largely 
lawless and therefore incredibly dangerous, Judges who 
have served-out their useful days have the option to take the 
Long Walk into the Cursed Earth, bringing law to the villainy 
that inhabits the rad-zones and countless shanty towns and 
ruined cities across the whole region. Despite its dangerous, 
treacherous nature, the Cursed Earth is used as a training 
ground for rookie Judges who undertake the infamous Hot 
Dog Runs to test their skills and capabilities whilst training at 
the Academy of Law.
Mega-City One receives extensive coverage in its own 
chapter.
2000 AD and Beyond
Judge Dredd is the creation of writer John 
Wagner and artist Carlos Ezquerra. He ﬁ rst 
appeared in Prog 2 of 2000 AD, Britain’s 
foremost science ﬁ ction comic and rapidly 
became the Top Thrill: Dredd’s saga continues, 
on a weekly basis, in 2000 AD’s pages, although 
the Judge Dredd Megazine and other spin-off 
titles offer additional outlets for his adventures. 
2000 AD appeared in 1977 and with barely a 
break in the saga, Judge Dredd has become 
one of the most extensive and detailed comic 
book and science ﬁ ction settings ever devised.
Dredd’s adventures, from his ﬁ rst appearance 
onwards, can be obtained as The Complete Case 
Files series, available from Rebellion.

===PAGE 11===
 Academy Of Law
10
There are, however, some differences necessary to re ﬂ ect 
the universe of Judge Dredd and Judges in general. These 
are explained below.
 Characteristics
Judges possess the standard Traveller characteristics of 
Strength, Endurance, Dexterity and Intelligence. Differences 
come in the handling of Education and Social Standing 
and the way that characteristics are decided for Judge 
characters.
Determining Characteristics
The standard Traveller method for determining characteristics 
is to roll 2d6 for each characteristic in turn. Judge Dredd 
handles things slightly differently to reﬂ ect the unique training 
and nature of the Judges.
Characteristics for Judges are: Strength, Dexterity, 
Endurance, Intelligence and Inﬂ uence (see below). Education 
and Social Standing do not receive a dice roll, as described 
in the following paragraphs.
Players should choose two of their Judge’s characteristics 
– one physical characteristic (Str, Dex or End) and either 
Inﬂ uence or Intelligence and roll these on 1d6+6. The others 
are rolled on 2d6 as normal. 
For example, a player is rolling Judge Walker; he chooses 
Dexterity and Inﬂ uence as the characteristics to beneﬁ t from 
the 1d6+6 roll but rolls Str, End and Int on 2d6, as normal.
 Education
A Judge enters the Academy of Law at the age of 5 and goes 
through 15 years of training before undergoing his ‘Full Eagle 
Day’ at the age of 20. This training creates an Edu of 10 for 
all Judges, so it is not necessary to roll 2d6 to determine its 
result. Modi ﬁ ers accrued during training will in ﬂ uence this 
base score but if a Judge emerges from the academy with 
an unmodiﬁ ed Edu characteristic, it will have a value of 10.
 Social Standing
Judges are unique in society: Social Standing, as it applies 
to most Traveller characters, does not apply to Judges. If 
Soc is necessary, all Judges are assumed to have a Soc of 
10, with people such as the Heads of Departments having 
a Soc of 11, Deputy Chief Judge 12 and Chief Judge 13. In 
most cases, Soc is not applicable: Judges are The Law.
 Influence
One characteristic Judges do require is In ﬂ uence – the 
ability for a Judge to have a commanding presence and 
manner when dealing with the myriad perps of Mega-City 
One. Consider In ﬂ uence (abbreviated to Inf) to replace 
Social Standing; it is rolled, like the standard characteristics, 
on 2d6 and will be modi ﬁ ed during training. Judges with an 
Inf score of 1-2 are very meek and perhaps better suited to 
non-street work; those with 3-5 are somewhat reticent and 
lacking in that command of authority that the best Judges 
have; 6-8 is the natural expectation for an effective Judge 
– authoritative, ﬁ rm, fair and commanding; 9-11 indicates 
Judges who command the attention and respect of those 
around them simply by showing-up; and Judges with Inf of 
12 and above are those who command absolute respect – or 
fear (such as Judge Dredd).
Inﬂ uence is used for Judges only. Everyday citizens of Mega-
City One use the standard six Traveller characteristics.
 Age and Career Terms
Your Judge character starts at the age of 5. His training is handled 
in blocks of time known as Terms. The ﬁ rst Term represents 
three years of the cadet’s training; Terms Two, Three and Four 
represent four years. After 4 Terms, when he has reached 20, 
he undergoes a ﬁ nal stretch of training which is called Full Eagle 
Day. If the Judge passes Full Eagle Day, he graduates as a 
fully-ﬂ edged Judge, ready to patrol Mega-City One.
Your Judge Dredd campaign may call for, or be better with, 
seasoned Judges. A newly graduated Judge is certainly 
equipped for life on the streets but seasoned Judges have 
the edge in terms of sheer experience. At the Referee’s 
discretion, players may take their character through up to 
two more Terms before play begins; this will produce a more 
experienced Judge in the mould of Hershey, Giant or even 
Dredd: but, remember, life on the streets of Mega-City One 
is very dangerous and going through an additional one or 
two Terms to ‘season’ your Judge may result in a fatality 
– and that means back to the Academy of Law with a wide-
eyed 5-year old juve to be trained all over again.
 Enlistment
There is no roll for enlistment or qualiﬁ cation: your character 
is automatically accepted into the Academy of Law. Also, a 
character continues through each Term unless he is either a) 
killed; or b) expelled for failing to make the grade. Either way, it 
spells the end for that particular character. A failed Judge cannot 
move into an alternative career and continue his development.
 Skills and Special Techniques
Judge Dredd introduces several new skills to Traveller, 
designed to ﬁ t the atmosphere of Dredd’s universe. It also 
introduces a set of Special Techniques; these are specialised 
skills, manoeuvres, tricks and so forth that Judges are taught 

===PAGE 12===
 Academy Of Law
11
and trained in to make them exceptional combatants and 
expert survivalists. New skills and Special Techniques are 
marked in italics in the Skill Tables and described in more 
detail in the Skills and Special Techniques chapter.
 Benefits
Judges gain no bene ﬁ ts as do other Traveller characters. 
Once graduated, every Judge receives his uniform, 
Lawmaster, Lawgiver, daystick, full eagle, armour and so 
forth. He also gains an apartment in a Justice Department-
owned block and a regular stipend to cover meals and 
general living expenses. Otherwise, he receives no other 
material or ﬁ nancial bene ﬁ ts. The Justice Department 
provides for his every need and no Judge should want for 
anything else (save those items necessary to a case or the 
occasional wad of Credits to pay an informer). Those that 
do want more tend to be the ones who go rotten – like Rico 
Dredd and Gibson – and that generally guarantees a twenty 
year stretch on Titan.
About the 
Academy
The Academy covers a massive area of Sector One of Mega-
City One – the region once known as Upper West Side in old 
New York. Within its halls are dormitories, classrooms, lecture 
theatres, gymnasiums, Sleep-Learn theatres (even whilst 
asleep, rookie Judges are fed coursework, such is the intensity 
of their preparation) and the Simulation Halls. The Sim Halls 
are life-sized recreations of some of Mega-City One’s most 
hazardous districts: in the fourth Term of training, rookies 
must demonstrate their competence in live- ﬁ re situations, 
apprehending/subduing and sentencing robot perps in order 
to hone their skills. Failure in the Sim Hall environment can 
mean serious injury or death – and rookies use, and face, live 
ammunition in these gruelling tests. But, once passed, the 
rookie is on his way to Full Eagle Day and graduation.
The Academy is staffed by human and robot tutors. The 
human contingent consists of Judges forced out of active 
service due to injury in the line of duty. Chief Judge Grif ﬁ n, 
once the Academy’s principal, is the most famous of the 
Academy’s staff – many of whom fought and died in the 
struggle against the tyrannical rule of Judge Cal. These 
Judges have countless years experience in the ﬁ eld and 
are eminently capable of passing on their knowledge to the 
Judges of tomorrow. Robot tutors focus on the standard 
academic fare: numeracy, literacy, history, geography, 
and so forth. Every rookie Judge is educated to college-
standard although the emphasis is clearly on the Law and its 
application, in addition to combat and survival skills.
Rookie Judges join at the age of 5 and that is the last they 
will see of their parents (save for the occasional Open Day or 
special exhibition) until their graduation. Once enrolled, the 
child is given over to the care of the Academy in its entirety. 
There is great prestige in entering; and proud Mega-City 
parents, saddened by the loss of a child, are comforted 
in knowing that their son or daughter is passing into the 
greatest educational establishment in the world (and the 
most dangerous).
Training 
Cycle
The training cycle for Judges follows the traditional Traveller 
format but there are some differences. (characters start as 5 
year olds, for example and the fact that no alternative career 
paths are used). The checklist is as follows:
Generation Process
Basic character generation uses the following steps:
1. Come up with a name for the character (you know he 
will be a Judge).
Survival at the 
Academy of Law
The standard approach to Traveller character 
creation is the Survival roll, made each Term, to 
see if a character gets through without injury or 
mishap. Characters failing their Survival roll are 
often forced to leave their chosen career.
Survival in Judge Dredd still exists for each Term 
but is handled somewhat differently. Failing the 
Survival roll does not automatically remove you 
from the Academy of Law – even if you roll a 
natural 2. Staying on as a Judge is determined 
by the Mishap table.
As an alternative (and if the Referee rules this 
permissible as an option), if your Judge ﬁ nds 
himself in circumstances, determined by the 
Mishap table, that would force him to leave the 
Academy, he may remain but must reduce 
one of his characteristics by 1 point. The 
characteristic to be reduced is at the 
player’s discretion.

===PAGE 13===
 Academy Of Law
12
2. Roll characteristics and determine characteristic 
modiﬁ ers. Remember that Education has a base of 10 
and Inﬂ uence replaces Social Standing. Virtually all 
Judges have a Soc of 10.
3. Receive your basic training for the Term.
4. In Term Three, your Judge has the opportunity to specialise. 
You will decide that specialisation in that term. 
5. Choose skills and training tables for this Term and make 
rolls as directed for that Term. 
 a. Roll for Survival for this term.
 b. If you succeed, go to Step 6.
 c. If you did not succeed, then events may have forced 
you from this career. Roll on the Mishap table, then 
go to Step 3 for your next four-year Term. Optionally, 
establish a Connection with another player character.
6. a. Roll for Events.
 b. Optionally, establish a Connection with another player 
character.
Then return to Step 3 and continue until you have completed 
four Terms. At the end of Term Four, complete the Full Eagle 
Day procedure to see how (and if) your Judge graduates.
Finally, if seasoned Judges are being used, take your Judge 
through one or two Terms, as agreed by the Referee, of the 
Seasoned Judge procedure – rolling for Survival, Mishaps 
and Events, just as you have before.
 Events and  Untrained Skills
Throughout your time at the Academy of Law, you will 
experience all manner of events which will inevitably test 
your skills. Many of the skills tested will be ones your Judge 
has trained in but some will be in skills you have not yet 
developed. The standard Traveller procedure is to apply a 
–3 DM to rolls where a character uses an untrained skill; 
for the purposes of Judge Dredd, this is handled a little 
differently. If you character is called upon to roll using a skill 
he does not already have, at level 0 or higher, choose one of 
the following resolution methods:
• Treat the roll as though your Judge has level 0 in the 
skill, thereby avoiding the –3 DM. Trainee Judges have 
all sorts of resources, including robot tutors, simulation 
programmes and other mechanisms to help develop his 
learning capabilities. For the purposes of training at the 
Academy of Law, these resources can be called upon 
to help attain success.
Or Alternatively...
• You may make your roll with the untrained skill, taking 
the –3 DM as per standard Traveller procedure. If you 
do this and succeed in the roll, then you gain that skill at 
level 0. Subsequent instances of the skill will increase it 
by one level each time.
The resolution method you choose must be stated in 
advance; you cannot change to the other method if things 
go against you.
 ‘By Grud, you’re a 
little advanced for 
your age, aren’t you?’
Astute readers will have noticed that creating a 
5 year old trainee Judge is exactly the same as 
creating an 18-year old Traveller character, in that 
2d6 is rolled for the characteristics. Why is this?
There are two reasons. The ﬁ rst is sheer 
expedience; better to keep the character creation 
system as simple as possible rather than work 
out realistic but perhaps lengthy mechanisms for 
aging from childhood, through teens and puberty 
and into early adulthood.
The second reason, which sounds more like an 
excuse but is actually part of the design thinking, 
is that the characteristics rolled now, whilst the 
character is at such a young age, reﬂ ect the potential 
the Judges at the Academy of Law recognise in 
the entrance assessments and can accurately 
forecast, chart and grow. Judges are trained to be 
razor-sharp, lightning-fast and hard-as-hell: only by 
understanding, realising and growing that potential, 
can the Academy be certain of turning out top-notch 
Judges each year.
The characteristics you roll for that 5 year 
old represent the man (or woman) he (or 
she) is destined to be...

===PAGE 14===
 Academy Of Law
13
 Term One
Term One represents the ﬁ rst three years and the formative 
stages of the ﬂ edgling Judge’s life with the Law. Schooling 
is the focus: literacy, numeracy, creative play and cognitive 
reasoning. For the ﬁ rst three years every child in the 
Academy is studied, monitored, nurtured and coached. 
Ideas are planted; hard knocks are introduced. The tutor 
robots offer sympathy; the human Judges offer reality: begin 
to think, feel and act like a Judge and you will be a Judge.
Basic Training
All characters gain the following skills: Athletics (any) 1, 
Melee (unarmed combat) 1, Survival 1.
Skills
Specialisations are not selected as part of Term One. The 
available tables are below.
Term One Skills and Training 
(Roll on ONE of the tables this Term)
Roll

Development Skills
Special 
Techniques

Perception
Crowd Control
2 +1 Dex Law Perp Sense

6 Jack of All Trades Melee 
(bludgeon)
Recovery
Survival
Roll 4+ on 2d6. You may add your highest characteristic DM 
to this roll.
Failing Survival at this stage does not necessarily get the 
character ejected from the Academy. A roll on the Mishap 
table is necessary and that determines if the character stays 
or goes.

===PAGE 15===
 Academy Of Law
14
Mishap
If your Judge fails his Survival roll for this Term, roll on the Mishap table, below and apply the effects.
Mishap
2d6 Mishap
2 You are severely wounded during basic combat training. The Academy medics make you feel better but you still have to 
leave the Academy. Still, you get to go back to your family and you have missed your toys. You much prefer toy guns to 
the real thing.
3 It is hard work learning all the things Judges have to know. Roll Int 6+ to successfully pass your end of term exams. If you 
fail, you have to leave the Academy; you should have paid more attention!
4 You feel very homesick and want to see your family badly. The Judges talk to you sternly about continuing with your 
training. If you can roll End 6+, then the Judges let you see your parents for an hour and that makes you feel better. You 
continue your training. If you fail, it is obvious you are not cut out for being a Judge and you return to your family. Daddy is 
upset but he will get over it.
5 Bullies in the dormitory pick on you. Roll Inf 8+. If you succeed, you stand up to them and they back-down. If you fail, they 
keep picking on you and you gain one of them as an Enemy.
6 One of the other cadets likes to play practical jokes and you get the blame. The Head of the Academy demands to know 
if you are the culprit. Roll Int 8+. If you succeed, you understand that you are in training to become a Judge and must 
always tell the truth, so you identify the boy who really played the tricks. You gain the Advocate skill at 0. If you fail the roll, 
you take the blame and are punished. Roll End 8+. If you succeed, you take your punishment like a Judge should and 
gain Inf +1. If you fail you are mocked by the other cadets for failing to take the punishment on the chin and lose 1 Inf.
7 During combat practice you injure another cadet although you did not mean to hit him so hard with the practice daystick. 
You gain that cadet as a Rival but gain Melee (bludgeon) 1.
8 You have trouble with one of the skills taught during Basic Training. Choose one Basic Training skill. This skill will stay at  
level 0 when you next come to improve it but will increase to level 1 on the third improvement, if you get the opportunity.
9 You are not good at physical training. Pick one from Str, Dex or End. If you roll for an improvement of that characteristic 
on the Personal Development table, it stays as it is – although if you get the chance to improve it after that, it will increase.
10 You commit some misdemeanour that gets you into serious trouble with the Academy Judges. Roll Inf 8+ and note the 
Effect:
5-6: Your recklessness has shown initiative. Gain an additional roll on the Skills table
3-4: You are admonished for your actions but have displayed remorse. Roll on the Personal Development table.
1-2: The Judges will be keeping an eye on you as a potential troublemaker. There are no further repercussions
0: You are punished for your actions. Your Survival roll for Term Two will suffer a –1 DM.
–1-–2: You are in deep trouble; roll Int 6+ to explain yourself. If you succeed, then your Survival roll for Term Two will 
suffer a –1 DM. If you fail, you are expelled from the Academy.
–3 or less: You are automatically expelled.
11 You are injured during training. Roll on the Injury table on page 37 of the Traveller rules but you remain at the Academy.
12 You are injured during training. Roll on the Injury table on page 37 of the Traveller rules but you remain at the Academy 
as long as you can successfully roll Inf 8+.

16
 Term Two
Term Two sees the start of rigorous training begin in 
earnest. The focus is on developing the key skills for Judges 
in combat, application of the law and developing Special 
Techniques. Cadets are expected to be showing maturity 
and independence in their decision making and grasping 
how sentencing, clemency and leniency operate when 
dealing with criminals and crime.
Basic Training
All characters gain a level in the following skills: Drive 
(Lawmaster), Gun Combat (Lawgiver), Law, Mega-City One 
Geography, Street Perception.
Skills
Judges gain two rolls this Term; one roll on either the 
Personal Development OR Special Techniques table; and 
one roll on the Skills table – along with any additional rolls 
granted by Term One events.

Survival
Roll 5+ on 2d6. You may add your highest characteristic DM 
to this roll.
Cadets are subjected to their ﬁ rst live ammunition simulations and 
frequent sim trials in the Academy’s mock-up of Mega-City One 
streets. The tactics of famous Judges like Dredd, Fargo, Hershey 
and Giant are studied so that cadets can learn from the greats.

===PAGE 20===
 Academy Of Law
19
 Term Three
Term Three sees two additional aspects added to the 
cadets’ training. The ﬁ rst is the Hot Dog Run – a mandatory 
trip into the Cursed Earth to test the cadet’s skills, tenacity 
and to educate him in the hellish wilderness beyond the 
conﬁ nes of the city walls. Term Three is also the beginning 
of specialisations. Cadets, if eligible, can choose to be 
streamed into one of the following specialist training areas: 
Street Judges, Psi Division, Tek or Med.
If your Judge has already been identi ﬁ ed as moving into 
one of these branches in a Term One or Two event, then 
your Judge will know which area he is to specialise in. If not, 
follow the below procedure:
1. Test for Psionics. Roll 2d6. If the result is 11+, the 
character is psionic and Psionic Strength is determined 
as per page 152 of the Traveller Core Rulebook. Your 
Judge may move into the Psi Division Specialisation if 
he so chooses. If you fail, go to step 2.
2. If your character has Int 9+ and/or Computers, Engineer 
or Mechanic, he may specialise as a Tek Judge.
3. If your character has Dex 9+ and/or Medic he may 
specialise as a Med Judge. 
4. All other Judges are Street Judges.
Basic Training
Characters gain basic training skills based on their stream, 
as follows:
Street Judges: Gain a level of Gun Combat (Lawgiver), gain a 
level of Drive (Lawmaster), gain a level of Law, Melee (bludgeon) 1.
Psi Division : Gain one Psionic Talent, chosen by the 
Judge, from any of the Talents, Basic or Advanced, found in 
the Thought Crimes chapter.
Tek Judges: Gain a level of Computers, gain a level of 
Engineer (any), gain a level of Mechanic.
Med Judges: Gain a level of Medic, gain Social Sciences 
(psychology) 1, gain Physical Sciences (biology) 1.
Survival
Roll 7+ on 2d6. You may add your highest characteristic DM 
to this roll, plus any DMs for your specialisation, as follows:
Street Judges: Gun Combat or Melee
Psi Judges: Psionic Strength
Tek Judges: Mechanics or Engineer
Med Judges: Medic or Computers

4 To pass a Sim Hall exercise you ﬁ nd a way to bend the rules in your favour. Roll Int 10+ or Deception 10+. If you 
succeed, you get away with it. If you fail you are hauled before the Principal and forced to explain your actions. 
Roll Int+Tactics 8+; if you succeed, you successfully show that you used initiative and exploited a ﬂ aw in the 
testing process. If you fail, you are expelled from the Academy.
5 You take part in a Lawmaster control exercise where a dangerous manoeuvre is called for. Roll Drive (Lawmaster) 
8+. If you succeed, you pull-off the manoeuvre with aplomb and gain a level in Drive (Lawmaster). If you fail, roll 
twice on the Injury table (Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) and take the lowest result. You do, however, remain 
at the Academy.
6 Interrogation tuition involves the live interrogation of a real-life perp. Roll Inf 8+ with DMs for Persuasion. If 
you succeed, you extract a full confession from the perp and gain Interrogation 1. If you fail, the perp makes a 
mockery of your clumsy attempts to intimidate him; lose 1 Inf.
7 As part of your studies you must focus on the Robot War, studying all the vid-records, tri-dees and other 
documents. Judges who fought against Call-Me-Kenneth and his robots offer ﬁ rst-hand testimony of the madness 
of the time. Roll End 8+. If you succeed, gain a level in Mega-City One History. If you fail, you gain a life-long 
hatred and distrust of all robots, including your robotic tutors.
8 A Sim Hall robot exercise involving the apprehension of a ﬂ eeing perp, who has just killed a citizen during a street 
robbery, leaves you with no choice but to shoot the perp in the back as he ignores your commands to halt. Roll 
End 8+. If you succeed, you shoot the perp without hesitation; there is no doubt that, in real life, he would have 
killed again.
If you fail, the robot perp escapes and you are called before the Principal who questions your commitment to 
justice. Roll Inf 8+ to convince him that this was a momentary lapse of reason and will not happen again. If you 
succeed, you remain at the Academy. If you fail, you are expelled.
9 You study the history of Mega-City One crime down the ages. Roll End 8+ to avoid developing an irrational hatred 
of one of the following (roll 2d6):
2: Aliens (especially Kleggs), 3: Bank Robbers, 4: Boingers®, 5: Corrupt Judges, 6: Fatties, 7: Judge Killers, 8: 
Murderers, 9: Muties, 10: Organ Leggers, 11: Futsies, 12: Terrorists
When dealing with the object of your hatred you must successfully roll Int 8+ to avoid acting with bias when 
applying Law rolls to this group. If you fail, your prejudice impairs your ability to apply the law correctly. All Law 
skill rolls will be at –2. If you rolled Corrupt Judges, you may roll Inf 11+ if you so choose. If successful, you can, 
on graduating, apply to join the SJS.
10 You fail to apply the law in an appropriate way during a training exercise. Roll Law 8+ to immediately rectify your 
mistake. If you succeed, you pass the test. If you fail, you are called before the Principal who questions your 
commitment to justice. Roll Inf 8+ to convince him that this was a momentary lapse of reason and will not happen 
again. If you succeed, you remain at the Academy. If you fail, you are expelled.
11 You suspect that one of your tutors is engaged in corruption. Report him and gain him as an Enemy, even though 
an internal investigation ﬁ nds little to support your suspicions.
Alternatively, roll Investigate 8+ to gain further evidence. Add the Effect of your roll to a Law 8+ roll. If the Law roll 
succeeds you are able to prove your suspicions to the Principal: gain an additional skill roll from the Term Three 
skills. If you fail, you gain the tutor as an Enemy. You have to explain your conduct to the Principal but he accepts 
that you were attempting to uncover potential corruption. You lose 1 Inf but remain at the Academy.
12 You are injured. Roll on the Injury table on page 37 of the Traveller Core Rulebook.

best way of getting good results. You shun the use of 22
nd Century advanced tools in favour of antiques from 
the 19th, 20th and 21st Centuries. Your skills are unaffected but accomplishing a task takes you twice as long as 
someone using modern tools.
7 You gain an irrational fear and loathing for a particular type of technology. When dealing with this technology 
your skills suffer a –2 DM. Roll 1d6 to determine what it is you fear: 1: Alien technology, 2: Bombs/Explosives, 
3: Computers, 4: Robots, 5: Antique Vehicles, 6: Any two of the above
8 You ﬁ nd yourself easily distracted when working on a task. Either cure this by taking the Steady Hand Special Technique 
in lieu of your next skill roll, or suffer a –2 DM whenever you are working on Tek assignments with others around you.
9 Become an obsessive bore about one piece of technology. At every available opportunity you talk, ad-nauseum, about 
your obsession, especially when allowed to work on the item you love so much. Roll 1d6 to determine what it is: 1: Alien 
technology, 2: Bombs/Explosives, 3: Computers, 4: Robots, 5: Antique Vehicles, 6: Any two of the above 
10 You develop a deep loathing of Futsies, even though you know this is a medical condition that requires patience 
and sympathy. Whenever faced with a Futsie, you ﬂ y into a rage unless you can roll End 8+.
11 You are incredibly impatient with anyone who cannot grasp the simplicities of advanced Mega-City science. 
Anyone who fails to display an interest in technology of any kind gets a ﬁ erce reprimand unless you can hold 
your tongue by rolling End 8+.
12 You are injured. Roll on the Injury table on page 37 of the Traveller Core Rulebook.

===PAGE 27===
 Academy Of Law
26
Med Judge Mishap 
2d6 Mishap
2 Severely injured. Roll twice on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) and take the lowest result. To 
remain in the Academy, roll End+Inf 8+; otherwise you are forced to leave.
3 You begin to suspect that you might be developing criminal tendencies as a result of studying the criminal mind. 
Roll Inf 8+. If you succeed, you realise this is all in your mind; you are one of the good guys. If you fail, you are 
compelled to hand yourself over to the SJS; leave the Academy.
4 Your training involves the study of mutation. As a result you become sympathetic to the plight of mutants who 
are banned from Mega-City One. In the future, when faced with any mutant, you will act in a sympathetic manner 
towards his condition, irrespective of what he has done or any crime committed.
5 Your training involves human dissection. Roll End 8+. If you succeed, there are no ill effects. If you fail, you gain 
an unhealthy interest and obsession with the internal workings of the human body. You become visibly more 
animated whenever you are in the vicinity of injuries that expose the innards of the victim and delight in the 
rigours of operations and invasive surgery.
6 During a series of medical experiments you are accidentally exposed to something unpleasant that may infect 
you if you fail a roll of End 8+. If you are infected, roll once on the Injury table. The infection or disease may also 
re-occur at some point in the future; your Referee will decide when but when it does strike you will have to roll 
End 8+ to shake off its effects. Roll 1d6 to see what effect that will be:
1: Bed-ridden for 1d6 days.
2: Become prone to headaches for 2d6 hours, suffering –1 to all skills.
3: Subject to nausea causing vomiting and the shakes that last for 1d6 minutes; you can do nothing during such 
attacks.
4: Double vision and dizziness lasting 1d6 hours. –2 to all skills.
5: Flu-like symptoms; coughing, sneezing and hot ﬂ ushes. –1 to all skills for 1d3 days.
6: Bed-ridden for 3d6 days.
7 You develop the urge to play practical jokes; this gets you into constant trouble with the tutors. Roll Stealth 8+ or 
Deception 8+ to avoid being caught. If you are, you are forced to re-sit Term Two in its entirety before going onto 
Term Four. If you gain any new skills, you may keep them. But if you receive the same skill as one you already 
have, it does not improve.
8 During a ﬁ  eld exercise you misdiagnose a condition that affects the health of a fellow cadet. Roll Medic 8+. If you 
succeed, you manage to correct the mistake; if not, the cadet is made worse and you gain him as a Rival.
9 The life of a Med Judge is tough: not only must you complete your medical training, you have to double-up on the 
standard Judge training too and the workload takes its toll. Roll 1d6 to determine how you cope with the strain:
1: Develop a sick and morbid sense of humour.
2: Become emotionless and detached from the world – more like a machine than a human being.
3: Decide that all crime is a sickness that can be cured through medical means. Insist on giving all perps a 
thorough medical examination in the hope ﬁ nding a cure for their illness.
4: Become obsessed with the medical shows of the late 20
th and early 21st Centuries; believe that old medical 
techniques relying on skill, rather than machinery, are the most effective.
5: Become deeply unsympathetic to any but the most seriously injured. It is perfectly possible to function with a 
hand or foot missing – no use in being lily-livered about things...
6: Become an expert on a single type of disease or medical condition. You see potential symptoms for this 
everywhere. Does that Judge have a cold? Could be – or it could be Jigsaw Disease in its early stages...
10 You develop a deep distrust of Futsies, even though you know this is a medical condition that requires patience 
and sympathy. Whenever faced with a Futsie, you ﬂ y into a rage unless you can roll End 10+.
11 You succumb to a destructive vice as a result of coping the pressure of the Academy. Roll 1d6 to determine 
what it is:
1: Addiction to Umpty Candy, 2: Addiction to Comic Books, 3: Gambling for the highest possible stakes, 4: 
Reckless, self-destructive behaviour, 5: Smoking, 6: Over-eating – you gain considerable weight.
You must regularly and secretly, fulﬁ l your urges or suffer a negative DM to skills. Your Referee will decide the 
intensity of the penalty and for how long.
12 You are injured. Roll on the Injury table on page 37 of the Traveller Core Rulebook.

Enemy.

51 Someone close to you outside the Academy – a family member or close childhood friend – dies. Roll 1d6 
for the circumstances:
1: Natural causes, 2: A long illness, 3: A tragic accident, 4: A sudden illness, 5: Accidentally shot by a 
Judge, 6: Murdered
If you rolled 1-4 you are given a few hours of compassionate leave to attend the funeral and the Academy 
sends a nice bouquet of synthi-ﬂ owers. You continue your career as normal.
If you rolled 5, throw Int+End 10+ to understand that this was a tragic accident and the Judge will be 
feeling remorseful over the accidental death. If you fail the roll, you are suspicious of the circumstances 
and vow to investigate the death as soon as you have graduated. The Judge may be innocent; but you 
want to be sure.
If you rolled 6, roll a further 1d6. A result of 1-4 and the creep was arrested and sentenced to life in an 
iso-cube. On a 5-6, he managed to escape and is still at large. Roll Int+Law 10+. If you succeed you know 
that you must remain detached from the case: Judges should not seek personal revenge. If you fail, you 
decide that, the minute you graduate, you will be out on the streets hunting for this perp – and, when you 
ﬁ nd him, you’ll make him regret ever being born...

Effect.

2-3: Your performance is good but you know you can do better.
0-1: Adequate performance but you know you should have done much better. Gain a Rival.
If you fail, your team loses. Lose 1 Inf.

to escape on technicalities or light sentencing. You may roll Inf 10+ to point this out to your superiors;

Four skill roll as a reward. If you fail, the tutors study your recommendations but conclude that they would 
require additional, extensive revisions that would be detrimental for more serious offences.

53 You are selected for additional training. Roll on either the Skills or Special Techniques table.
54 You accompany a real-life Justice Department Med team on a routine Mega-City One shift. 
Gain a Contact from amongst that Med Team and gain +1 Int.
55 You study the criminal mind. Gain a level in Social Science (psychology).
56 You study the history of the city. Gain a level in Mega-City One History.
61 During a practical medical experiment you ﬁ nd yourself in command of your tutor group. Roll 
Medic 10+. If successful, gain a level of Leadership. If you fail, your command is good but not 
outstanding.
62 An accident in the sim halls tests your medical capabilities to the limit. Roll Medic 8+. If you 
succeed, you gain valuable experience and conﬁ dence in providing medical attention under 
ﬁ re: gain the Life Saver special technique.
63 You make a study of those who suffer from Future Shock and other psychological disorders 
associated with the pace of life of 22
nd Century mega cities. When dealing with such people 
you may add your Int or Inf DM to any DMs for the skill you are using.
64 You are selected for additional training. Roll on either the Skills or Special Techniques table.
65 You demonstrate particular aptitude in one of the skills gained in Basic Training. Increase it by 
one level.
66 You emerge as top of your class in this Term’s exams. You gain a special commendation from 
the Head of the Academy and you gain an additional roll on any of this Term’s skill tables.
d66 Event

===PAGE 36===
 Academy Of Law
35
 Hotdog Run
All Judges, irrespective of their specialisation, undertake the hazardous Hotdog Run into the Cursed Earth. Here, the cadets are 
expected to become acquainted with life beyond the conﬁ nes of Mega-City One, demonstrate their skills in handling the variety 
of hazards to be found in the devastated wildlands and emerge as more rounded individuals.
Characters do not roll for Survival separately for their Hotdog run. Instead, note the Survival roll made earlier in this Term.  If 
it was passed, then the character gains a +2 DM to the Hot Dog Run Outcome roll (see below). If Survival was failed and the 
cadet has remained in the Academy, he gains a –2 DM to the Outcome roll.
Hotdog Run Outcome Roll
Roll 2d6 and apply the positive or negative DM for Survival, then consult the Outcome table for the appropriate result.
2d6 Hotdog Run Outcome
0 Severely Injured. Roll twice on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) and take the lowest result
1 Injured. Roll on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37).
2 Recurring Radiation Sickness. The cadet suffers from radiation sickness following a failure of safety procedures 
or abnormally high levels of rads in the area where the run takes place. The character loses 1 point from either 
Str, End or Int. In addition, the sickness recurs. Your Referee will decide when but when it does strike you will 
have to roll End 8+ to shake off its effect. Roll 1d6 to see what effect that will be:
1: Bed-ridden for 1d6 days.
2: Become prone to headaches for 2d6 hours, suffering –1 to all skills.
3: Subject to nausea causing vomiting and the shakes that last for 1d6 minutes; you can do nothing during such 
attacks.
4: Double vision and dizziness lasting 1d6 hours. –2 to all skills.
5: Flu-like symptoms; coughing, sneezing and hot ﬂ ushes. –1 to all skills for 1d6 days.
6: Bed-ridden for 3d6 days.
3 Temporary Radiation Sickness. The cadet suffers from radiation sickness following a failure of safety procedures 
or abnormally high levels of rads in the area where the run takes place. The character loses 1 point from either 
Str, End or Int.
4 Mutie or Monster attack. Your group is attacked by muties or Cursed Earth monsters insistent on your blood. Roll 
one of the following: Gun Combat 8+, Drive (Lawmaster) 8+, Mechanic 8+, Engineering 8+, Medic 8+, Psionic 
Strength 8+ or Survival 8+. If you succeed, you beat-off the attack successfully. If you fail, roll on the Injury table 
(Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37).
5 Mutant Justice. You are taken into a shanty town or village populated by mutants where you must assess existing 
crime and return your verdict on how justice should best be dispensed. Roll Law 8+. If successful, you gain +1 
Inf.
6 Your group is faced with extreme conditions that take every ounce of skill and technique to deal with. Roll 9+ on 
2d6, using your highest skill as a DM. If you succeed, you may increase your lowest skill by 1 level or gain +1 to 
any of your characteristics.
7 Your dealings with the denizens of the Cursed Earth is ﬁ rm but fair. You gain a Contact from amongst the Cursed 
Earth’s inhabitants.
8 The community of Cursed Earth denizens you come across treats the Judges with respect and humility and learn 
how to communicate with them effectively. You gain a +1 DM to Inﬂ uence whenever you are dealing with Cursed 
Earth mutants.
9 Roll Inf+Law 8+. If successful you assist in bringing a notorious perp hiding in the Cursed Earth to justice; gain an 
additional roll on any of the tables for this Term. However, you also gain that perp as an Enemy.
10 You ﬁ nd the Cursed Earth strangely compelling, for all its horror and weirdness. You know that when you retire, 
you will deﬁ nitely take the Long Walk. Until then, gain Survival 1.
11 The Cursed Earth makes you physically more resilient. Gain either Survival 1 or +1 End.
12 The Cursed Earth helps you make better decisions, having seen its depredations. Gain either Law 1 or +1 Int.
13 Through your experience in the Cursed Earth, you have a better understanding of the foibles of Mega-City One’s 
citizens. Gain +1 Inf or a level of Street Perception.
14 You handle the rigours of the Cursed Earth with distinction. Gain one level in any skill of your choosing and a roll 
on the Special Techniques table for this Term.

Survival
Roll 7+ on 2d6. You may add your highest characteristic DM 
to this roll, plus any DMs for your specialisation, as follows:
Street Judges: Gun Combat (any) or Melee (any)
Psi Judges: Psionic Strength
Tek Judges: Mechanics or Engineer
Med Judges: Medic or Computers
 Full Eagle 
Day
After ﬁ fteen years of the toughest, most rigorous training on earth, 
this is what it all comes down to; the culmination of the hard work 
and effort, injuries, set-backs and triumphs: Full Eagle Day.
Wearing the white helmet and half-eagle badge of a 
rookie Judge, the cadet goes onto the streets to spend a 
working day with a seasoned Judge who then decides if 
the cadet is suitable for graduation. The assessing Judge 
has considerable leeway in passing or failing cadets but his 
decision is ﬁ nal; there is no appeal and no second chance. If 
the assessing Judge fails the rookie then that is it – he must 
ﬁ nd something else to do with his life.
To pass Full Eagle Day, the cadet goes through the following steps:
Make a successful Survival roll for Full Eagle Day. This is 12+ 
but the cadet applies DMs according to his specialisation, as 
below:
• Street Judge: Str or Dex characteristic DM, Gun 
Combat (Lawgiver), Law, plus a DM equal to his highest 
skill, not already used in this list.
• Psi Judge: Psionic Strength characteristic DM, any 
two Psionic Talents; plus a DM equal to his highest skill, 
not already used in this list.
• Tek Judge: Int characteristic DM, Computers, 
Engineering OR Mechanic; plus a DM equal to his 
highest skill, not already used in this list.
• Med Judge: Int characteristic DM, Medic, Science 
(any) OR Persuasion OR Stealth; plus a DM equal to 
his highest skill, not already used in this list.
The Survival roll is further modi ﬁ ed by the attitude of the 
Assessing Judge...
Assessing Judge’s Attitude
Judges are not all as stony-faced and meticulous as Dredd. 
Some, recalling how hard life as a rookie is and knowing 
how valuable it is to have Judges on the street, are more 
lenient than others. Roll on the Attitude Table to determine 
the Judge’s attitude towards his Full Eagle Day cadet, and 
the DM this applies to the Survival roll.

If the Survival Roll is 
Passed…
Then the cadet passes his Full Eagle Day assessment and 
graduates as a full Judge, being awarded his Full Eagle 
and black helmet. He passes out in the grand but solemn, 
Graduation Parade and is almost immediately assigned to a 
Sector House to begin his career.
You may begin play with your Judge as he is or, if the 
Referee wishes it, your Judge can be enhanced under the 
Seasoned Judges process, explained below.
If the Survival Roll is 
Failed…
Then there is a chance that your Judge will not be passed by 
the assessing Judge and his career is over. As with any failure 
to make a Survival roll, roll on the Full Eagle Mishaps table.
2d6 Mishap
2 Disaster! Everything that could go wrong has gone wrong. The assessing Judge concludes that 15 years of training has 
still not made you ready and there is little prospect of being so. You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
3 You are severely wounded. Roll twice on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) and apply the lowest result. 
If you can succeed in making an Inf 8+ roll, the assessing Judge is still pleased with your performance and he passes 
you. Congratulations. If you fail the roll, the assessing Judge concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you 
ready and there is little prospect of you ever being so. You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
4 You are wounded. Roll on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37). If you can succeed in making an Inf 7+ 
roll, the assessing Judge is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations.
If you fail the roll, the assessing Judge concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready and there is little 
prospect of you ever being so. You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
5 Although things go smoothly, applying the law to a perp you apprehend seems to be more complicated than you had 
thought. Roll Law 8+. If you succeed, the assessing Judge is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. 
Congratulations. If you fail the roll, the assessing Judge concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready 
and there is little prospect of you ever being so. You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
6 You ﬁ nd yourself in a combat situation. Roll Gun Combat (Lawgiver) 8+. If you succeed, the assessing Judge is still 
pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations. If you fail the roll, The assessing Judge concludes 
that 15 years of training have still not made you ready and there is little prospect of being so. You are Failed and your 
career as a Judge ends here.
7 Passing Full Eagle Day requires you to chase-down a ﬂ eeing Perp. Roll either Athletics (any) 8+ or Street Perception 
8+. If you succeed, the assessing Judge is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations. If 
you fail the roll, the assessing Judge concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready and there is little 
prospect of you ever being so. You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
8 Your assessment takes you out onto the skedways of Mega-City One and you must apprehend a perp committing a 
trafﬁ c offence from the saddle of your Lawmaster. Throw Drive (Lawmaster) 8+. If you succeed, the assessing Judge 
is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations. If you fail the roll, the assessing Judge 
concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready and there is little prospect of you ever being so. You are 
failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
9 Your survival instincts are put to the test on Full Eagle Day. Roll Medic 8+ or Survival 8+. If you succeed, the assessing 
Judge is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations. If you fail the roll, the assessing 
Judge concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready and there is little prospect of you ever being so. 
You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
10 Your powers of persuasion are needed if you are to pass Full Eagle Day. Throw Persuade 8+. If you succeed, the 
assessing Judge is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations. If you fail the roll, the 
assessing Judge concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready and there is little prospect of you ever 
being so. You are failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
11 Choose one of your skills (whether basic training or a specialisation) and roll 8+. If you succeed, the assessing Judge 
is still pleased with your performance and he passes you. Congratulations. If you fail the roll, the assessing Judge 
concludes that 15 years of training has still not made you ready and there is little prospect of you ever being so. You are 
failed and your career as a Judge ends here.
12 Despite seeming to have failed in every task set before you, the assessing Judge was placing individual tests of your 
mettle. To your surprise and relief you are passed. Congratulations.

===PAGE 40===
 Academy Of Law
 Academy Of Law
 Seasoned   
Judges
39
If your Judge Dredd campaign is to use experienced Judges, 
then the Seasoned Judges procedure can be applied to produce 
a more savvy, rounded Judge character. The procedure can be 
run up to two times, with each application of it 
representing four years on the streets.
Survival
The different specialisations have different 
Survival rolls, as below.
Street Judge: End 7+
Psi Judge: Psi 7+
Tek Judge: Int 7+
Med Judge: Int 7+
Mishaps
If the Survival throw is failed, roll on the Mishaps table. 
All specialisations use the same Mishap table.
2d6 Mishap
2 Severely injured. Roll twice on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) and take the lowest result.
3 Severe injury takes you out of action for several months. Reduce the Skill points available for developing skills from 
5 to 3.
4 Injury takes you out of action for several weeks. Reduce the Skill points available for developing skills from 5 to 4
5 You are faced with an especially dif ﬁ cult case to crack. Throw the skill indicated by your specialisation:
Street Judge: Law 10+
Psi Judge: Psionic Strength 10+
Tek Judge: Engineering 10+
Med Judge: Medic 10+
If you succeed, the case is cracked without any detriment. If you fail, you gain an Enemy. You must also roll End 8+ 
or suffer an injury and roll on the Injury table (Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37).
6 You are subject to an SJS investigation. Roll In ﬂ uence 8+ to convince the SJS of your innocence. If you succeed, 
you are fully exonerated. If you fail, SJS remain suspicious of you for various reasons. Gain one SJS Judge as an 
Enemy.
7 An innocent civilian is killed as a result of your zealous pursuit of a perp. You gain 1d6 Enemies in the form of the 
dead citizen’s family and friends. You must throw Inf+Law 10+ to demonstrate to your superiors that your actions 
were unavoidable. If you succeed, it is acknowledged that you were only doing your job and this was a tragic 
accident. If you fail, you are investigated by the SJS. Roll Int 8+ or Advocate 8+ to prove your innocence to them. If 
you fail, you are found negligent and given a bum posting. Lose 1 Inﬂ uence as a result of the experience.
8 Your handling of a case or incident is criticised by your fellow Judges. You must explain your actions to the Sector 
Chief. Roll either Advocate or Persuasion 8+ (with a DM for your In ﬂ uence); if you succeed, the Sector Chief is 
satisﬁ ed with your actions; however you gain 1 Judge as a Rival. If you fail the roll, you are reprimanded publicly: 
gain 1 Judge as a Rival and lose 1 point of Inf.
9 An arrest and sentence you carried out goes to appeal. Roll Law 10+ to prove your reasoning for the sentence. If 
successful, the appeal fails and your reputation is undiminished. If you fail, you are rebuked by the Sector Chief for 
failing to understand and apply the law correctly. You lose 1 point of Inf as a result.

===PAGE 41===
 Academy Of Law
40
10 Working a tough case with a partner, you lose concentration momentarily. Roll 1d6 to see what happens:
1: Both of you come under ﬁ re from the perps you are chasing. You are wounded (roll on the Injury table, Traveller 
Core Rulebook, page 37) but your partner is killed. Gain 1d6 Rivals in the form of your partners close colleagues 
who blame you for his death.
2: Both of you come under ﬁ re from the perps you are chasing. You are both wounded (roll on the Injury table, 
Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) for yourself and your partner. Your partner now becomes an Enemy, as he feels 
he cannot trust your judgement or ability to concentrate.
3: Both of you come under ﬁ re from the perps and your partner is wounded whilst you are unscathed. Your partner 
now becomes a Rival.
4: Neither of you are hurt but your partner is suspicious of your abilities. Roll Inf+Advocate 10+ to convince him that 
this was a temporary aberration. If you fail the roll, he becomes a Rival.
5: The perps escape and your partner blames you. Roll Inf+Law 10+ to justify yourself to your Sector Head. If you 
fail, lose 1 Inf.
6: As for 5 but your negligence is unforgivable. You are forced to take the Long Walk, and your career as a Mega-
City One Judge is over.
11 A fellow Judge who you consider a good friend and partner turns bad. You have a choice:
1. Confront him and arrest him. Roll Inf+Law 10+. If you succeed, he breaks-down and admits his crimes. He is 
arrested and sentenced to 12 years on Titan. If you fail, he tries to kill you. Roll Gun Combat (Lawgiver) 10+. If you 
succeed, you kill him but have enough evidence to prove your case to the SJS. If you fail, roll twice on the Injury 
table (Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37) and take the lowest result. He goes rogue and you gain an Enemy.
OR 2. Attempt to cover up for him. Roll Deception 10+ to help him cover his tracks. If you succeed, you must live 
with the guilt of being complicit in his corruption. If you fail, SJS catches-up with you and you must take the rap for 
his crimes. You serve 20 years on Titan and your career as a Mega-City One Judge is over.
12 Injured. Roll on the Injury table ( Traveller Core Rulebook, page 37).
Skills
For each four year Term spent creating a Seasoned Judge, 
your Judge’s skills increase in the following ways:
• Distribute 5 points amongst skills (or Talents, for Psi 
Judges) you have already accrued. It costs 1 point to 
raise a level 0 skill to level 1; 2 points to raise a level 1 
skill to level 2; 3 points to raise level 2 to level 3. No skill 
can be raised to more than 3.
• If you wish, you may use some of your 5 points to buy a Special 
Technique. It costs 3 points per technique and you cannot 
choose a technique that is speciﬁ c to a specialisation.
• Increasing a characteristic costs 2 points per 1 point of increase
• Choose a new skill from those available in the Traveller 
Core Rulebook or the new skills described in the next 
chapter. You gain this skill at level 0.
 Allies,  Contacts,  Enemies and 
 Informers
Seasoned Judges have usually built up a whole network of 
Allies, Contacts and Informers who can assist them in their 
work. Similarly, a Judge always makes Enemies – a citizen 
reprimanded for something minor; a perp sentenced to a stiff 
sentence, a relative of someone who has fallen-foul of the 
law, and so on.
2d6 Mishap
Some of these relationships may have been formed during 
the time in the Academy of Law but once out on the street, the 
seasoned Judge starts making – and using – these contacts 
in earnest. The way they work is described on page 35 of the 
Traveller Core Rulebook. When creating a seasoned Judge 
you should also determine what relationships you have 
made. For each four year Term as a seasoned Judge you 
should roll three times on the Relationships table:
Roll Relationships
1 Ally
2 Contact
3 Enemy
4 Informer
5 Rival
6 Roll twice
Informers are a new relationship for Judge Dredd. An 
Informer works in the same way as Contact but will always 
want paying for his help. Payment can be in Credits (the 
department of Justice has a budget for paying informers) or 
in other favours. Note that no Judge will ever bend or break 
the law for an Informer but he may be in a position to use his 
inﬂ uence or skills in a beneﬁ cial way that is entirely legal.

===PAGE 42===
 Academy Of Law
41
Take a little time to put names, roles and potted histories to 
your relationships. How did you gain that Informer? How did 
that normal citizen become an Enemy? What did you do to 
gain an Ally in the SJS? You do not need to write reams on 
each relationship but a little detail will help the Referee to 
use them during game play.
 Special Judicial Service, 
 Holocaust Squad and  Wally 
Squad
There are three additional areas of speciality that have not 
been covered as the Judge goes through Academy Training. 
As a seasoned Judge, and with the Referee’s permission, 
they can be applied now.
Special Judicial Service
The feared SJS is the department responsible for policing 
and investigating other Judges. Its reputation was badly 
shaken during the reign of Chief Judge Cal, who used the 
SJS as his personal terror squad, but since then the SJS 
has managed to salvage its reputation under the watchful 
eye of Judge McGruder. The SJS is to be rightly feared by 
any Judge who has been so weak or foolish as to fall from 
the path of true justice.
Any Judge that has broken or transcended the law can be 
subject to an SJS investigation. SJS Judges specialise in 
tracking through a Judge’s career, arrest record, personal, 
off-duty habits and so forth, on the look out for potential 
transgressions. Most emerge scot-free but some, those who 
have been prone to temptation, can be disciplined, punished 
or even sent to Titan.
If a seasoned Judge wants to become a member of the SJS, 
he must:
• Have no Event or Mishap on his record that has involved 
him in any crime.
• Have Inf and End of 9+.
• Roll Inf 10+ to gain acceptance to the SJS.
SJS Judges gain Investigate 1 as a bonus skill. They also 
gain the right to review all aspects of the lives of their fellow 
Judges, whenever suspicion is aroused but also on routine, 
internal Crime Blitzes where a randomly selected Judge has 
every single aspect of his career scrutinised for the smallest 
possible lapse in adherence to the law.
Holocaust Squad
The Holocaust Squad is a highly specialised unit of Judges 
who tackle the most dangerous hazards and disasters facing 
Mega-City One; those that threaten hundreds of thousands 
of lives and the city’s very existence. Every Holocaust Squad 
Judge knows that he may very well die as a result of saving 
others – but that is of no concern to him. He is prepared 
to lay-down his life in sel ﬂ ess sacriﬁ ce for the law and the 
mega city.
For the most part, Holocaust Squad Judges are serving Street 
Judges who have volunteered to act as part of a Holocaust Squad 
unit when the need arises. Every Holocaust Judge pledges to 
put his life secondary to justice and the safety of Mega-City One 
and undergoes mental tests and holocaust simulations to prove 
that he is fearless in the face of self-sacriﬁ ce.
To become an accepted Holocaust Squad volunteer, a 
Judge must have Survival 2 and Endurance 10 or higher. He 
gains an additional skill and, when called upon to serve his 
Holocaust Squad unit, cannot ignore the summons.
Wally Squad
The Justice Department’s undercover branch, Wally Squad 
Judges abandon the familiar uniform of their colleagues 
for civilian clothes and spend their days mingling with the 
citizens of Mega-City One, in ﬁ ltrating the organised crime 
gangs, becoming agitators and troublemakers to serve 
Justice Department’s agenda, and undertaking a variety of 
deep cover activities.
Wally Squad members can spend years impersonating 
someone else and, even though their training at the 
Academy of Law prepares them for the rigours of city-life, 
the temptations that affect normal citizens are strewn before 
Wally Squad operatives to exactly the same degree. It is 
therefore not unknown for Wally Squad Judges, like Lenny 
Zero, to ‘go native’ and fall prey to the temptations that 
corrupt average citizens. 
If a Judge wants to join the Wally Squad, he must have level 
1 in at least one of the following skills: Broker, Carouse, 
Gamble or Stealth. Acceptance into the Wally Squad is 
achieved on a roll of Inf 8+ with a further DM for the highest 
rated of the above skills. Any Judge who serves with the 
Wally Squad automatically gains 1d6 Contacts from amongst 
the criminal fraternity.
Improving By 
Experience
The procedure for creating a seasoned Judge simulates four 
years of work on the streets of Mega-City One and takes into 
account the rapid learning curve all Judges go through when 
out of the Academy of Law and assigned to their duties.

===PAGE 43===
 Academy Of Law
42
Every Judge learns through continued experience, as found 
in game play during the campaign. Every four years of game 
time, Referees can allow player character Judges to improve 
their characters using a similar process as that used for 
Seasoned Judges:
• You gain 4 points that can be used to raise skills. Talents 
or buy Special Techniques.
• It costs 1 point to raise a level 0 skill to level 1; 2 points 
to raise a level 1 skill to level 2; 3 points to raise level 2 
to level 3; and so on.
• Learning a skill you do not have, so that you gain it at 
level 0, costs 1 point.
• Learning a new Special Technique costs 4 points.
• Increasing a characteristic costs 2 points per 1 point of 
increase
• No more than 1 unused point can be carried over to the 
next allocation of points.
Example 
Character – 
Judge Walker
As an example of creating a Judge, we will take Judge 
Walker through his Academy experience, Full Eagle Day 
and then into two Terms of Seasoned Judge experience.
To begin with, Walker’s characteristics are: Str 5 (–1), Dex 8 
(+0), End 8 (+0), Int 5 (–1), Inf 11 (+1). Remember that the 
Academy provides an Education of 10 and Social Standing 
is not important for a Judge (although it can be considered to 
be at 10). Walker is quite weedy but a little over average in 
terms of health and dexterity. He is certainly not the smartest 
tool in the box but his personality is forceful; perhaps he is a 
stickler for the law.
Term One
Walker enters the Academy as a somewhat frightened ﬁ ve 
year-old. The Academy takes him under its wing and for 
basic training he receives Athletics (co-ordination) 1, Melee 
(unarmed combat) 1 and Survival 1. The aim is to toughen 
him up for the hard years ahead. Let’s see how he copes 
with the process.
Walker gains one roll on either the Personal Development, 
Skills or Special Techniques for Term One: he goes for 
Personal Development, hoping to improve himself physically. 
The 1d6 roll is 4: +1 Int. The schooling he gains from the 
robot tutors is making him a bit smarter – no bad thing in a 
Judge. Walker’s characteristics are now Str 5 (–1), Dex 8 
(+0), End 8 (+0), Int 6 (+0), Inf 11 (+1).
For his Survival, Walker has to roll Inf 4+. The roll is 9 – so 
he sails through his ﬁ rst Term without mishap. Next, for his 
Event, Walker rolls 64 (using d66): Walker forms a deep 
friendship with 2 other cadets in his year and they pledge to 
support each other through thick and thin. He now has two 
Allies and the Referee declares that these are cadet Judges 
Cisco and Strom – the two other characters being created 
for this campaign. As the Referee is using the Connections 
rule (page 8 of the Traveller Core Rulebook ), he decides 
that Walker, Cisco and Strom will bene ﬁ t from each other’s 
experiences. The skills each can gain – 2 maximum from 
Connections – will be ﬁ nalised at the end of Term 4. Until 
then, they are ﬁ rm friends.
Term One is complete; Walker has survived. His 
characteristics and skills are as follows:
Str 5 (–1), Dex 8 (+0), End 8 (+0), Int 6 (+0), Inf 11 (+1).
Athletics (co-ordination) 1, Melee (unarmed combat) 1, 
Survival 1
Term Two
The training becomes far more serious but Walker is up to 
the challenge. For basic training he gains Drive (Lawmaster) 
1, Gun Combat (Lawgiver) 1, Law 1, Mega-City One 
Geography 1 and Street Perception 1. These skills re ﬂ ect 
the essentials all Judges need to know for life in the tumult 
of the megacity.
For additional skills, Walker can roll on either the Personal 
Development or Special Techniques table and once on the 
Skills table. He decides to learn a Special Technique and 
throws 5: Combat Acuity. Walker is quick to react in an array of 
combat simulations, his talent astounding his marksmanship 
tutors. For his skill roll, he throws 3 on 1d6. Walker decides 
to take Computers 1; clearly he has an aptitude for using 
MAC’s phenomenal computing resources.
Time for Survival. It is harder this Term and Walker needs 
to throw Inf 5+: he rolls 4, which means he fails and must 
therefore roll on the Mishap table. The Mishap result is 5: 
high jinks with Cisco and Strom land Walker in trouble and 
he is hauled before the Principal to explain why he thought 
it would be amusing to paint several of the tutor robots pink 
whilst they were recharging their batteries. Abusing paint 
and Academy property is a serious offence. Walker has to 
throw Inf 8+ to successfully come up with an explanation. 
His Inﬂ uence is good, giving him a +1 DM. Walker rolls 11; 
he comes up with an excuse that most likely does not dupe 
the Principal but is so convincingly told and delivered with 
such a calm and authoritative voice, that there is no option 
but to reprimand him and ban him from the paint stores for 
a Term.
