Now for the Term Two Event. Walker rolls 21 on d66: He 
(and Cisco and Strom, the Referee decides), are selected 
to take an exchange visit to Brit-Cit. The young cadets gain 
a Contact from this visit; the Referee says this is the Brit-Cit 
Judge assigned to look after them during their stay – Judge 
Morrison. Walker also gains an additional roll on the Term 
Two Skill table – he rolls 4 and chooses Street Lore. A 
further 1d6 roll shows that Walker has picked up Streetwise 
1 whilst in Brit-Cit.
So, at the end of Term Two, Walker looks like this:
Str 5 (–1), Dex 8 (+0), End 8 (+0), Int 6 (+0), Inf 11 (+1).
Athletics (co-ordination) 1, Computers 1, Drive (Lawmaster) 
1, Gun Combat (Lawgiver) 1, Law 1, Mega-City One 
Geography 1, Melee (unarmed combat) 1, Street Perception 
1, Streetwise 1, Survival 1
Special Techniques: Combat Acuity
Term Three
This is the year when cadets specialise. Cisco has already 
qualiﬁ ed as a psionic but Walker needs to check for Psionic 
Potential too. He rolls 2d6 and scores 10; he is not psionic 
but perhaps shows some potential. Walker’s Int is only 6, 
so he does not qualify as a Tek Judge on those grounds 
although he does have Computers 1. Despite this, he 
decides to continue as a Street Judge – that is where true 
justice is metered out.
As a Street Judge, his basic training is Gun Combat 
(Lawgiver), Drive (Lawmaster) and Law; all building on his 
Term two skills. Also, after extensive training with the Judges’ 
daystick, he gains Melee (bludgeon) 1. Walker concentrates 
on his skills in Term Three and rolls on the Skills table (1d6 
this time) and gets 5 – Investigate. Walker has a nose for 
getting to the bottom of cases.
His Survival roll is End 7+. Walker’s roll is 6 – so another 
Mishap. Rolling on the Street Judge Mishap table (7), 
Walker studies the Robot Wars; he must roll End 8+ to avoid 
developing a deep distrust of robots. He rolls 6. Clearly, the 
experiences of painting the tutor robots pink and how those 
particular robots treated him, has left him bitter towards robots 
for all kinds. They rebelled before; they could do so again...
For his Event, Walker rolls 41 – a simulated investigation in 
the sim halls goes awry. Walker must roll Investigate, Street 
Perception 10+ or Streetwise 10+; since he has 1 in all three 
skills, it matters little but at least he has that choice. His roll 
is 3! Even with his talent for understanding investigative 
techniques he must roll on the Mishap table again, this time 
throwing 8. The exercise involves apprehending a perp: 
Walker has no option but to shoot the criminal in the back, 
which goes against his training. He needs to throw End 8+ to 
steel himself and make the shot: he rolls 7. At the last instant, 
Walker ﬁ res high and the perp gets away – to potentially kill 
again. Walker is hauled once more before the Principal who 
interrogates him on why he let a violent offender – albeit a 
robot simulation – escape when justice was needed. Walker 
knows this is serious; he must roll In ﬂ uence 8+ to get his 
argument across forcefully enough to evade expulsion. The 
dice are rolled and Walker rolls 7. Fortunately, his Inf DM 
is +1 and he manages – just – to explain his reasons. The 
Principal shakes his head, makes some notes in a pink-
splotched ﬁ le and dismisses Walker back to his studies.
This Term, Walker also has to take the hazardous Hotdog 
Run. Because he failed his Survival roll this Term, he 
receives a –2 penalty to his roll on the Outcome table. He 
scores a 9, which brings his result down to 7. Cadet Walker 
meets a mutant leader called Sad Bob (the mutation is fairly 
self explanatory) who becomes a Contact.
So, after an eventful Term Three, Walker looks like this:
Str 5 (–1), Dex 8 (+0), End 8 (+0), Int 6 (+0), Inf 11 (+1).
Athletics (co-ordination) 1, Computers 1, Drive (Lawmaster) 
2, Gun Combat (Lawgiver) 2, Investigate 1, Law 2, Mega-
City One Geography 1, Melee (bludgeon) 1, Melee (unarmed 
combat) 1, Street Perception 1, Streetwise 1, Survival 1
Special Techniques: Combat Acuity
Hates Robots.
Contact: Sad Bob (Mutant)
Term Four
The ﬁ nal Term. Basic training brings Walker levels in Law, 
Street Perception and Survival. For his development, 
Walker gains one roll on the Skills, Personal Development 
and Special Techniques tables. His results are: Jack of All 
Trades 1 (Personal Development), Formidable Presence 
(Special Technique) and Interrogation 1 (Skill). Clearly his 
Inﬂ uence, coupled with his skills in Interrogation, make 
Walker an imposing presence as a Judge.
For Survival, Walker needs to roll Inf 8+. His throw is 7 and 
he is forced to roll on the Mishap table. The result is 10 – 
once again, during a training exercise, Walker has problems 
applying the law. He needs to roll Law 10+ to get through 
this – the dice return 8 and Walker’s Law 3 take him to 11 
– he manages to sentence the offending perp (the same 
robot he let go last Term) to 40 years in the Iso-Cubes for a 
whole litany of offences. The Principal, who is watching the 
exercise on the vid screens, nods his approval and makes 
some more notes in that pink-blotched ﬁ le.

Walker’s Event result is 46: Walker is rewarded with an 
unsolved case to test his Investigate skill. The case is a 
series of unsolved robberies from six years ago that have 
bafﬂ ed the Judges. Walker needs to roll Investigate 10+. He 
rolls 10 and his skill DM takes this to 11. Walker’s insight and 
investigative technique sheds new light on a suspect who 
was dismissed. His tutors are pleased and Walker gains one 
skill choice from Deception, Investigate or Social Sciences 
(psychology). He chooses Investigate, further consolidating

like this:
Str 5 (–1), Dex 8 (+0), End 8 (+0), Int 6 (+0), Inf 11 (+1).

Full Eagle Day
This is the day Walker has been working towards. His 
assessing Judge, Gant, is moderate in his attitude towards 
cadets, so Walker gains no bonus or penalty to his Full Eagle 
Survival roll of 9+. He does, however, gain an overall DM of 
+7 for his Gun Combat (Lawgiver) and Law skills and for his 
highest skill not listed for the Street Judges (Walker has a 
number of skills at level 2) – so, to pass Full Eagle Day, he 
needs to roll 1 or more. His roll is 8: Walker works diligently, 
applies the law, shoots a ﬂ eeing perp in the thigh, catches a 
slowster on the skedway and convinces Judge Gant that this 
young man with the serious attitude is everything a Judge 
needs to be.
Walker gains his Full Eagle and, along with Cisco and Strom, 
passes out of the Academy of Justice to take his place on 
Mega-City One’s streets.
Seasoned Judge
As Walker is going to be a Seasoned Judge, the Referee 
allows him to go through two Terms of the Seasoned Judge 
process. Before that, though, Walker, Cisco and Strom need 
to formalise Connections. As Walker has one connection 
each for Cisco and Strom, he gains 2 skills. He can raise 
existing skills but not above 3 or take new ones, at 0. Walker 
decides to take Computers and Mega-City One Geography 
(giving him 2 levels in each).
For his ﬁ rst Term out on the streets, Walker’s Survival roll 
is End 8+. His result is 3 – so once more, Judge Walker 
is checking for Mishaps. The roll on the Mishap Table is 7 
– and innocent citizen gets caught in the cross ﬁ re between 
Judge Walker and Zebedee Wit-Nolan, the infamous Mo-
Pad highwayman. The poor sap who caught Walker’s stray 
bullet was Delores Compound, a beautician who cruised 
the skedways selling makeovers from her mobile cosmetics 
salon. As a result, Walker makes Enemies from four of her 
customers who are convinced Walker has doomed them to 
ugliness. Back at the Sector House, Judge Walker has to 
prove that the death was an accident by throwing Inf+Law  
10+. Walker has a combined DM for these skills of +4, so 
he needs to throw a 6, which he makes with ease (rolling 
an 8). Delores Compound’s death was a terrible accident 
and Walker accepts that, occasionally, the innocent are 
casualties of the justice process. However Delores’s 
incandescent customers want Judge Walker’s badge – they 
may make trouble at a later date.
The Mishap over, Walker’s skill package for his ﬁ rst Term of 
active service is 5 points be distributed across his skills, as 
discussed on page 40. He decides to spend 2 points raising 
Athletics (Co-ordination) to 2 and the remaining 3 points on 
the Crowd Control Special Technique.
He has also developed 1 Ally, 1 Contact and 1 Informer.
His second Term as a seasoned Judge see a further 
Survival roll of End 8+. He rolls 4 (and, by now, Walker is 
worried that painting those robots pink might have jinxed 
him) with a Mishap table result of 9. Walker arrests an 
illegal Boinger® who claims he was prescribed the activity 
by his doctor and should therefore be treated with leniency. 
Judge Walker sentenced the perp to ﬁ ve years in the 
cubes but he must attend the appeal hearing at the Sector 
House where the perp’s doctor, lawyer and therapist have 
gathered to deride the harshness of the sentence. Outside, 
Delores Compound’s supporters, all wearing masks to cover 
their increasing hideousness, wave ‘Down With Walker!’ 
placards. Walker must throw Law 10+ to prove the sentence 
was justiﬁ ed. His roll is 2, which, with his Law DM of 3 only 
gives him a 5. The perp wins the appeal and the sentence is 
commuted to 6 months, and Walker’s In ﬂ uence is reduced 
by 1 point. Outside, Delores’s supporters go wild and Judge 
Walker is forced to walk past them, fuming but knowing that, 
as a Judge, he cannot argue with the Law.
For the last time, Walker has 5 points to improve his skills. 
He decides to increase his Strength by 1 point, which costs 
2 of his skill points and to increase his Melee (unarmed 
combat) to 2, for a further 2 points. He spends his last point 
on developing Leadership 0.

New  Skills
 Combat Engineering
The Judge is trained in the rapid construction of ﬁ eld 
fortiﬁ cations, large-scale camou ﬂ age, the appraisal of 
a structure’s ability to withstand enemy ﬁ re, landmine 
placement and removal and/or placement of passive ground 
sensors. Essentially the battleﬁ eld is the character’s to mould 
as he sees ﬁ t.
Specialities
Fortiﬁ cations: The character is trained how to build ﬁ eld 
defences from trenches to bunkers. 
Building a ﬁ eld fortiﬁ cation (trench, sandbag wall, etc.): 1-
6 Hours, Education or Endurance, Average (+0). Resulting 
fortiﬁ cation grants maximum cover equal to half Effect (round 
up).
Building a defensive forti ﬁ cation (wall, bunker, etc.): 10-60 
Days, Education, Dif ﬁ cult (–2). Resulting forti ﬁ cation grants 
maximum cover equal to Effect.
Finding weak point in enemy forti ﬁ cations: 1-6 Seconds, 
Intelligence, Dif ﬁ cult (–2). Character can ignore the cover 
DM of targets behind forti ﬁ cation, up to an amount equal to 
Effect. 
Camouﬂ age: The character is trained to camou ﬂ age 
vehicles, fortiﬁ cations and ﬁ eld sites.
Constructing sufﬁ cient camouﬂ age to hide a single vehicle: 
10-60 Minutes, Intelligence, Average (+0). Grants a Stealth 
skill to the vehicle equal to Effect until vehicle is moved.
Constructing suf ﬁ cient camou ﬂ age to hide a single small 
building or defensive forti ﬁ cation: 1-6 Hours, Intelligence, 
Average (+0). Applies a skill DM penalty to those looking to 
visually ﬁ nd it equal to Effect until discovered.
Hiding a doorway or hatch from view: 1-6 Minutes, 
Intelligence, Average (+0). Grants a Stealth skill to the 
doorway/hatch equal to Effect until discovered.
IEDs and Mines: The ability to place, search for and 
remove Improvised Explosive Devices (IEDs) and land 
mines.
Avoiding previously placed mineﬁ elds: 1-6 Minutes, Dexterity 
or Intelligence, Average (+0)
Removing an active land mine: 1-6 minutes, Dexterity, Very 
Difﬁ cult (–4)
Sensor Surveillance: The use and placement of 
specially crafted ground sensors.
Properly placing a ground sensor: 10-60 Minutes, Intelligence, 
Routine (+2)
Avoiding triggering existing ground sensors: 10-60 Seconds, 
Dexterity or Intelligence, Difﬁ cult (–2)
 Flyer (personal)
This branch of the Flyer skill covers the use of personal 
devices such as jetpacks, powerboards and bat-gliders – 
any form of single-user ﬂ ying aid, whether it is jet-propelled 
(solid/liquid fuel), anti-grav or, as in the case of the bat-glider, 
using thermals and air currents for lift.
 Gun Combat (Lawgiver)
The Lawgiver is a sophisticated sidearm with many features 
simply not found on standard handguns. Judges are trained 
speciﬁ cally in handling the Lawgiver to greatest effect 
(especially important, given the different types of ammunition 
the Lawgiver ﬁ res), almost to the exclusion of other handguns. 
However, a Judge can pick-up any other handgun and use 
it at a skill level 1 less than his Gun Combat (Lawgiver) skill, 
rather than at the –3 DM for untrained skill use.
 Interrogation
Judges are frequently forced to draw information out of 
sources that do not wish to give it – both perps and witnesses. 
This places the Judge in a position to use any number of 
methods to extract it. This skill governs several ways to force 
others to give up what they know – knowingly or not.
Specialities
Doubletalk: The Judge knows how to talk circles around 
most people, getting them to say or admit to things they 
never meant to with pressured conversation. With enough 
time to grill them properly, the character can get anyone to 
spill their secrets.
Badgering an admission of guilt out of a captive: 1-6 Hours, 
Intelligence, Difﬁ cult (–2). 
Uncovering a secret through focussed conversation: 10-60 
Minutes, Intelligence, Difﬁ cult (–2).
Torture: This speciality governs the collection of emotional, 
physical and psychological methods in which an interrogator 
can get a victim to break. Through an assortment of devious 
and unsavoury tactics, the character can get anyone to 
say what he needs them to. NOTE: Using this technique 

===PAGE 49===
 New Skills and Special Techniques
 New Skills and Special Techniques
48

===PAGE 50===
 New Skills and Special Techniques
49
against innocent citizens is a crime. It may be used against 
particularly vicious or dangerous perps if all other methods 
have been exhausted and the Sector Chief or Chief Judge 
sanctions it.
Knowing a true confession from a false one: 1-6 Seconds, 
Intelligence, Routine (+2).
Using pain or discomfort to elicit an answer to an inquiry: 1-6 
Hours, Inﬂ uence or Strength, Average (+0). 
Using emotional or psychological response to elicit an 
answer to an inquiry: 10-60 Hours, Inﬂ uence or Intelligence, 
Average (+0).
 Drive (Lawmaster)
The Lawmaster is more than just a hefty motorcycle: it is the 
workhouse of the Justice Department and a sophisticated 
crime-ﬁ ghting machine in its own right. A Lawmaster is ﬁ tted 
with a staggering array of devices, special adaptations, 
crime detection technology and weapon systems. The Drive 
(Lawmaster) skill therefore covers more than simply riding 
the most advanced road-going bike in existence; it covers 
the use of the onboard weapons and technology and the 
special riding techniques a Judge needs to use to get the 
best out of his vehicle. A Judge can use Drive (wheeled) at a 
skill level 1 less than his Drive (Lawmaster) skill, rather than 
at the –3 DM for untrained skill use.
Swerving around a routine obstacle: Instantaneous, Average 
(+0)
Cracking a perp round the back of the head with a daystick 
whilst keeping the Lawmaster under control: Signiﬁ cant 
Action, Average (+0)
Blasting away with bike cannon whilst travelling at speed 
and avoiding oncoming trafﬁ c: Instantaneous, Difﬁ cult (–2)
Skidding the bike under a low-hanging obstacle, coming 
through the other side and getting back into an upright 
position: Signiﬁ cant Action, Very Difﬁ cult (–4)
Using a ramp or other piece of debris to leap over an obstacle, 
chasm or span: Instantaneous, Very Difﬁ cult (–4)
 Law
Understanding the law is fundamental to a Judge. This skill 
covers how the law works; sentencing, exceptions, leniency, 
speciﬁ c cases and so forth. Every Judge emerges with 
Law when he graduates and undergoes regular refresher 
courses to remain competent; but some, like Judge Dredd, 
even study the huge and complex tomes of Mega-City Law 
in their spare time.
The Referee may apply a DM to the standard target number 
of 8+ depending on the circumstances. If, for example, a 
Judge sees a crime in progress, has witnesses and is in 
absolutely no doubt about the perp’s guilt, then a +4 DM is 
warranted. If circumstances are tenuous, based on hearsay 
or evidence is absent altogether, than a DM of –2 or –4 
might be warranted. For the most part, though, Judges need 
to roll Law 8+.
Sentence a perp correctly for a routine crime: 1-6 seconds, 
Very Easy (+4)
Sentence a perp for a more complex crime with different 
elements that might conﬂ ict: 1-6 minutes, Average (+0)
Assess a complex criminal case, with many disparate 
elements, each requiring separate consideration and 
sentencing elements: 10-60 minutes, Difﬁ cult (–2)
Assess the most ﬁ endish, complex cases, taking into 
account alibis, counter-claims, multiple, con ﬂ icting witness 
statements and clear expressions of guilt and mitigation: 1-6 
hours, Very Difﬁ cult (–4)
If a Law skill attempt is failed, then the Judge has failed to 
apply the law fairly and correctly, whether in interpretation or 
sentencing. Although appeals in Mega-City One are dif ﬁ cult 
to lodge (Judges are, after all, meant to be infallible), they 
are possible. Note the Effect of the Law roll’s failure. To 
successfully lodge an appeal, a perp needs to roll Advocate 
10+; he can use the Effect of the Judge’s failed Law roll as a 
positive DM to his appeal lodgement. If successful, another 
Judge reviews the case and adjusts sentencing accordingly 
– and may even set a perp free as a result. If the case is 
especially sensitive or contentious, the Judge who passed 
the original judgment may ﬁ nd himself called to account for 
his actions and even be subject to retraining, disciplinary 
action or, in the worst case, investigation by the SJS.
For example, Judge Madison has Law 2 and is sentencing 
a perp for jay-walking a skedway. The result of his roll is 2, 
modiﬁ ed to 4 for Madison’s Law skill: Madison sentences 
the jay-walker to 10 years in an Iso-Cube. However, this 
is still a failure with an Effect of 4 (4-8 = –4). The perp 
decides to appeal. He must roll Advocate 10+ but Madison’s 
overzealous behaviour has given the perp a +4 DM to his 
dice roll. The perp rolls 7; the DM takes this to 11 and the 
perp successfully appeals. Madison has some explaining to 
do as the perp walks free with a Cr. 1,000 ﬁ ne...

===PAGE 51===
 New Skills and Special Techniques
50
 Mega-City One Geography
Every good Judge knows the general layout of Mega-City 
One, its sector locations, old names and so forth and how they 
connect together; but you have an aptitude for the more detailed 
geography of the city, spending many hours pouring over the 
old maps, vid-displays, tri-dees and so on. The city’s layout and 
development fascinates you. This skill allows you to understand 
the geography of a particular district or sector intimately, recalling 
its layout and key landmarks/buildings without having to refer to 
your hand computer, bike computer or MAC.
Understand the general layout of a sector or area: 1-6 
minutes, Easy (+2)
Work out the best and fastest route between A and B: 2-12 
minutes, Average (+0)
Pinpoint a speci ﬁ c but unobvious building in an area or 
sector: 1-60 Minutes, Difﬁ cult (–2)
Recall the hidden, underground facilities, service shafts, 
tunnels and so forth, of a sector: 1-6 Hours, Very Difﬁ cult (–4)
 Mega-City One History
You have studied the history of Mega-City One, since its 
conception, right up to the present day (including all the 
wars, strange occurrences and, in particular, the history of 
its crime). This skill allows you to call on that knowledge to 
recall facts about a particular district or sector which may 
provide an insight into current happenings.
Recalling the general history of a sector: 1-6 minutes, Easy (+2)
Recalling a speciﬁ c historical event of a sector: 2-12 minutes, 
Average (+0)
Recalling an obscure, very speci ﬁ c fact about a sector: 1-6 
hours, Difﬁ cult (–2)
Recalling an obscure, very speci ﬁ c fact about a speci ﬁ c 
building, person or event: 1-6 hours, Very Difﬁ cult (–4)
 Street Perception
The Judge knows and understands how, to read the nuances 
of Mega-City One’s sprawling, chaotic streets. He can spot 
which areas are the likely hangouts of perps, when a Block 
War is likely to kick-off, when a gathering of juves is likely to 
result in a mugging and whether that illegally parked car has 
been strategically placed to assist with a robbery just about 
to happen. The Judge’s training has run through just about 
every criminal habit manifested and displayed in Mega-City 
One, its symptoms distilled and, through study, sleep training 
and simulations, the Judge is attuned to the environment.
Assessing the likelihood of a crime about to happen: 1-6 
seconds, Average (+0)
Spotting something incongruous that might signal something 
bigger: 1-6 minutes, Difﬁ cult (–2)
Getting the feel for the mood of a particular group of people, 
a city block and so on, even when all appears calm: 1-6 
minutes, Very Difﬁ cult (–4)
 Special 
Techniques
Special Techniques are the means by which Judges perform 
superhuman acts of dexterity, endurance and intuition, 
keeping them several steps ahead of the law breakers who 
would happily put a bullet in their back with a spitgun. Special 
Techniques typically modify certain elements of the rules 
(such as Combat) or augment certain skills (such as Drive 
(Lawmaster)). Others have more subtle effects. The description 
of each technique speciﬁ es how and when, it works. 
 Combat Acuity
The Judge is adept at reading the nuances of a tactical 
situation and greatly improves his chances of reacting ﬁ rst 
in a combat environment. He rolls Initiative on 3d6 rather 
than 2d6.
 Control Crash
When a crash is unavoidable, whatever vehicle he is 
controlling or a passenger of, the Judge is able to take the 
necessary steps to minimise damage to the vehicle, its 
occupants and innocent bystanders. The Judge is able to 
make either a Drive+Dexterity 8+ (if driving) roll or a Dexterity 
8+ roll (if a passenger); note the Effect:

===PAGE 52===
 New Skills and Special Techniques
51
Effect Result
6-7 The Judge uses such skill that the vehicle 
suffers minimal damage and all personnel 
inside and in the vicinity are unharmed.
4-5 The Judge signi ﬁ cantly minimises 
damage to the vehicle, occupants and 
all personnel in the vicinity; damage is 
reduced by three quarters.
2-3 The Judge minimises damage to the 
vehicle, occupants and all personnel in 
the vicinity; damage is reduced by half.
0-1 The Judge manages to reduce damage to 
the vehicle, occupants and all personnel in 
the vicinity; damage is reduced by a quarter.
–1 or less The Judge is powerless to prevent the 
crash. Full damage is sustained to the 
vehicle, occupants and all personnel in 
the vicinity.
 Crowd Control
The Judge understands the nature of crowds and mobs, 
being able to gauge their dynamics, ﬂ are-points and 
general tendencies to swarm. He can use techniques such 
as Formidable Presence and skills such as Persuade, on 
a number of people equal to his Inf multiplied by 10. His 
ability to project his authority into the crowd affects everyone 
within the Inf x10 range, assuming that he is also using a 
technique such as Formidable Presence or Persuasion to 
get the crowd to do his bidding.
 Data Access
The Judge is skilled in speed-reading, sifting and assimilating 
computer-based data. His mind acts almost as an extension 
of the data source providing the information, allowing the 
Judge to assess information quickly and accurately.
When using this technique, the Judge is able to assimilate 
and understand computer-generated data in a number 
of seconds equal to 20 minus his Int characteristic. More 
complex data sets might take longer; a highly complex 
case-ﬁ le might require 30 minus Int seconds or might take 
20 minus Int minutes, depending on how much detail is 
presented but essentially the Judge rapidly understands 
what he is presented with without having to waste hours 
reading and cross-referencing information.
 Dead Halt
The Judge can bring his Lawmaster or any other ground-
based/ground effect vehicle he is able to drive to a dead 
halt safely and under control. He does not need to make a 
Drive roll to successfully halt his vehicle and his senses are 
perfectly attuned to the circumstances around him.
 Dead Zone (Psi Judges Only)
Using this technique the Psi Judge reaches across the limbo 
of death and may use any power on a dead person or undead 
creature, treating the target as though it was still living. Using 
this technique costs an additional Psi point.
 Enhanced Power (Psi Judges Only)
The range for a psionic power can be doubled by the Psi 
Judge but the cost in terms of Psi points is +2 to the power’s 
base cost.
 Enhanced Talent (Psi Judges Only)
The Psi Judge chooses one power from any Talent 
permissible within Judge Dredd and adds it as a power 
usable under a Talent he already possesses.
 Formidable Presence
The Judge literally embodies the full majesty and weight of 
Mega-City law. His mere expression shows his command 
of both the situation and the facts at hand. Any Non-Player 
Character within a number of metres equal to the Judge’s 
Inﬂ uence characteristic must make a successful Endurance 
roll at 10+ or be rooted to the spot, paralysed with either fear 
and/or respect, for a number of minutes equal to the Judge’s 
Inﬂ uence characteristic.
 Inner Strength (Psi Judges Only)
The Psi Judge adds his End or Str characteristic DM (his 
choice) to any one Talent. The Talent must be speci ﬁ ed 
when taking this technique. If the technique is taken again, 
then a new Talent must be speci ﬁ ed. If the Judge has a 
negative or zero characteristic DM in Str or End, he gains 
a ‘wild card’ DM equal to 1d3. This roll must be made every 
time the Psi Judge uses the nominated Talent.
 Jerry-Rig (Tek Judges Only)
The Tek Judge can ﬁ nd a way to effect ad-hoc repairs 
using just the things he has around him and can use parts 
of a disparate machine to ﬁ x another. He is also able to 
rig two completely different devices together so that they 
work together, seamlessly; or to improvise the functions 
of one device within another (within the bounds of reason, 
of course). Using this technique takes 2d6 hours and the 
referee can increase this or set a minimum limit based on 
the circumstances, if he so chooses.
 Life Saver (Med Judges Only)
Med Judges are taught the advanced ﬁ rst aid techniques 
to save lives even when all looks bleak. If a Med Judge 
is confronted with a casualty who has reduced to 0 
characteristics (and no more than –4) through physical 
damage and can act within 3 rounds of the damage being 

===PAGE 53===
 New Skills and Special Techniques
52
inﬂ icted, he is able to apply rapid ﬁ rst aid that restores the 
injured person to 1 point in any one physical characteristic, 
snatching them from the very jaws of death, although the 
casualty will remain unconscious and requires immediate 
medical attention (through the Medic skill or a fully equipped 
Med Team or hospital).
 Oh, So That’s What It Does... 
(Tek Judges Only)
When faced with a piece of completely new or alien 
technology, the Tek Judge can, after studying it for 1d6 
hours, deduce its function. This is often accomplished 
through pressing various buttons, switches and levers but 
also involves a fair amount of standing back, sucking in 
breath and wandering around and around the device for a 
lengthy period, scratching his chin.
 Perp Sense
The Judge knows the signals perps and potential perps give 
out – even when they are successfully acting as innocently 
as possible – either just before or just after a crime. The 
Judge makes an Int+Streetwise (or Street Perception) 8+ 
roll. The Effect of the roll reﬂ ects the strength of the Judge’s 
intuition and accuracy in assessing the situation. Use the 
Perp Sense table below as a guideline.
 Rapid Aim
The Judge can aim and take bead on a target with lightning-
fast precision, even if the target is attempting to move into 
complete cover. The Judge anticipates what the opponent’s 
position will be and can lead his shot to make a precise hit. 
He must still take a minor action to aim, but he gains a +2 aim 
bonus for each minor action spent in setting-up the shot.
Perp Sense
Effect Result
6-7 The Judge is 100% certain that the perp has committed a crime or is just about to. He also senses 
what type of crime has been, or will be, committed based on the perp’s body language, mannerisms 
and any other vital signs (the bulge of a concealed gun; or an expensive chronometer on the wrist of 
a shabbily dressed mugger).
4-5 The Judge knows that the perp has just committed a crime or is about to and has a reasonable idea 
of the crime itself but needs further evidence to be 100% certain.
2-3 The perp is looking and acting as guilty as hell but the Judge needs to check with control to see if any 
crime has been reported and if that crime matches with the typical modus operandi associated with 
the kinds of signals the perp is giving out.
0-1 There is something not right about the way this perp or potential perp is acting. Detain and question, 
to be on the safe side.
–1 and less The Judge is suspicious but there is a lack of evidence for detaining and questioning the perp at this 
time. Might be worth additional surveillance or checking the area for recent criminal activity.
 Rapid Assessment (Tek Judges Only)
The Tek Judge is adept at immediately diagnosing a problem with 
a piece of machinery, based on knowledge, training, deduction 
and hunches. Repairing the problem still requires a successful 
Engineer or Mechanic roll but the diagnosis is always accurate.
 Rapid Diagnosis (Med Judges Only)
The Med Judge can take one look at someone and 
immediately understand, from a variety of physical and 
verbal symptoms, what is medically wrong with that person 
– be it Umpty Candy withdrawal, Stookie Gland addiction, 
Level 10 Rad Poisoning, and so forth.
 Recovery
Judges are trained to be resilient and work through the pain 
barrier. With this technique, your Judge remains conscious 
(but physically) inactive if reduced to 0 in two physical 
characteristics – when unconsciousness is the result for most 
people. He is also considered to be Seriously Wounded (see 
pages 74-75 of the Traveller Core Rulebook) if he has lost 2 
points from each physical characteristic.
 Sixth Sense
The Judge’s sixth sense alerts him to potential dangers to his life or 
the life of others, although the precise source of the danger cannot 
be spotted. If surprised or ambushed, the Judge always gets the 
chance to react, in a way most appropriate to the circumstances.
 Steady Hand (Tek Judges Only)
The Tek Judge maintains calm under pressure whenever 
dealing with delicate instruments, machines or circuits. He is 
able to ignore outside distractions, no matter how severe, to 
accomplish the task at hand.

The Judge immediately keys onto pertinent facts about a perp: height, colouring, hairstyle, gait and so forth. When tracking 
a perp through a crowd or into an area where sight can be easily lost, the Judge still manages to follow his quarry without
