progress within his Sector, major crimes elsewhere in 
the city, alerts for back up by other Judges, crime status 
and arrest reports for cases other Judges in the Sector 
are handling and newsfeed bulletins on general Mega-
City One news. 
Cable Winch
Mounted under the rear fairing, just behind the wheel, is a 
Tikkra TT cable winch with 20 yards of line. Fully motorised, 
the cable is stressed for weights of over one ton, though the 
Lawmaster can realistically pull only half of this before losing 
stability.
Communications Array
The comms array provides audio and visual contact with 
both the Sector House and Justice HQ. The unit’s vid-screen 
transmits as well as receives video and is voice activated. It 
contains a hand-held comms unit, connected to the array 
with 2m of coiled cable. Fitted into the corner of the unit is the 
small red Emergency button: this is the Judge’s SOS alarm, 
which immediately summons other Judges by sending out 
a blanket alert signal to all other Lawmaster comms arrays 
within the Sector. Use of the Emergency button is a last 
resort: Judges are expected to be self-reliant and, if back 
up is needed, to communicate the request via Sector House 
control (so that Judges not engaged in tackling crime can be 
despatched, rather than disturbing all Judges in a Sector). 
The Emergency button is therefore rarely used, and when it 
is, it signals a dire need for additional assistance.
 Daystick
The daystick is a metre-long club made from reinforced 
plastisteel. Used in crowd control or simply when blunt trauma 
is preferable to a Standard Execution round, the daystick 
is sometimes nicknamed the ‘Two Second Lobotomy’. The 
daystick’s bulk means that most Street Judges leave the 
weapon in the Lawmaster’s stowage, bringing it out only 
when necessary – although it can be hooked onto the utility 
belt if a Judge needs to carry it as a deterrent or an active 
weapon.
The daystick functions as a Club ( Traveller Core Rulebook, 
page 98)
Firerock Tyres
The wide, plastisteel and rubber tyres are high performance, 
run-ﬂ at and bullet proof. Despite their width, they still 
The standard ﬁ xtures and ﬁ ttings of the Lawmaster are as 
follows:
Armoured Saddle
The saddle is ﬁ tted with 12mm plastisteel armour, which 
affords 12 points of armour protection to the Judge if targeted 
from the rear. The saddle itself is made from memory foam, 
which contours to the Judge’s frame and offers maximum 
comfort for the long hours spent on patrol.
Automatic Mode
The Lawmaster has a full automatic function which allows 
it to manoeuvre accurately, either according to a Judge’s 
communicated instructions or to co-ordinates programmed 
into its onboard nav-computer. Its weapon systems can be 
activated remotely but only via a Judge’s direct command.
Operating a Lawmaster in automatic mode requires the 
Judge to make a Drive (Lawmaster) roll at a –2 DM, although 
he gains a DM for his Remote Operations skill, if he has it.
 Bike Computer
The Synitron GK13 autopilot computer forms the heart of the 
Lawmaster and is responsible for keeping a Judge on his bike 
and active, even in the most adverse of conditions. In order 
to achieve this, the bike computer’s gyro logic units make the 
entire vehicle both light to control and yet very stable as they 
constantly make corrections to the bike’s speed and position 
on the road. The bike computer is sophisticated enough to 
control the Lawmaster without a Judge’s input altogether. 
Linked into the Likron GS 4T telecommunications unit, the 
bike computer is also capable of connecting its Judge with 
virtually any resource of the Justice Department quickly 
and easily, with the minimum of interaction from the Judge 
himself. The Likron GS 4T has a maximum range of well over 
260 kilometres. Just like the Lawgiver, the bike computer 
can accept verbal commands and relay information either 
verbally or on its high-resolution screen. The bike computer 
combines an automatic target acquisition system with ﬁ re 
control, allowing it to ﬁ re the Lawmaster’s weapons without 
intervention from the
 Judge. The computer is equipped with 
the following software:
• Automode/0 – Governs all automatic and remote 
operations.
• Navigation/1 – A full set of Mega-City One navigation 
charts that govern its navigation systems. The software 
gives a +1 DM to a Judge’s Mega-City One Geography 
skill or allows him to use Mega-City One Geography at 
0 (if he does not have the skill) when riding the bike.
• Fire Control/0 – The weapon system software allowing 
the Lawmaster to operate its weaponry via remote 
commands.

offer excellent handling in all weather, and most terrain, 
conditions.
Lamps
Each Lawmaster has ﬁ ve standard headlamps and a single 
Nite-Eye ﬂ ood and infrared lamp. The standard headlamps 
are arranged in two banks on either side of the Nite-Eye: the 
array consists of two main lamps and then three back-up 
lamps, which are used in fog or heavy rain. The Nite-Eye’s 
intensity in ﬂ ood mode illuminates a 10 metre, 180-degree 
arc of darkness to daylight levels; in infrared mode, is used 
to spot heat traces, such as recent footprints (although such 
heat signatures tend to dissipate within 10 minutes).
 Scattergun
Holstered on the side of the fuel tank, this shotgun-like 
back-up weapon is pump-action and ﬁ res either standard 
12-gauge plastisteel shot, plasteen baton rounds or stumm 
gas rounds. The Scattergun can hold up to 12 rounds in its 
magazine and different rounds can be mixed in the same 
magazine or individually loaded into the breech. This weapon 
works exactly as a shotgun, as per page 99 of the Traveller 
Core Rulebook.
Siren
All Lawmasters carry a high-powered siren to alert citizens 
of a Judge in hot pursuit of a perp. The maximum power 
setting of a Lawmaster’s siren is deafening, having a clear 
range of about a mile, though that is of little discomfort to 
a Judge wearing his helmet. Senior Judges are sometimes 
permitted to alter the tone and frequency of their sirens so 
as to create a distinctive sound, allowing perps to know just 
who is tracking them down.
Storage Panniers
Straddling the rear wheel, the storage panniers are armoured 
(6 points of protection) and carry the following items:
• Spares for Bike Cannon, Bike Computer, Communications 
Unit, Cyclops Laser, Headlamps, Nite-Eye Lamp, 
Lawgiver and Vocal Synthesiser.
• Ammunition for Bike Cannon, Scattergun, Lawgiver.
• Cling Net (x2), Cuffs (x2), Spare Medipack, Rad Suit, 
Respirator and Stumm Grenades (x10).
 Lawmaster Armaments
The Lawmaster comes replete with a pair of forward-
mounted 20mm bike cannon and a single, powerful, Cyclops 
laser, housed in the conical collimation focus on the very 
front of the bike and below the Nite Eye.
All the armaments on the Lawmaster can be controlled 
manually, vocally and remotely, with pre-programmed ﬁ re 
instructions given to the bike computer (‘Smash through that 
door, halt after 10 metres and give it both barrels, rapid-ﬁ re, 
with the bike cannon, for 3 seconds...’).
The cannon are linked but can be ﬁ red individually. The 
cannon’s magazines hold 200 20mm shells apiece. The 
Cyclops laser is power-heavy; one burst can be ﬁ red every 
10 rounds (60 seconds), with the laser’s independent 
powerplant recharging from the engine in between.

Message Pod
A special shell that is used with the bike cannon is the message pod. This hollow shell is ﬁ tted with a homing device

at a speed of 200 kilometres per hour until it reaches its homing threshold and drops to a safe retrieval speed.
Every Lawmaster carries three message pods.

===PAGE 104===
 Equipment
103
General 
Mega-City One 
Equipment
The following items are commonly found in Mega-City One 
and are available to citizens, Judges and perps alike.
 Armour and Clothing
Anti-Mugging Suit
First judged too dangerous to be used by the citizens of 
Mega-City One, these armoured suits with vicious spikes 
were later deemed legal by Chief Judge McGruder.
Chameleon Suit
Bending light around the wearer through the use of an alien 
photochromatic material, this lightweight body suit grants a 
+4 DM to all Stealth skill rolls.
Energy Shield
A small, belt-attached device of alien manufacture, the energy 
shield is both rare and expensive, yet in great demand. 
Generating an invisible shield of pulsating energy around any 
medium-sized or small object, this device provides an advanced 
level of protection and is capable of turning aside the strongest 
laser beam or shell – the user gains 12 points of armour 
protection. The internal power system is good for 10 hours of 
continuous use before it needs recharging. Note: the energy 
shield does not provide any protection against melee attacks.
Exo-Suit
Restricted by the Justice Department to factory and 
warehouse use, the exo-suit is a semi-robotic body that is 
strapped in place over the wearer’s own limbs and torso. 
As well as granting a great deal of protection in combat, the 
servo-motors and hydraulics that power the exo-suit greatly 
magnify the wearer’s own strength. An exo-suit will grant a 
+3 bonus to Strength but a –2 penalty to Dexterity whilst it 
is worn.
Radsuit
This all-in-one suit grants the wearer protection from radiation 
exposure and contains a one-hour self-contained air supply. 
The radsuit may be worn over other clothing and armour 
without penalty. The radsuit protects against up to 600 rads 
(see Traveller Core Rulebook , page 142), both initial and 
cumulative exposure.
Pad Armour
Every juve worth his place on the streets knows what the 
current height of fashion is, usually involving improbably 
large knee, elbow and probably shoulder pads. Older punks 
recognise the defensive beneﬁ ts of this ‘pad’ armour and so it 
is popular amongst any who are regularly involved in brawls. 
Pad armour is of little use in a gun ﬁ ght but can provide a 
measure of protection against knives and clubs.
Pad armour offers 4 points of armour protection against 
melee attacks. 
Scramble Helmet
A lightweight helmet with advanced electronics designed to 
block all psionic intrusions, the scramble helmet is an illegal 
device often used by perps who wish to avoid the all-seeing eye 
of the Psi Judges. The scramble helmet penalises any psion 
using Talents or Powers against the wearer with a –6 DM.
Shell Jacket
The shell jacket is standard equipment for the vast majority 
of Citi-Def units and comprises of layers of toughened kevlar 
laminated to a thin plasteel compound. The result, whilst 
heavy, provides a great deal of protection for those expecting 
to enter heavy combat.
A shell jacket provides 10 points of armour protection against 
all attacks.
Sports Armour
An evolution of pad armour, sports armour adds a helmet and 
chest protection, often with plastisteel ribbing within the pads 
to greatly enhance its durability. Though usually seen on the 
jetball ﬁ elds, street versions are available, ostensibly so fans 
can show support for their favourite teams. However, sports 
armour is also common amongst tap gangs who expect to 
face resistance to their operations. It is very common for 
citizens to pay three or even four times the listed amount 
for their sports armour, as the most popular teams typically 
charge a great deal more for their of ﬁ cial merchandise and 
any juve seen supporting a mediocre team is likely to get into 
a great deal of trouble amongst his peers.
Sports armour provides 6 points of armour protection against 
melee attacks.
Thermal Masking Suit
Through using re ﬂ ective and infrared-absorbing materials, 
this thin and lightweight suit is capable of completely 
suppressing the wearer’s heat signature. A character wearing 
a thermal masking suit is invisible to infrared imaging (such 
as a Judge’s Mk II helmet infrared visor ﬁ lter). In addition, 
heat-seeking rounds such as those carried by the Lawgiver 
cannot lock onto the wearer’s heat signature.

Standard Equipment
Bedset
A citizen never knows when he must leave his apartment, 
block or hideout and sleep rough. This bedroll will ensure a 
good night’s sleep wherever he must lay his head. Consisting 
of an integral sleeping bag and self-in ﬂ ating airbed, the 
bedset can be deﬂ ated, rolled and stored in a small, pouch-
sized covering that ﬁ ts into a carryall for easy portability.
Carryall
This is a simple synthi-leather backpack that is strapped 
to a character’s back, allowing the easy carrying of many 
small objects.
Chain, Plastisteel
A 5 metre length of plastisteel chain can support up to 
1,000kg in weight and withstand up to 20 points of direct 
damage delivered by a ballistic or energy attack.
Communicator
Dozens of different styles of personal communicator are 
available to the citizens of Mega-City One, from tiny throat 
mikes of extremely limited range, to powerful vehicle-
mounted models capable of sending clear messages past 
the upper reaches of the atmosphere to spacecraft waiting 
in high orbit. The maximum range of each communicator is 
listed by its entry in the table below. It should be noted that 
whilst any communicator may be set to a ‘private’ frequency

105
to avoid interference, the Justice Department is capable 
of monitoring them all. Using a communicator requires a 
Comms skill roll of 4+. Calibrating a communicator to evade 
Justice Department detection and monitoring requires a 
Comms roll of 12+.

Crowbar
Used by many unsophisticated criminals to defeat 
electronic locks, the plastisteel crowbar may also be used 
as a crude weapon if they are discovered breaking in to 
a citizen’s apartment. Treat as a Club ( Traveller Core 
Rulebook, page 98).
Face-Changing Machine
Heavily regulated by the Justice Department, a citizen 
may have his face literally changed within ten minutes 
through the use of this specialised atom-scrambling 
machine. Virtually impossible to obtain without extensive 
checks being made into a citizen’s background most 
are purchased on the black market, where they prove 
incredibly expensive. However, actually owning a face-
changing machine permits perps to alter their identities 
without being tracked by the Judges – although voice, 
DNA and fingerprint identifiers remain unchanged.
Most face-changing is handled through licensed premises 
such as the popular Nu-You chain found across the city. 
All such parlours are required to provide a list of clients, 
with images of their new look, to Justice Central on a daily 
basis.
Fire Foam
Designed to give a single powerful burst of ﬁ re-quenching 
foam, this small canister allows a character to ﬁ ght ﬁ res. The 
use of ﬁ re foam extinguishes medium-sized ﬁ res, taking 1d6 
rounds to do so.
Firearms Permit
Virtually impossible for any normal citizen to get hold 
of, permits for ﬁ rearms are issued only by the Justice 
Department and they are strictly controlled. Citizens wanting 
one must demonstrate an impeccable character history and 
demonstrate they are both trustworthy and have a de ﬁ nite 
need of a weapon. This usually requires an Advocate or 
Broker 12+ roll, with a further DM for Social Standing.
The forgery and sale of ﬁ rearms permits on the black market 
is therefore rife. Genuine ﬁ rearm permits are only ever signed 
over to one individual for a single weapon and it has been 
known for many counterfeit permits to make the mistake 
of listing several weapons. The details of every ﬁ rearms 
permit issued by the Justice Department is kept on record 
within MAC and is immediately accessible to every Judge, 
although citizens are also given a small slug containing the 
relevant information. Counterfeit ﬁ rearms permits are highly 
unlikely to have records altered within MAC. 
Flashlight
A simple handheld xenon torch to provide illumination 
within darkness. Provides a beam of light that extends in 
a cone up to 200 feet long and 20 feet wide. The battery 
is widely available for Cr. 2 and is good for 1,000 hours of 
continuous use.
Grappling Hook
A far cry from the relatively sophisticated sucker gun and 
claw grapple, the grappling hook is merely a ﬁ xed metal, 
multi-pronged hook attached to the end of a 20-metre line. A 
grappling hook can support 200 kilograms of weight before 
snapping.
Hammer
A simple tool with a multitude of uses in mechanical, technical 
and computer ﬁ elds. The hammer may be used as a club in 
melee combat.
Handcuffs
Though a far cry from the Justice Department issue, these 
handcuffs are moulded from toughened plasteen and are still 
capable of holding a subject ﬁ rm. An Athletics (co-ordination) 
12+ roll must be made to be free from the handcuffs. 
Infrared Visor
This is a lightweight but technologically sophisticated visor 
that will allow any character to see in absolute darkness up 
to a distance of 10 metres by registering the heat signatures 
of his surroundings and nearby creatures.
Life Support Machine
Standard issue within hospitals and well-equipped 
ambulances, the life support machine promises salvation to 
even those grievously wounded. It takes a Signiﬁ cant Action 
and a Medic 8+ roll to properly hook up a wounded character 
to a life support machine; however, once inside, the character 
is automatically stabilised, no matter how badly wounded he 

===PAGE 107===
 Equipment
106
is and will be kept alive regardless of the effects of poison, 
disease or radiation, until specialist medical aid is available.
Lock, Electronic
The standard measure to secure any box, door or car within 
Mega-City One, electronic locks can be relatively cheap, 
though many wealthier citizens are keen to invest in the very 
latest technology to protect their belongings. Those bought on 
the black market may not be automatically opened by a Judge’s 
override card and are thus illegal. Locks have a security rating 
between 8 and 15. Any character with an electronic toolkit may 
by-pass an electronic lock as a Signiﬁ cant Action by making 
an Engineer (electronics) roll using the security rating of the 
lock as the target to throw on 2d6.
Lock, Mechanical
Virtually obsolete in Mega-City One, mechanical locks 
provide a measure of false security to poorer citizens. In 
practice, such mechanical devices operated by keys do 
little more than slow down a perp intent on gaining entry to 
whatever it protects. Mechanical locks have a security rating 
between 2 and 8. Any character with a mechanical toolkit 
may bypass a mechanical lock as a Signi ﬁ cant Action by 
making a successful Mechanic roll using the security rating 
of the lock as the target to throw on 2d6.
Lock Hacker
A more primitive device than a Judge’s override card, the 
lock hacker is a small plastic slug that allows a citizen to 
break through any electronic lock with a successful Engineer 
or Mechanic roll using the security rating of the lock as the 
target to throw on 2d6. The hacker can also be used on 
mechanical locks as it is thin enough to slide between doors 
and frames. 
The possession of a lock hacker will earn a citizen at least 3 
years in an iso-cube if he is caught with one by a Judge.
Medipack
This medipack contains magnabind bandages, painkiller hypos 
and usually, anti-rad pills, allowing a character to treat almost any 
injury. Each medipack is good for one use only. The standard 
medipack contains a variety of dressings that can be used to 
arrest bleeding and prevent further damage from complications 
if a successful Medic roll is made. The medipack does not, 
itself, augment the Medic skill, but it contains the essential items 
necessary to apply First Aid at the scene, pending additional 
medical treatment at a suitable facility.
Respirator
This is a full-face mask designed to ﬁ lter out harmful 
gases and atmospheres. However, it also has its own self-
contained 10 minute oxygen supply. If attacked by gas whilst 
not wearing a respirator, a character must make a Dexterity 
8+ roll in order to ﬁ t it in time.
Riot Foam Cutters
An unlicensed and highly illegal version of Justice 
Department issue equipment, a pair of riot foam cutters 
utilises sharp cutting blades combined with a chemical agent 
that neutralises the foam on contact. These cutters can free 
a citizen trapped in riot foam in one standard action.
Rope
Constructed of high tensile, woven plasteen ﬁ bres, the common 
rope is still a tool of great use in the high-technology metropolis 
of Mega-City One. A standard length of rope is 10 metres.
Scope
There are many different models of scope available in Mega-
City One but they are all designed to accomplish the same 
function. Mounted upon any weapon of large size or greater, a 
scope incorporates telescopic lenses and laser range-ﬁ nding 
to greatly increase the accuracy of any shot ﬁ red at extreme 
ranges. A scope provides a +1 DM to any weapon that has 
been aimed, as per the Traveller Core Rulebook, page 102.
Silencer
A silencer deadens the sound of ﬁ ring from any projectile 
weapon. Fitting a silencer is a Signi ﬁ cant Action and the 
weapon will issue no more than a dull, muf ﬂ ed thud within 
3 metres; beyond that, nothing can be heard. A silenced 
weapon may not be rapid- ﬁ red. Only projectile pistol or ri ﬂ e 
weapons may be ﬁ tted with a silencer.
Speedheal Machine
Once stabilised by a life support machine, a seriously injured 
character greatly bene ﬁ ts from a speedheal machine. Only 
found in Sector Houses and the best of hospitals, the 
speedheal machine is an expensive device that, through 
molecular manipulation, rapidly cures and heals any subject 
placed within its sensors. It requires a Medic 8+ roll to 
successfully operate a speedheal machine and a subject 
cannot speedheal himself. The machine provides healing as 
per Medical Care ( Traveller Core Rulebook , page 75) but 
does so within 6 hours, rather than 24.
Toolkit, Electrical
Designed to allow a character to perform various repairs and 
modiﬁ cations to high technology devices such as advanced 
weapons, vehicles and robots, the electrical toolkit provides 
all the tools necessary. Engineer (electronics) rolls are made 
in conjunction with the electrical toolkit; some tasks are 
impossible without one but in general, the toolkit provides a 
+1 DM to the skill roll.

===PAGE 108===
 Equipment
107
Toolkit, Electronic
Comprising of a set of high technology tools such as 
chip decoders, circuit analysis scanners and the like, the 
electronic toolkit is essential for any Engineer (electronics) 
or Computers roll made to disable, repair or modify devices 
such as computer systems, electronic locks and robot 
brains.
Toolkit, Mechanical
A set of basic and low technology tools (screwdrivers, 
spanners, feeler gauges and so on) with which to perform 
any mechanically-based task, such as repairing a vehicle’s 
engine, breaking through a mechanical lock or modifying a 
simple weapon. The toolkit is essential for some tasks that 
make use of either the Engineer or Mechanic skill.

Craze Equipment
Mega-City One has dozens of legal and illegal crazes, many 
of which rely on equipment, drugs and augmentation to 
make them possible.
Appetite Inducer
An illegal ingredient necessary to produce the largest fatties 
in the city. Proscribed by the Justice Department, just a few 
drops of an appetite inducer will cause a subject to crave 
food above all else for a period of one hour. Greater doses 
can have a disastrous effect as the subject will be forced 
to make an Endurance 8+ roll every round for 1-3 hours or 
be forced to consume whatever objects are nearby, be they 
dead, living or inanimate.
 Batglider
Also called a bat suit, this is an all-in-one clothing set with 
large, ﬂ exible wings stretching between hands and feet 
that, when ﬁ lled with oncoming air, provide lift, allowing the 
wearer to glide. Batting is incredibly popular within some 
blocks and the air around their peaks is often ﬁ lled with 
batters every evening, catching thermals and experiencing 
a kind of freedom previously unknown to them. A batglider 
can support up to 150kg and provides ﬂ ight for up to 60 
minutes. The wearer must make a Flyer (personal) 8+ roll 
to successfully control the batsuit. The batglider, possessing 
no engine, is absolutely silent in use.
 Belliwheel
An essential device for any fattie wishing to retain a degree 
of mobility, the belliwheel is simply a sitting device on which 
two heavily reinforced wheels are mounted. A fattie sits 
upon the belliwheel and moves himself around with his legs, 
supported by the device. A belliwheel will change a fattie’s 
speed to 6 metres per Combat Round and permit him to run 
(instead of waddle).
 Boing®
Launched as a miracle spray, Boing® envelops its user in 
an almost impervious bubble, allowing him to bounce from 
the highest blocks without harm. Often seen as the best rush 
available in Mega-City One, any use of Boing® outside of the 
Palais-de-Boing® is rewarded with a mandatory sentence 
of twenty years in an iso-cube. Using a can of Boing® is a 
Signiﬁ cant Action. Whilst encased in Boing®, a character is 
immune to any amount of falling damage and gains armour 
to the equivalent of 30 points. If a Boing® bubble takes any 
amount of ﬁ re damage, it will immediately stick to the next 
surface it strikes, holding the user immobile until cut free. 
Anything struck by a fast-moving bouncing Boing® bubble 
sustains 4d6 points of damage and causes the bubble to 
bounce away. A successful Dex or Athletics (co-ordination) 
8+ roll halves this damage. Boing® can only be safely 

===PAGE 109===
 Equipment
108
removed through the use of specialised Boing® cutters. It 
may only be purchased legally in the Palais-de-Boing® and 
its removal from the building is not permitted – but this does 
not prevent unscrupulous perps – Boing®-Peddlers – from 
gaining access to illegal supplies and selling aerosols to 
eager Boingers® at vastly in ﬂ ated prices. The sentence for 
illegally supplying Boing® is forty years in an iso-cube.
Boing® Cutters
A set of heavy-duty cutters, not unlike bolt-cutters, this device 
secretes a chemical agent along its blades that tears through 
Boing® quickly and easily. Their availability is restricted to 
the Justice Department and the Palais-de-Boing® in order to 
cut down on the use of illegal Boinging® in public.
Ganja
Currently enjoying a resurgence within Mega-City One, 
Mega-Skunk is generally agreed to be the best strain of 
this home-grown weed and is guaranteed to mellow out any 
citizen. Naturally, it is highly illegal.
Instant Fuzz
Available in a spray canister, this is spray-on hair, attaching 
itself to any exposed skin and growing exponentially to create 
thick, matted fur. This is typically used against unsuspecting 
victims, who are not harmed by the experience but are likely 
to be left completely humiliated until they can wash or shave 
it off.
Para-Glider
The forerunner of the batglider, para-gliders are large wings 
attached to a frame in which one or two citizens sit or lie 
before hurling themselves off the tallest block they can ﬁ nd 
and soaring for many hours. The batglider has now overtaken 
this model in terms of popularity and most are now found 
only within the armouries of Citi-Def units mad enough to 
contemplate airborne assaults. Like the batglider, this device 
is silent in use and operates in a similar way. It can carry up 
to 500kg in weight.
Spray Paint
Specially formulated spray paints may be purchased, 
resistant to all attempts to remove their stain aside from the 
Justice Department’s own clean up squads. Available in a 
variety of colours.
Sugar
Listed as being one of the less potent drugs available on the 
black market, the Justice Department works hard to quash any 
possession for it not only causes extreme damage to teeth but 
often leads to harder substances. No Judge believes a citizen 
has the willpower to take just a little sugar each day to improve 
his life and stay immune to its addictive effects.
 Umpty Candy
Declared illegal, with its inventor, Uncle Ump, being exiled 
to roam the galaxy aboard a single spaceship, Umpty Candy 
is in exceedingly short supply and thus commands vast 
sums, affordable by only the insanely rich. Nevertheless, 
secret Umpty Parties, fuelled by small amounts of Umpty 
Candy, are popular with Mega-City One’s wealthy. The 
candy is, perhaps, the most delicious taste ever created. 
It is also highly addictive. Anyone consuming more than 
1d6+1 portions (about the size of an average chocolate 
bar) becomes addicted. He must make an Endurance 10+ 
roll every day to control the cravings (or starts to suffer 
hallucinations involving giant bars of Umpty Candy) and to 
prevent from becoming violent when denied a supply.

Food, Drink and Shelter
 Munce and Foodstuffs A, B 
and C
A bewildering variety of foodstuffs are available to Mega-City 
One’s citizens. By far the most common are those derived 
from munce, a disgusting plant-derived protein paste that 
can be reﬁ ned through many processes to create almost 
any taste and texture: few citizens realise what they are truly 
eating. Other synthetic foodstuffs abound, all produced by 
the competing corporations of Mega-City One. A relatively 
new entry into the food market is the Justice Department 
itself, producing the extremely tasty Foodstuffs A, B and 
C. Being a Justice Department product, no ingredients are 
listed on the packages of these foods but many citizens are 
coming to agree that these are the very best tasting meals 
on the market, despite the bland name. 
Living Accommodation
Every family in Mega-City One is assigned a single 
apartment within one of the immense city blocks towering 
over the streets and skedways. Most Judge characters 
reside in Sector House dormitories. However, some may 
eventually have a three-roomed apartment in a Justice 

===PAGE 110===
 Equipment
109
Department-owned skyscraper, rather than a city block, and 
it is not uncommon for several Judges to share several ﬂ oors 
of a building or for an entire building to house nothing but 
Judges.
A standard apartment consists of a living room, bathroom 
and bedroom, modestly equipped with reasonable furniture, 
a vid screen, computer terminal (with access to MAC and 
the Sector House system) and cooking facilities. At this 
standard, the Justice Department pays all accommodation 
costs but if a Judge wants something fancier or larger, he 
will have to ﬁ nance it himself somehow.

Special Mega-City One Services
The advanced technology available in Mega-City One 
means that many remarkable, desirable and often illegal 
services are within the grasp of citizens and, thus, perps. 
New services, based on the clever and creative use of 
technology appear every few months, last for as long as the 
craze does and then quietly lapse into obsolescence. Others 
endure and these are described here.
 Face-Changing
Heavily monitored by the Justice Department, there are 
many face-changing clinics dotted around the sectors of 
Mega-City One. Utilising molecular-scrambling technologies, 
these clinics change the appearance of any citizen, willing 
to pay the fee, within minutes and for many of the richer 
residents this can almost turn into a craze, with faces being 
changed every week. However, the Judges keep track of 
all face-changing operations, automatically updating their 
Public Surveillance Unit computers, in order to foil attempts 
by perps on the run to conceal their identities.
Using a Face Changing clinic, such as Nu-You, costs Cr. 
1,000 for a basic operation. The customer selects the 
face type he wants from a series of menu options and the 
molecular ﬁ zzog conﬁ guration machines get to work. More 
radical operations cost upwards of Cr. 1,000, usually in 
blocks of Cr. 500. It is possible to have one’s face changed 
to approximate an alien, a ﬁ lm star or a historical ﬁ gure but 
such operations always cost premium prices.
When using a Face Changer the customer must roll 2d6. 
On a natural roll of 2 something untoward has happened: a 
malfunction or other error. Roll 1d6 for the result:
1. Face is changed to something completely against 
what the client ordered.
2. Client is rendered ugly.
3. Client is rendered very ugly.
4. 1d3 facial features are distorted (diminished or 
enhanced) giving the appearance of mutation.
5. 1d3 facial features are scrambled – moved around 
the face and giving the appearance of mutation.
6. 1d3 facial features are removed and not 
reassembled; the affected area is covered with a 
perfectly smooth patch of skin.
 Palais-de-Boing®
Boing® returns every few years as a popular craze within the 
city but, thus far, the only place where a citizen may legally 
use the miracle spray is within the giant Palais-de-Boing®, 
a block-sized structure ﬁ lled with tubes, gigantic pinball 
machines and high drops designed to provide the maximum 
enjoyment for the Boing® enthusiast. It is a sad fact, 
however, that many users still consider a Boing® through 
the actual city to be the ultimate thrill. An hour’s worth of 
time in a Palais-de-Boing® costs Cr. 200 and includes both 
Boing® spray and being cut out of the stuff at the end of the 
session.
 Smokatorium
Smoking tobacco of any form (the hugely expensive ‘real’ 
tobacco or the more common synthibac, made from munce) 
is illegal anywhere in Mega-City One with the exception of 
a Smokatorium. Here, smokers, clad in protective, ﬁ shbowl-
helmeted suits, puff away in their thousands, ﬁ lling the levels 
of the Smokatorium with a vastly poisonous mixture of 
chemicals that are, eventually, ﬁ ltered and removed. Anyone 

===PAGE 111===
 Equipment
110
stepping into a Smokatorium without breathing apparatus 
must succeed in an End 10+ roll or sustain 1d6 suffocation and 
poisoning damage to his Endurance characteristic for each 
round that he remains in the Smokatorium unprotected.
It costs Cr. 50 for half an hour’s use of the Smokatorium, 
which includes suit rental and a complimentary packet of 3 
synthibac cigarillos (different brands and types of smokes 
can be purchased at the adjoining Smokatorium shop, with 
prices varying from Cr. 20 for a basic pack of 20 cigarettes, 
up to Cr. 10,000 for the ﬁ nest, vintage, Cuba City cigars.
Taxi Costs
It costs Cr. 5 for each kilometre travelled within one of the 
millions of taxis circulating the streets and megways of Mega-
City One. Human drivers are gradually being phased out in 
favour of robo-taxis which are a great deal more reliable and 
cheaper for the companies that run them, but a citizen will 
be able to ﬂ ag down a taxi within minutes in any but the most 
run-down slum sectors of the city.
 Weapons
Weapons can only be held legally with a Justice Department 
permit but, for those lucky enough to be granted a licence, 
there is an array of weaponry available.
Antique Revolver
Relics from the 20
th and 21 st Centuries, many citizens 
possess an antique revolver illegally, having had it passed 
down through the generations of their family as an heirloom. 
Six rounds are carried in each antique revolver, which must 
be loaded individually.
Claw Grapple
A far more powerful version of the sucker gun, this weapon 
is most often found in the armouries of Citi-Def units, though 
they are also popular amongst criminal organisations. 
Pneumatically powered claws enable the grapple to bite 
into the toughest of materials, allowing the wielder to either 
swing along the high-tensile mono ﬁ lament cable or select 
the retract switch and be pulled up to the grapple head. 
A maximum weight of 200kg may be suspended from the 
cable of a claw grapple. Firing a grapple successfully into 
its target requires a roll of Dex 9+ with further DMs for Gun 
Combat. The reel has a maximum range of 1,000 metres 
and, when set to winch its load, the rate of travel is 50 metres 
per 100kg of load.
Club
Not strictly a weapon as such, the club is any heavy, metre-long 
implement a citizen may pick up to smash the brains of another.
Concussion Grenade
Also called the stun grenade, this weapon is designed to 
simply subdue several enemies at once, rather than blast 
them apart. All characters within this weapon’s area of effect 
must make an End 10+ roll or be stunned for 1d3 rounds.
Electro-Prod
Used to herd wild creatures in the Cursed Earth, a few 
electro-prods have made their way into Mega-City One, 
where they are used as torture devices and by criminals 
who wish to avoid facing murder charges. The electro-prod 
causes electrical subdual damage.
Electra Zap Gun
A brightly coloured and decorative weapon, the electra 
zap gun has clean lines that were designed to intimidate. 
However, its electro-charge is utterly harmless and merely 
stuns its victims for a short period of time, making it suitable 
for the perp who does not wish to add a murder charge 
to his time in the iso-cubes or who constantly deals with 
heavily armoured enemies. The electra zap gun has a 
maximum range of 2 metres and any target struck is dealt 
an electrical jolt of 2d6 points of damage to a single physical 
characteristic. However, the effects of the electra zap gun 
are notoriously short-lived and injuries recovery at the rate of 
1 point every round. The greatest bene ﬁ t of the electra zap 
gun is its twin-barrelled design, making it extremely potent 
at such close ranges. The weapon uses a light power pack 
which holds enough energy for 40 shots.
Fire Bomb
Fire bombs are explosive grenades designed speci ﬁ cally 
to spread ﬁ re. Fire bombs deal 1d6 points of ﬁ re damage 
within their area of effect of 3 metres. A successful Dex 8+ 
or Survival 8+ roll halves the damage. The bomb continues 
to burn for 1d6 rounds, generating a further 1d6 points of ﬁ re 
damage every round until extinguished.
Flesh Disintegrator
A monstrous and highly illegal exotic weapon, most often 
used as a booby trap to protect vulnerable areas in a perp’s 
lair, due to its immense size. Any living creature caught within 
its area of effect must make either a Dex 8+ or Survival 8+ 
roll or be automatically slain as their ﬂ esh is disintegrated 
from their bones. The ﬂ esh disintegrator uses a heavy power 
pack, which holds enough energy for 5 shots.

111
Assault Carbine, West and 
Smithson .78 Magnum
A carbine-format assault weapon that is a favourite amongst perps wanting to pack hefty ﬁ repower, the assault carbine is ﬁ tted with a 
ﬂ ash suppressor, a fully retractable and detachable stock and a 60-round capacity magazine. The weapon can be set to Rapid Fire , 
allowing it to ﬁ re in Burst Mode, as described under the section on Automatic Weapons on page 62 of the Traveller Core Rulebook.


===PAGE 113===
 Equipment
112
Hand Bomb
One of the simplest of all modern weapons, the humble 
grenade still finds its way into the hands of perps in 
Mega-City One. Though often superseded by the hi-ex 
pack and concussion grenade, which both do a better job 
of causing devastation or subduing enemies respectively, 
the hand bomb is a cheap alternative for street gangs and 
low-life punks. 
Hand Cannon, West and 
Smithson .408 Recoilless
A fat, stubby handgun ﬁ tted with a gauss ﬁ eld generator along 
the length of the barrel, the hand cannon is very accurate 
at short ranges, completely recoilless and packs a massive 
punch with its .408 ammunition. Some variations of the hand 
cannon include an integral xenon projector which casts a 3 
metre diameter burst of daylight up to a range of 10 metres, 
when activated, bypassing any penalties for darkness. The 
magazine ﬁ ts into the handgrip and holds 18 rounds.
Hand Gun, Walther Mitsubishi 
PPK II
Probably the most common weapon on the streets of Mega-
City One, the PPK II is a good, solid design, enabling even 
the clumsiest citizen to successfully threaten his enemies. 
Both light and powerful, handguns form the staple of many 
criminal organisations, particularly in the lower ranks. The 
magazine of this weapon holds 20 shots.
Heavy Duty Disintegrator
Typically used in industrial construction and demolition, 
the licences for heavy duty disintegrators are rigorously 
controlled by the Justice Department, thus making them 
command a huge price on the black market. Highly effective 
at creating holes within walls and other obstructions, their 
effect on living creatures is lethal. Any living creature caught 
within the area of effect of a heavy duty disintegrator must 
make either a Dex 8+ or Survival 8+ roll or be automatically 
slain. The heavy duty disintegrator uses a heavy power pack 
which holds enough energy for 16 shots. 
Heavy Laser, Tac Systems Mx. 
Light Blaster
Legal heavy lasers in Mega-City One are to be found on the 
rebuilt City Wall bordering the Cursed Earth in the hands of 
Judges, though a few also exist within Citi-Def units. They are 
highly prized by perps looking to cause maximum damage in 
their criminal activities, for each heavy laser is fully capable 
of blasting apart almost any vehicle or small building with a 
single high-powered shot. Usually found in shoulder-mounted 
models, the heavy laser weighs an incredible amount but it 
can be difﬁ cult ﬁ nding a more powerful or versatile weapon 
in the same class. The heavy laser uses a heavy power pack 
which holds enough energy for 50 shots.
Heavy Spitgun, 
Rasterblaster RB-32
Manufactured by the renowned rapid ﬁ re experts, 
Rasterblaster, this larger spitgun features an elongated 
barrel, huge magazine capacity and a bipod to support its 
weight for stable ﬁ ring. It retains the high rate of ﬁ repower of 
the spitgun and, unlike its smaller cousins, has an ammunition 
capacity that allows it to keep ﬁ ring in extended battles. The 
magazine of the heavy spitgun holds 120 rounds.
Hi-Ex Missile
A simple, no-brainer weapon, the hi-ex missile is ﬁ red from a 
launcher and is capable of causing a vast amount of damage 
at great ranges.
Hi-Ex Pack
A ﬂ at-pack, high-explosive charge, this weapon has 
molecular hooks on its main surfaces, allowing it to literally 
stick to any surface on an accurate throw (Dex 8+ or 
Athletics (co-ordination) 8+). Though sometimes used as 
a large grenade, the hi-ex pack is more commonly used 
as a time-delayed bomb and is capable of causing a great 
degree of devastation when well placed. The blast can be 
delayed from initial priming for between 1 second and 24 
hours through a simple digital readout. Placing and priming 
a hi-ex pack is a Signiﬁ cant Action.
Homing Missile
Sacriﬁ cing an over-sized warhead for a tracking system and 
complex ﬂ ight controls, the homing missile is the payload of 
choice for those demanding the highest accuracy from their 
heavy weapons. As long as at least one signi ﬁ cant action is 
spent aiming, with this missile loaded in a launcher, the homing 
missile can lock on to any target of small size or larger, granting 
the wielder a +4 DM to his attack roll. A homing missile ﬁ red 
without spending at least one action aiming receives no DM.
Hypo Dart Gun
Developed from the tranquilliser gun used by med-squads 
to pacify kooks and futsies, the hypo dart gun carries a 
powerful tranquillising agent designed to knock out any 
living creature within seconds. A living target hit by a hypo 
dart gun must make an End 10+ roll every round for 1d6 
rounds or fall unconscious for 1d3 hours. However, the hypo 
dart is useless against armoured opponents. Any target that 
wears more than 2 points of armour protection is immune to 

===PAGE 114===
 Equipment
113
its effects. The hypo dart gun holds only one dart at any one 
time and must be manually reloaded after each shot.
Las-Burner
Designed as an industrial cutting tool, some perps favour the las-
burner as an assault weapon, despite its great bulk. Intended to 
cut through solid plastisteel girders and walls with ease, it can 
also make light work of the strongest bank vaults and safes. Any 
character with a Strength of less than 10 using a las-burner as a 
melee weapon suffers a –2 penalty to attack rolls.
Laser Lance, Weptek LL Mk II
A rare weapon that can be dif ﬁ cult to ﬁ nd and even harder 
to ﬁ nance, the laser lance is developed along the same 
principles as the laser riﬂ e but concentrates a huge amount 
of energy within its red-hot capacitors before releasing it in 
a torrent of searing light several inches in diameter. The 
laser lance can cause more damage than almost any other 
laser weapon of its size, though at the expense of draining 
its power pack at a far faster rate. The laser lance takes one 
Combat Round to recharge its capacitors after ﬁ ring and may 
not be used during this time. The laser lance uses a medium 
power pack which holds enough energy for 6 shots.
Laser Pistol, Walther 
Mitsubishi LPK I
Rare due to its sheer expense, the LPK I is an awesome 
weapon with perhaps only the Justice Department’s own 
Lawgiver matching its raw power as a pistol. The thin beam 
of coherent light emitted with each shot from a laser pistol is 
capable of causing serious injury and, despite the high value 
they command on the black market, they are becoming 
increasingly common on the streets, in the hands of perps 
seeking the ultimate killing-power. The laser pistol uses a 
light power pack which holds enough energy for 20 shots.
Las-Knife
Ostensibly a common kitchen utensil found in the homes 
of many citizens, the las-knife is also popular amongst 
skilled assassins looking for a completely untraceable, 
silent weapon. It retains all the disadvantages of an ordinary 
plasteen knife but has a much keener edge and is capable of 
causing serious injury when in the right hands.
Las-Saw
Commonly found in factories, sales of the las-saw are not 
restricted by the Justice Department and many citizens 
with a constructing or artistic bent in their crazes possess 
one. Though it does not have anything like the raw cutting 
power of the industrial strength las-burner, the las-saw is 
nevertheless a useful tool and its light weight enables it to 
be wielded effectively in combat.
Lazooka, General Arms Laser 
Destroyer Mk III
Confused by some as a missile launcher until it ﬁ res, the 
lazooka is seen by many Citi-Def personnel as the ultimate 
development in laser technology. Fired over the shoulder, 
just like a missile launcher, the lazooka expends the entire 
energy of its power pack in one, barely-controlled blast of 
unleashed energy. The lazooka uses a heavy power pack 
which holds enough energy for just a single shot.
Light Disintegrator
A light, hand-held device no larger than a pistol, this 
weapon packs lethal killing force and possession alone 
will send a citizen to the iso-cubes for a minimum of 6 
years. Working on the same principles as the more 
powerful heavy duty disintegrator, this version is fully 
capable of rendering a human to his component atoms. 
Any living creature hit by a light disintegrator must make 
either a Dex 6+ or Survival 6+ roll or be automatically 
slain. The light disintegrator uses a light power pack 
which holds enough energy for 3 shots.
Missile Launcher, Mauley H/HE 
‘Snub-Nose’
The missile launcher is a relatively cheap heavy weapon, 
though users will soon ﬁ nd its relative cheapness is 
outweighed by the cost of each missile it ﬁ res. However, it 
proves to be both a powerful and versatile weapon and yet 
remains simple to use. The missile launcher may only hold 
one missile at any one time and so must be reloaded every 
time it is ﬁ red.
Robowhip
Used to discipline troublesome robots, the two-metre long 
robowhip is covered with ﬁ ne electrodes producing a current 
of high voltage but low wattage that causes a mere tingle 
in a human but is incredibly painful to a droid. Each strike 
by a robowhip upon a robot will cause 1 point of damage 
to the robot’s Endurance that is recovered within one hour. 
A robot reduced to 0 Endurance by a robowhip will not be 
destroyed but instead disabled for a period of one hour, until 
its Endurance begins to return.
Sawn-off Stump Gun, General 
Arms Sg-1 X
A common modi ﬁ cation made to many stump guns is to 
reduce the length of their barrel. Aside from making the 
weapon far easier to carry and conceal, it also forces the 
pellet shot to spread out in a far greater arc, potentially 
causing greater casualties though at a greatly reduced 
range. The sawn-off stump gun stores 8 rounds, pumped 
from beneath its barrel, which must be loaded individually.

===PAGE 115===
 Equipment
114
Laser Rifle, Mauley M500 
‘Hunter-Stalker’
Laser riﬂ es are even more expensive than laser pistols but pack a far more powerful punch. They are favoured by killers 
who prefer to attack at great ranges in order to avoid detection and many will be retro ﬁ tted with advanced scopes and 
target ﬁ nders. The laser riﬂ e uses a medium power pack which holds enough energy for 10 shots.


===PAGE 116===
 Equipment
115
Smoke Bombs
Sometimes used by Justice Department riot squads to quell 
mobs or ﬂ ush out perps, many types of smoke bomb have 
made their way into the hands of citizens. Throwing up a 
dense cloud of smoke, they make perfect tools for covering 
an escape or confusing an enemy. Any character caught 
within its area of effect suffers a –4 DM to all attacks.
Sonic Cannon, Hondai 
Ultrasound
Normally found only mounted on larger vehicles, the sonic 
cannon is used by both the Justice Department and Citi-
Def units to pacify large mobs quickly and easily. The sonic 
cannon causes subdual damage to every living creature 
within its area of effect. A sonic cannon may be used against 
vehicles, buildings and other solid structures in the same 
way as the sonic blaster delivering 3d6 points of damage 
automatically every round without the need for any attack 
rolls, ignoring any Hull points or armour. When not directly 
powered by a vehicle it can accept a large power pack, which 
provides enough energy for 2 shots.
Spear
The spear is a primitive weapon and most citizens would 
expect to see one only in the hands of a primitive undercity 
dweller or a Cursed Earth mutant. However, some juves will 
take to sharpening long lengths of plasteen girder in order to 
use them in gang disputes when more powerful weapons are 
not available. The spear has reach and can strike opponents 
up to 2 metres distant. A spear can also be thrown.
Spitgun, Weptek 12mm
Using ammunition similar to that of a hand gun but slightly 
larger in calibre, the spitgun is a carbine-format ri ﬂ e with a 
high rate of ﬁ re. Spitguns can ﬁ re a single shot, a burst of 
three shots or on full automatic mode. The spitgun magazine 
holds 30 rounds.
Spit Pistol, Rasterblaster 
RB-03
A tiny weapon of lethal capability, the spit pistol owes 
much in design to its larger cousins but compacts a 
high rate of fire into a pistol no larger than a handgun. 
Its small magazine holds enough ammunition for only a 
few seconds worth of continuous firing but it is a highly 
concealable weapon that has gained a lot of respect 
amongst criminal organisations. The magazine of a spit 
pistol holds 12 rounds.
Stump Gun, General Arms Sg-1 X
The stump gun is a ri ﬂ e-sized weapon that ﬁ res cartridges 
ﬁ lled with many individual pellets. It is not capable of 
causing the damage of a traditional bullet or shell-based 
ﬁ rearm but instead throws its shot over a greater target 
area, potentially wounding several targets at once. A 
mechanical toolkit and a Mechanic 8+ roll turns a stump 
gun into a sawn-off stump gun. Failing the Mechanic roll 
ruins the weapon, rendering it unable to ﬁ re until a further 
Mechanic 10+ roll can be made to rectify the damage. The 
stump gun stores 8 rounds, loaded individually, pumped 
from beneath its barrel.
Sucker Gun
An older version of the claw grapple, sucker guns can 
now be picked up relatively cheaply and are not even 
classed as illegal by the Justice Department, though 
their actual use often is. The sucker gun has neither the 
range nor the strength of a claw grapple and uses a gas-
powered ‘sucker’ on the end of its line in order to gain 
a purchase on walls and ceilings. A maximum weight 
of 100kg may be suspended from the cable of a sucker 
gun. A normal attack roll is made to attach the sucker 
gun to a solid surface.
Zip Gun, Mauley ‘Madam 
Protector’
Often the ﬁ  rst gun possessed by a punk just embarking 
on his criminal career, the zip gun is of laughable power 
when compared to more sophisticated laser or rapid ﬁ re 
technology but it is still more than capable of slaying the 
average citizen with a single shot. Cheaper than a handgun, 
the tiny design of the zip gun limits the calibre of shells ﬁ red 
but hardened perps tend to pass it over simply because it 
lacks any ‘intimidation’ factor.

===PAGE 117===
 Equipment
116
Double-Barrelled Stump 
Gun, General Arms Sg-1 XX
There are several versions of the common stump gun, where two barrels and ﬁ ring chambers are mounted in tandem, 
either side-by-side as with the Sg-1 XX or one atop the other, allowing the wielder to ﬁ re both simultaneously. This weapon 
counts as being a twin-linked stump gun. It stores 8 rounds for each barrel, which must be loaded individually.


===PAGE 118===
 Equipment
117
Sonic Blaster, Hondai 
MegaSound
The sonic blaster is a riﬂ e-sized weapon typically found in service with the rare human sewer-sweeping teams who, whilst not 
permitted to ﬁ eld lethal weaponry, must still protect themselves from the mutants and monsters that exist in the dark, ﬁ lth-ridden 
tunnels. A sonic blaster may also be used against vehicles, buildings and other solid structures to great effect – literally shaking 
them apart. If both the sonic blaster and a non-living target are stationary, 2d6 points of damage will automatically be caused  
every round without the need for any attack rolls, ignoring any Hull or other damage reduction. The sonic blaster uses a medium 
power pack which holds enough energy for 40 shots.

===PAGE 123===
 Megways and Skedways
122
The roads of Mega-City One are therefore incredibly complex 
and extremely dangerous. Navigating the complexities of the 
city is impossible without GPS navigation, something that is 
ﬁ tted as standard on every road-going vehicle, along with 
reliable autopilot and autodrive systems which help keep 
road trafﬁ c accidents to a minimum. Every Judge has, as 
part of his regular shift pattern, a stint of Road Trafﬁ c Detail, 
cruising the road systems and watching for traf ﬁ c violations 
and other crimes, such as mopad piracy and car jacking. 
Even Judges, with their intensive training, need to rely on 
their bike computer to reliably navigate around Mega-City 
One although each Judge can be expected to know the road 
systems for his own sector without needing to consult his 
nav computer every few minutes.
Guide to 
Mega-City 
One Transit 
Systems
The different forms of roadways and pedestrian access 
ways are bewildering: this section de ﬁ nes the different 
transportation options available in Mega-City One.
Back Street
Two-way passages, usually no wider than two metres, Back 
Streets are found in the oldest and often most neglected, 
areas of the city – typically in Old Town (old central New 
York) and City Bottom. Back Streets are notorious gang-
haunts, rat-runs for muggers and scum of more violent 
varieties and generally far away from the general attention 
of the Judges. Periodic clean-up details of a sector’s back 
streets see Judges descend, in teams, into City Bottom and 
the old areas to clean-up and clear-out the perp dross that 
accumulates there. These Back Street Blitzes are one of the 
few mass-Judge lightning raids that citizens openly welcome 
and sometimes petition for, because, for a while at least, the 
Back Streets become safe again for decent citizens (many 
of whom cannot afford to live anywhere else).
 Boomway
A one- or two-way, multi-level megway (between four to 
ten lanes wide and two to four levels high) that cross a 
wide expanse between major megways. Boomways are 
equipped with high-side walls and rails to prevent vehicles 
from tumbling off the road and plummeting anything up to 
a mile down. To ensure that accidents are minimised the 
Boomways are under constant surveillance by Sector 
House trafﬁ c control and are equipped with multiple sensor 
and vehicle automated override systems to prevent barrier 
ruptures.
 BroadWay
Any large pedestrian plaza, typically found between City 
Blocks. Broadways are usually busy with pedestrian traf ﬁ c 
at all hours (vehicles are banned from crossing Broadways 
– save for Judges needing to use them as thoroughfares if 
in legitimate pursuit) and often home to street-vendors and 
opportunist perps such as taps.
Crossway/Crosslink
Any pedway intersection (see Pedway on the following page).
 Eeziglide
A one-way, conveyor system for pedestrians. Eeziglides 
convey foot traf ﬁ c at a steady 5km per hour over, under 
and around broadways, pedways and crossways. Vocalised 
warnings keep pedestrians informed about Eeziglide rules: 
no running, no walking or running in the opposite direction, 
advanced noti ﬁ cation of an Eezliglide’s termination point, 
and a stream of advertisement for services, shops and 
businesses located near to the Eezliglide’s embarkation and 
disembarkation points.
Filter
A one-way exit or entrance to and from parking areas.
Flyover/Overzoom
A skedway that passes over a City Block, with connections 
and ﬁ lter lanes into a block itself. By their nature, Flyovers 
and Overzooms are amongst the highest roadways in Mega-
City One and therefore subject to the same automated safety 
features as Boomways.
Inter-Block Zoom
Mega-City One’s elevated, enclosed, maglev train-system 
which replaced the old Sky-Rail network in the late Twenty-
ﬁ rst Century. Interblock Zooms provide a link between all the 
city blocks in any given sector. During a Block War, Sector 
House trafﬁ c control immediately locks-down all Inter-Block 
Zoom transit capsules to prevent them from carrying either 
assailants or bombs into enemy blocks. Riding the Inter-Block 
Zoom costs Cr. 1 per trip or Cr. 5 for ten trips, if purchased 
in advance from the automated/robot ticket vendors in the 
Inter-Block Zoom interchange Broad-Ways.
Intersect
Any junction between a pedway,megway, skedway or other 
form of trafﬁ c conduit.
Judge’s Lane
A two-way road that runs parallel to major roadways and 
reserved for Justice Department usage. Observation ramps 
provide convenient parking for Lawmaster-mounted Judges 

===PAGE 124===
 Megways and Skedways
123
on trafﬁ  c detail, along with strategically located speed 
cameras to monitor both speedsters and slowsters. Judge’s 
Lanes are used by all Justice Department vehicles, from 
Lawmasters to Pat-Wagons and it is an offence (up to 1 
year in the cubes) for unauthorised vehicles to use them. 
Obs-Ramps are stationed every 5 kilometres along a stretch 
of road and are positioned reasonably high up to afford 
excellent visibility.
Median Strip
The central reservation running between two directions 
of roadway. Protective barriers line the Median Strip, 
preventing accidents in one half of a road spilling into the 
other. Most Median Strips are between 2 and 8 metres in 
width, depending on the type of roadway and number of 
lanes present on each side. Wider, faster megways have 
wider Median Strips. Information boards posted at regular 
intervals along the Median Strip offer traf ﬁ c information, a 
constant stream of advertisements and Justice Department 
reminders of the penalties for common traf ﬁ c violations 
(‘Speed Up! Or Slow Right Down to A Year in the 
Cubes!’).
Mega Circular
These bi-directional, six-lane megways bypass downtown 
sector trafﬁ c to join together major megways. The Mega 
Circulars are typically very fast and very congested stretches 
of road: vehicles tend to average speeds of 300kph or faster 
and the constant ﬂ ow of trafﬁ c across and through Mega-
City One means that the Mega Circulars are never quiet. It 
is the joining of North and South Mega Circulars that form 
the notorious Devil’s Island, a special prison completely 
surrounded by thundering traf ﬁ c, used by the Judges for 
punishing criminals who are deemed too dangerous even 
for the iso-cubes.
 MegWay
Megways are the largest road format in Mega-City One. 
Bi-directional and between four and twenty lanes wide, the 
megways run both across and up through Mega-City One 
for its whole breadth and length. Megway is the general term 
for these major, multi-lane highways and where a megway 
passes through a particular sector it may be given a local 
name that allows for easier differentiation and re ﬂ ects the 
original roadways that the megway replaced. Thus, a stretch 
of megway might be known as megway 10 in general but as 
the Sarah Palin X-Pressway where it passes through Sector 
21 and around the Sarah Palin City Block.
 Parkarama
Ground vehicle parks, usually many stories high and fully 
automated: a citizen drives his vehicle into a loading bay 
and robotic systems take the car to a free bay and stores 
it until the citizen comes to collect it. Parkaramas can hold 
thousands of vehicles in a variety of sizes from personal 
transport buggies up to mopads (although it is rare for a 
mopad to ever actually park-up) and parking charges are 
generally pricey (anything from Cr. 10 per hour through to 
Cr. 100 at peak times). Some parkaramas are still of the DIY 
variety where the driver has to cruise around the labyrinthine 
parking bays hunting for a parking space. Such manual 
parkaramas, usually in the poorer districts of a sector, are 
prime areas for taps and car jackers, who lurk in the shadows 
waiting for an easy mark or a desired vehicle, and then 
strike, relatively con ﬁ dent that they can escape with their 
crime. Some of the manual parkaramas are so large and so 
maze-like that it is not uncommon for citizens to get lost for 
hours or days at a time, racking up parking charges whilst 
trying to ﬁ nd a space, a parked car or simply just the way out. 
As the manual parkaramas are often privately owned, and 
owned by criminal gangs in some cases, they are a way of 
making easy money from unwary motorists who simply want 
somewhere safe to leave their car and do a little shopping. 
The costs of using them – both in terms of legitimate fees 
and the risks of being mugged or having one’s car stolen 
– make such shopping trips hideously expensive.
 Pedway
Pedestrian-only walkway found right across Mega-City One 
and at all levels of the city’s buildings. Pedways close to 
ground level are open to the elements but raised pedways – 
those hundreds of metres above street level – are enclosed 
either by high-sided, transparent plastisteel walls or full 
plastistell tubes. Subpeds are enclosed pedways that run 
under main pedways and beneath roads and buildings.
Podport
Hover vehicle parks; similar to parkaramas but designed for 
vehicles using hovercraft technology or antigrav units.
Skedway
Single direction highways of between one and ﬁ ve lanes. 
Interskeds, or feedways, connect one skedway to another. 
Underskeds are single-lane roads, often reserved for public 
service trafﬁ c, that pass underneath skeds. Overskeds are 
similar but pass above the skedways.
Sky-Rail
The now obsolete monorail public transit-system introduced 
in the early 21st century. One third of Mega-City One still uses 
the Sky-Rail network pending upgrades to the zoom-system. 
Sky-Rail uses a conventional, single track with electrically 
powered, remote-operated or robot piloted cars. The largest 
Sky-Rail intersection in the City is Hell’s Junction.

===PAGE 125===
 Megways and Skedways
124


===PAGE 126===
 Megways and Skedways
125
 Slipzoom
Found at larger interchanges, Slipzooms are one-way 
on/off ramps of between one to four lanes, used for larger 
interchanges. An underzoom (also known as a ﬂ yunder) is 
a single-lane road reserved for public service trafﬁ c passing 
under a Slipzoom.
 Superslab
The longest megway in Mega-City One, bisecting the city 
from north to south. The Superslab is twenty-four lanes, 1,220 
kilometres of poured plastisteel and ferrocrete that runs in 
a straight line through the city but undulates between City 
Bottom and the higher regions of City Blocks and is joined by 
hundreds of skedways, mega circulars and boomways, laced 
into the infrastructure by slipzooms and underzooms. The 
Superslab tunnels under the immense buildings of the city in 
many places, including the infamous Fox Tunnel, scene of the 
climax of Supersurf 7 and shot, backwards, by Chopper (AKA 
Marlon Shakespeare, the Midnight Surfer) in his daring bid to 
win the world Skysurf championship. The Superslab never 
sleeps, never stops, with trafﬁ c thundering along it morning, 
noon and night. Buildings close to it are specially strengthened 
and noise-insulated to cope with the incessant roar of the 
twenty-four hour trafﬁ c but this does not prevent the Superslab 
from driving some citizens to distraction. ‘Slab-happy’ is the 
term given to those who derive some form of comfort and 
peace from the proximity of the Superslab, whether that is 
living on it (in a mopad) or near it; Justice Department Med 
Centre studies have found that the noise and vibration of the 
Superslab has a kind of hypnotic effect on many citizens and, 
when removed from its environs, they become anxious and 
prone to panic attacks, unable to cope without the constant 
background roar of the immense highway.
 Wayby
Small parking zones set aside along megways and skedways 
at regular intervals where drivers can pull-off and temporarily 
park their vehicles. Waybys tend to be crowded round the 
clock; both by parked vehicles and cheap roadside cafes 
selling poor-quality munce products and beverages.
 Zipstrip
One or two-way pedestrian walkways linking City Blocks, 
sectors and smaller interchanges. Enclosed zipstrips, 
whether elevated or not, are called Pipeways.
 Zoomtube
The most recent traf ﬁ c innovation in Mega-City One. A 
zoomtube is a fully enclosed road-system where all traf ﬁ c 
is fully computer-controlled for optimum speed and driver-
safety. Only vehicles ﬁ tted with Zoomtube compatible 
control overrides are permitted to ride the Zoomtubes. 
These electronic control management systems plug into 
the vehicle’s powerplant and hand all control of the vehicle 
over to the pre-programmed Zoomtube system. Vehicle 
speed, proximity, lane occupancy, and so on, is constantly 
monitored and adjusted to keep traf ﬁ c moving at a steady 
180kph (faster, when and where permissible) and to regulate 
the volume of traf ﬁ c entering and leaving the Zoomtube. 
Emergency vehicles and Lawmasters are, naturally, exempt 
from Zoomtube Control – and just as well, because, for 
all the failsafe mechanisms and integrity of the Zoomtube 
computer infrastructure, they are not infallible. Even minute 
discrepancies in control procedures could result in a vast and 
catastrophic pile-up, especially where Zoomtubes intersect 
with megways and, especially, the Superslab.
 Vehicles
Fossil fuels, as the main source of fuel for vehicles, were 
generally replaced by cheap, ef ﬁ cient hydrogen fuel cells 
and bio-diesel hybrids in the early decades of the 21
st 
Century. Further developments introduced fusion-powered 
engines and now almost all Mega-City One vehicles are 
relatively quiet, non-polluting and highly energy ef ﬁ cient. A 
fad still remains for the petrol-burners of way-back-when 
with antique car collectors paying huge sums for even the 
rustiest, most clapped-out relic from the pre-war days.
The vehicles featured below are described in the following 
terms:
Speed Limits for Mega-City One Highways
Some Mega-City One highways have a minimum speed limit for vehicles; drivers using these highways that drive beneath the 
minimum speed limit (‘Slowsters’) are liable for arrest.
Highway Type Minimum Speed Maximum Speed
Megway/Mega Circular 120kph 500kph
Skedway/Flyover/Overzoom — 250kph
Superslab 200kph 500kph
Zoomtube — 180kph

Dimensions
Height: The vehicle’s height in millimetres, centimetres or 
metres (for larger vehicles).
Width: The vehicle’s width, measured at its widest point, in 
millimetres, centimetres or metres (for large vehicles).
Length: The vehicle’s length, from nose to tail in 
millimetres, centimetres or metres (for larger vehicles).
Wheelbase: The width of the vehicle’s wheelbase in 
millimetres, centimetres or metres (for larger vehicles).
Weight: The vehicle’s weight in kilograms (or tons, for 
larger vehicles).
Fuel Capacity: How much fuel, in litres, the vehicle can 
carry.
Powerplant: The make and type of the vehicle’s engine/
powerplant.
Full Speed: The maximum speed, in kilometres per hour, 
the vehicle can attain.
Max Speed: The vehicle’s maximum speed in metres per 
Combat Round.
Acceleration: The vehicle’s rate of acceleration in metres 
per Combat Round.
Deceleration: The vehicle’s rate of deceleration in metres 
per Combat Round.
Cost: In Credits.
Statistics
Skill: What skill is required to drive or use the vehicle.
Agility: How easy the vehicle is to drive, expressed as a 
DM to the driver’s skill check.
Crew: How many passengers, including the driver, the 
vehicle can carry.
Open/Closed: If the vehicle is open or closed (see 
Traveller Core Rulebook, page 66).
Armour: How much armour, if any, the vehicle has (see 
Traveller Core Rulebook, page 66).
Hull/Structure: The number of hits the vehicle can 
sustain before being disabled.
Cargo: The amount of cargo that can be carried, in 
kilograms.
Weapons: On the rare occasions where a vehicle is 
armed, what armaments it carries.
The statistics entries relate to the standard vehicle handling 
rules found on page 103 of the Traveller Core Rulebook. 
Any additional information about a vehicle’s handling or 
performance is given in the description.
Block Buggy
A common feature of every modern city block, very few block buggies are owned by citizens but are instead the property of the city. Given the 
sheer size of most city blocks, even the most able-bodied citizen may have trouble traversing the length of even a single ﬂ oor, and so simple, 
easy to use block buggies are supplied for the use of the inhabitants in order to safely take them from park, to mall, to home again without 
fuss. The buggy is powered by a standard, rechargeable, Hi-Lux battery which gives an operating time of 8 hours between charges. It can 
comfortably accommodate a single passenger and up to 100kg of additional cargo – although, when fully laden, its range drops to 6 hours of 
battery life and its speed halves. The buggy is reasonably agile and the driver gains a +1 DM to his Drive skill when using the vehicle.

Citi-Def Gunship, Sirocco
Slowly being phased out in favour of less manoeuvrable ground-based vehicles, due to a number of Block Wars where its 
armaments caused large numbers of casualties, many Citi-Def units cling hopelessly to their prized Sirocco gunships. Fast, well 
armed and armoured, the gunship is the perfect vehicle for destroying light targets and transporting small squads whilst under 
heavy ﬁ re. Unfortunately, it is these same qualities that have made it lethal during Block Wars, rather than in the defence of the 
city as was originally intended.
The gunship is powered by the Kyrix neutro-grav, anti-gravity power unit of a kind similar to Justice Department H Wagons. The 
Sirocco is the most common gunship variant found amongst Citi-Def crews but other variants have similar characteristics.

Mo-Pad, General DT-10 Humber
Mo-pads are permanently mobile habitation units, perpetually cruising the megways and skeds, their occupants itinerant citizens 
who rarely pause in their movement save for vital refuelling provision stops at the vast, mobile service units that also ply th e 
highways. Citizens forego the comfortable, static life of the city block in favour of independence and an opportunity to see more 
of Mega-City One than most citizens will realise.
Mo-pads carry all mod-cons: a living/dining/galley area, bedrooms, showering facilities, entertainment units and a spacious cab. 
Using the mobile services that ply the meg-lanes, there is no need for a mo-pad citizen to set foot on static ground.
The DT-10 Humber is a prime example of an older mo-pad found on the Mega-City One highways. It gives a lot to newer models 
in terms of size and comfort, though it is still able to keep up to the minimum speed limits on the megway, where most examples 
spend their entire working lives. The interior can be described as basic and the autopilot is notoriously unreliable in emergency 
situations, forcing drivers to at least be present in the cramped cockpit at all times. However, owners tend to scrape and save  
every credit they can ﬁ nd in order to upgrade the autopilot and better equip the living quarters.

Mo-Pad, Mercury Royale
Mercury’s entry into the lucrative mo-pad market was to be in a blaze of glory and, in truth, they created a vehicle which had 
few equals on the megways of the city. A truly immense vehicle, the Royale had every feature and luxurious extra that could be 
squeezed into its giant hull, including a full swimming pool, enclosed roof garden and wall-spanning Tri-D screens. There was 
truly no better way to travel in Mega-City One. Unfortunately, citizens forced on to the roads of the city were rarely in a position 
to lay out the credits for such a mo-pad and so very few were ever sold. The Royale is an incredibly expensive mo-pad, due to 
Mercury’s constant price changes designed to recoup development costs and so has become a status symbol of sorts in high 
society, purchased by citizens and corporations that like to create an impression during business negotiations

Powerboard, Chowder Boards McKenzie Extreme
Seen by many juves as the ultimate sport, powerboards are a craze that sweeps through Mega-City One every few years. 
Taking the form of a jet-powered, ﬂ ying surfboard, powerboards were made famous by Supersurf champion Chopper. A simple 
throttle is operated by the rear foot of a powerboarder who uses the weight of his body to guide the machine through any 
manoeuvre. When the inevitable happens and the user falls from his powerboard, it immediately halts in mid-air, keeping the 
user safe from harm as he dangles from a safety line attached to one of his ankles. Cheap and easy to maintain, the McKenzie 
Extreme is one of the most popular powerboards in the world, though it lacks the top end speed of later models.

Street Bike, Krapasaki TD-4
The dream of many young juves, the Krapasaki is known for being a cheap, no-frills bike designed only for thrilling speeds. 
Despite many Lightweight measures taken, the Notron 2000cc v4 powerplant cannot hope to match the raw output of the Otomo 
or Lawmaster but as a cheap alternative for the young, it has proved a good seller for Krapasaki.

Justice Department Vehicles
The Justice Department maintains an enormous and diverse ﬂ eet of different vehicles. Every Sector House has its own Justice 
Garage, administered and maintained by Tek Division, which keeps, for Sector use, a compliment of Pat, Meat and H wagons. 
Larger vehicles, such as the Manta and Land Raider, are specialised vehicles that must be called-in from Justice Central.
 Pat Wagon – Mk III
After the Lawmaster, the Mk III Pat-Wagon is the most commonly sighted Justice Department vehicle seen on the streets of 
Mega-City One. Designed to be ﬂ exible enough to bring the law to citizen riots and armoured perps, as well as ful ﬁ l the grunt 
work of catch and meat wagon teams, the Mk III Pat-Wagon is well respected amongst the Judges for being able to take just 
about any punishment the streets can give. The turret-mounted street cannon is a specialised variant of the bike cannon found 
on the Lawmaster and is capable of ﬁ ring a variety of ammunition, such as ﬂ are, gas and solid rounds. The Pat Wagon also 
carries a Riot Foam dispenser used to encase and quell angry mobs during riots, demonstrations and Block Wars. 
The Mk IV Pat-Wagon, an anti-grav version of the Mk III, is also common in many sectors and grants freedom to pursue perps 
over rougher ground than its predecessor. The Mk IV has space for only 1 ton of cargo and 8 passengers, due to the increased 
size of its powerplant.

The Manta Prowl Tank was the Justice Department’s answer to a growing crime wave. Often regarded as a mobile ﬂ oating

Two pilots control its great bulk with an exceptional agility, whilst four Street Judges constantly monitor the on-board scanners 
for any sign of local crime, ready to take the Law to perps outside upon Lawmasters also stored within the Manta’s forward 
bay. There are compartments that allow the onboard Tek and Med Judges to function effectively, whilst more Street Judges 
monitor the holding cells, communications and weapon systems of the tank, while overall command is given to a senior Judge.

 Land Raider
A highly specialised combat vehicle, the Lezney A2000 Land Raider is a two-part vehicle that links to form a single ﬁ ghting

The Land Raider’s most famous assignment was the trek across the Cursed Earth undertaken by Dredd and Spikes Harvey 
Rotten to deliver the vaccine for the 2T-FRU-T virus af ﬂ icting Mega-City 2. This was considered a trial mission and, although

to advance the Land Raider programme beyond the six or so already built and held at the Tek Division’s Vehicle Testing HQ.
