Notable City 
Blocks:
Sector 13 has seven major city blocks and around a dozen 
smaller blocks (conapts, luxy-blocks, crock-blocks and so 
on). The seven city blocks are:
Buzz Aldrin Block
Population: 35,000
Block Assembly: BABS
Allies:  Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni
Enemies: Dotty Parker
Fashioned in the shape of the ancient Saturn V moon rocket 
(but a taller, wider, more bloated version of it), Buzz Aldrin 
block mistakenly believes itself to be foremost of all the 
Sector 13 blocks purely due to the fact that the man it is 
named for was one of the ﬁ rst men to visit the Moon and is 
thus a national hero. Clearly such an auspicious individual is 
watching over a city block named for him and so the Buzzers 
consider themselves superior, in just about every respect, to 
the other six blocks.
Naturally enough this causes constant tension and 
resentment. In the Block Mania before the Apocalypse 
War, Buzz Aldrin block took-on all six of the other city 
blocks simultaneously. Unsurprisingly, it came-off the worst, 
especially against the vindictive dirty tricks of Dotty Parker 
block but the Block Mania defeat has done nothing to dent 
the Buzzers’ arrogance. The block likes to brag that at least 
it had the guts to take-on all comers alone and it took six 
– count ‘em, SIX – other blocks to bring it down.
Buzz Aldrin block is close to Dotty Parker and Jimmy 
Gandolﬁ ni blocks. Its natural enemy is Dotty Parker and the 
last full-scale Block War (which resulted in 5,000 arrests 
and 800 dead, just in Buzz Aldrin alone) was ﬁ ve years 
ago. Dotty Parker came off the worst in that conﬂ ict, with six 
luxury ﬂ oors wiped-out completely by a baby tactical nuke the 
Buzzers’ Citi-Def procured from somewhere. The bitterness 
still lingers between the two blocks but Buzz Aldrin block 
has been careful to display its co-operative credentials in 
recent years as it does not want heavy Justice Department 
reprisals lodging against it.

===PAGE 260===
 Sector 13
259
Relations between Buzz Aldrin and Jimmy Gandolﬁ ni blocks 
are cordial – almost friendly; but that is likely because Jimmy 
Gandolﬁ ni block has had its own spats with Dotty Parker in 
the past couple of years and because Buzz Aldrin needs all 
the support it can get.
The block is controlled by the BABS – Buzz Aldrin Block 
Syndicate, a bunch of semi-insane, petty power brokers who 
self-righteously believe they understand the will of the block’s 
35,000 citizens. although if truth be told they do not even 
understand how to secure their own kneepads. The BABS 
are all wealthy (occupying ﬂ oors AD to AA), patronising and 
hopelessly misguided. They operate under the impression 
that wealth and status equate to wisdom and political 
acuity; unfortunately, they have had considerable success 
in conveying that impression to the block’s citizens through 
the rather cunning machinations of the BABS spin doctor, 
Spiral J Ague, the silver-tongued spokesman for the group 
and all-round creep. Through deft rumour-mongering Ague 
has convinced most residents of how much BABS have the 
block’s interests at heart and how Dotty Parker block is the 
root cause of any woes the Buzzers might be suffering. His 
daily vid show, broadcast over the block’s internal newscast 
service, is called ‘Your Pal Spiral’ and he patronises the 
Buzz Aldrin populace daily with stories about the untiring 
efforts of BABS members on Buzzer citizens’ behalf. The 
show gains record viewing ﬁ gures but only because all other 
programming is blocked for the two hours ‘Your Pal Spiral’ 
lasts. Unbeknown to the members of BABS and the rest of 
the block, Spiral J Ague is a serial peeper with a vast array of 
listening devices and cameras planted throughout the block. 
He has a secret listening station in the sub-levels of the 
block where he goes to catch-up on his favourite recordings 
of the rich, famous and scandalous – all to feed his prime-
time show.
Jon Bon Jovi Block
Population: 42,000
Block Assembly: Lost Highwaymen
Allies:  Zeb Pike
Enemies: Bruce Springsteen
One of the two ‘Rock Blocks’ (the other being Bruce 
Springsteen block), Jon Bon Jovi (or JBJ) block is enthralled 
to the legend that was the 20
th Century’s foremost rock-god-
superstar, Jon Bon Jovi who, legend says, was born and 
raised in the old city that lies beneath JBJ’s foundations (a 
pack of complete lies but who’s to argue?).
JBJ block is stuck in a groove. The holy music of Jon Bon 
Jovi is pumped through every corridor, plaza and apartment 
ﬂ oor in the block, in a never-ending loop. Images of the Great 
Man himself are everywhere and, hanging in pride of place 
in Jonny B Plaza, encased in transparasteen, is Jonny B’s 
six string, once touched by the Great Man’s own hand and 
the most prized possession of the block.
Hated enemy of JBJ is Bruce Springsteen block, which 
claims that Bruce, not Jon, is the REAL god of rock and 
continually point out that, when alive, Brucie was known as 
The Boss, thereby sealing his credentials. JBJ refutes this, 
pointing out that JBJ inspired billions with his hairstyle and 
single-handedly brought about world peace with his iconic 
song ‘Keep The Faith’ (JBJ’s block anthem). The most 
fanatical residents of JBJ – which is to say, most of them 
– affect versions of Jon’s hairstyle; the bigger, shaggier 
and longer the better. Others wear trademark synthi-leather 
pants, synthi-denim waistcoats and cowboy hats. Big hair 
and hats are in; kneepads and pudding-bowl haircuts are 
out. At least, in JBJ block. The hatred between JBJ and 
Springsteen is formidable, especially as the two blocks face 
each other over E Street Plaza and the two blocks enjoy 
sporadic Block Wars fought not with guns but with their 
respective rock god’s songs, played at mega volume from 
specially designed external loudspeakers. That is, until the 
Judges serve a noise-abatement order, arrests are made 
and all goes quiet until the next time.
The block assembly is known as the Lost Highwaymen; 
those who are the most devout of the Bonjovites (as the 
cult of Jon Bon Jovi calls itself) and they call for holy rock 
war against all ‘false’ rock and the kind of tripe preferred 
by the Springsteen blockers. The Lost Highwaymen are 
a laid-back, slow drawling crowd with the biggest hair 
and highest Stetsons in the whole block. Reasonably 
democratic, occasionally concerned with wider affairs, the 
Lost Highwaymen are led by High Priest Ricardo Sambora 
(real name: Gurney Felps) and his wife Reeta. The two are 
indistinguishable as they wear their hair in identical, shaggy, 
Jonny styles and wear identical waistcoats, pants, chaps and 
hats. The two care only to see the Great Man honoured and 
his music live forever. Just like them. Both are Stookie Gland 
users obtaining their supplies from the Stookie Glanders in 
Jimmy Gandolﬁ ni block, plundering the block’s ﬁ nances to 
fund their addiction.
Beholden to Ricardo and Reeta is the Slippery ‘n’ Wet juve 
gang, which seeks to control the lower levels of the block 
and the E Street Plaza. The gang numbers some 500 juves 
and membership is dependent on every member knowing 
every lyric to every Jon song ever written (not hard, given the 
24/7 air-time his recordings get). Unlike Ricardo and Reeta, 
the Slipperies are a violent bunch of juvenile sociopaths who 
are desperate for a decent, old-fashioned, guns and missiles 
Block War with their enemies, the E Street Gang, over in 
Springsteen. The gang leader, Teeko Tortez, did ﬁ ve in the 

===PAGE 261===
 Sector 13
260
juve cubes for aggravated assault and emerged with near 
hero-status. He watches Ricardo and Reeta with interest, 
suspecting they have something to hide and intending to use 
it, one day, to his advantage.
Lloyd H Conover Block
Population: 38,000
Block Assembly: LHC Block Rep Committee
Allies:  None
Enemies: Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni
Lloyd H Conover block has the dubious reputation of having 
the lowest crime rate in the whole of Sector 13; dubious, 
because, as everyone in Sector 13 knows, everyone in LHC 
is an inveterate liar. Street gossip has it that LHC block lies 
about everything: it has the wealthiest citizens (disputed by 
Dotty Parker), it has the biggest dramarena (Springsteen’s is 
bigger and has a better PA), it has the highest employment 
(nope; Gandol ﬁ ni’s is a staggering 1.5% – double LHC’s) 
and it was the winner of the Block War it fought against 
Dotty Parker (so how come Judges arrested more LHC 
combatants than Dotty’s?). Lies. LHC residents perpetrate 
them every time they open their mouths.
There is no doubting that LHC has one thing that is undisputed: 
the highest rate of Justice Department sanctioned snitchers 
and peepers. Justice Department relies on LHC for around 
5% of its tip-offs and there is an entire ﬂ oor (level C) that 
consists of residents who do nothing all day but train 
telescopes, monoculars and bugs on the streets and plaza 
surrounding their block and snitch to the Sector House to 
their heart’s content.
Consequently, Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni block hates LHC with a 
vengeance. Everyone in Sector 13 dislikes Buzz Aldrin’s 
arrogance but at least it has the guts to admit what it is 
and stands up for itself. LHC, on the other hand, skulks 
and snitches. And, because much of that snitching impacts 
on Gandolﬁ ni’s business interests, LHC is hated by Jimmy 
G with unbridled passion. Block war between LHC and 
Gandolﬁ ni has been threatened for a long time but its an 
open secret that a war between the two would bring down 
the Judges harder on Gandolﬁ ni and, for a variety of reasons, 
Gandolﬁ ni does not want Justice Department Crime Blitzing 
its apartments. So Block War between LHC and Gandol ﬁ ni 
remains a hotly anticipated rumour, rather than becoming a 
reality.
Head of LHC’s Block Rep Committee is ‘Justice’ Radley 
Paglett. Paglett was expelled from the Academy of Justice in 
his ﬁ nal term for cowardice during an Applied Violence study 
project but he has embellished his history to make out that 
he graduated to Full Eagle and was given an honourable 
discharge after being seriously wounded foiling a heist in 
Sector 97. He plays heavily on his training and knowledge as 
a Judge and stops just short of running a jimping charge. He 
considers it his duty to assist the Sector House in keeping 
Sector 13 clean and this is why he has managed to turn his 
block into a band of informants. The rest of Block Committee 
is formed of like-minded citizens who lament the levels of 
crime in their Sector. All of them enjoy the power and prestige 
snitching to the Sector House has brought them: LHC enjoys 
its own Judge patrol and numerous civic awards grace the 
walls of the mall thoroughfares citing LHC’s co-operation in 
ending one racket or another.
But LHC has its own dirty little secrets. Gandolﬁ ni block has 
inﬁ ltrated LHC with its own wise guys and they snitch on 
the snitchers – ensuring Gandol ﬁ ni stays one step ahead 
of the game. It has its own people in the LHC juve gang, 
the Orange Boys, and they have been pushing the gang’s 
boundaries steadily and craftily for a couple of years, urging 
them on to ever greater offences. Justice Radley Paglett 
would clamp down on the Orange boys if his own, beloved 
son (rejected by the Academy of Justice) were not running 
with them. And so the LHC Block Rep Committee turns a 
blind eye to the gang’s scrawling, tapping and other, petty 
crimes, because although snitching is LHC’s business, one 
does not snitch on one’s own: a maxim Gandolﬁ ni is looking 
to seriously exploit when the time is right.
Jimmy Gandolfini Block
Population: 27,000
Block Assembly: JG Choir Assembly
Allies:  Buzz Aldrin
Enemies: Lloyd H Conover
The real rotten apple in the Sector 13 barrel, Jimmy Gandolﬁ ni 
is run by the Contralto Crew, the sector’s largest criminal 
gang. At the head of the crew is Max Contralto, overweight, 
lugubrious but with a sharp brain and a history of violence. 
Max is smart; just as his predecessors, Junior, Angio, Luigi 
and Antonelli were smart. The Crew has ensured that all 
its rackets, scams and crimes are insulated from the block 
through elaborate, painstakingly crafted shell companies, 
front organisations (who do ‘charitable works’, sometimes 
with las-cutters) and well-placed bribes. The Judges know 
the Contraltos are rotten; but there is insufﬁ cient evidence to 
pull-down the head men – Max and his cousins (and capos) 
Paulie, Stevie, Gus and Kris.
The Contraltos ensure that Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni block works: 
both in terms of efﬁ ciency and in terms of employment. The 
Crew runs the labour centres for Eustace Fargo spaceport 
and around 400 of the spaceport’s human workers are 
from Gandol ﬁ ni. These lucky citizens therefore serve the 

===PAGE 262===
 Sector 13
261
Crew’s interests at the spaceport, assisting in rackets like 
perp running, chump-dumping, Stookie Glanding, arms 
importation, illegal substance importation and all manner of 
other crimes that get lost in the morass of a busy, inefﬁ cient, 
spaceport hub. In terms of ef ﬁ ciency, Gandol ﬁ ni shuns 
robots and uses human staff to do the routine maintenance, 
again, buying-in favours for the Crew but ensuring that the 
block has no need to attract outside attention and municipal 
repair teams, like the other blocks. Jimmy Gandolﬁ ni block is 
well run, clean, tidy and as corrupt as hell.
Everyone in Gandol ﬁ ni lives in fear of Max Contralto and 
his Crew but respect his business methods. Petty crime of 
the type found in the other blocks is low – the Crew sees to 
that through beating the spug out of any upstart juve who 
thinks he can take advantage. Max redistributes his pro ﬁ ts 
occasionally to supplement welfare payments. Only last 
year, young Donny Osmosis, known for his wonderful singing 
voice, was sponsored by Max to the tune of Cr. 1.5 million at 
the suave Mega Academy of Music and Dance. Donny will 
go on to be a major singing star and he will owe it all to his 
Uncle Max. Gee, Max Contralto has a heart of gold!
The ﬂ y in the ointment is Lloyd H Conover block. Liars 
and informants, LHC is constantly trying to dig the dirt on 
Contralto’s extensive business interests. The Judges bust a 
racket every now an again and send some Crew employees 
to the cubes for a spell and it is all down to LHC sticking its 
nose into things that do not concern it. The Crew lets these 
little busts happen; it diverts attention from the big, money 
spinning rackets like Stookie Glanding and perp running. It 
lets the Sector House believe it has the Contraltos under 
control but really it has nothing of the sort. The network of 
interests, fronts, shell companies and so on means that 
unravelling the Contralto crime empire will take years and 
can be slowed down considerably through the appeals 
process. The legal ﬁ rm (based in Gandol ﬁ ni obviously but 
with branches across the sector) of Hagen, Petrocelli 
and Daaz represents the Contraltos and is highly effective 
at stymieing the wheels of justice. Thus, Max Contralto gets 
away with it.
But there is something else too. Something only the Contralto 
heads know and one other person. During the Apocalypse 
War, when the East-Meg troops invaded Sector 13, the 
Contraltos were amongst the ﬁ rst to shake-off Block Mania 
and ﬁ ght back against the invader. Contralto Crew foot 
soldiers stood side by side with the Judges of Sector 13 and 
fought the Sov Bloc invaders as ﬁ ercely as they could. Sector 
13 held out because of the Contraltos. Leading the Justice 
Department resistance at the time was Judge Hal DiMaggio. 
Junior Contralto headed the crew back then and he saved 
DiMaggio’s life. DiMaggio never forgot that; and so, when 
he became Sector Chief of 13, he deliberately decided to go 
easier on Gandol ﬁ ni block than another Sector Chief might 
have done. Max Contralto keeps this unspoken bond, this 

===PAGE 263===
 Sector 13
262
debt of honour, a secret from everyone save those who have 
a right to know. He does not, exactly, have Chief DiMaggio 
in his pocket but there is an in ﬂ uence there; something that 
works between Gandolﬁ ni and the Justice Department.
LHC can snitch all it wants. Sure, Contralto interests will 
get busted and men will go down; but Max has a Sector 
Chief’s leniency on his side – and that is worth keeping silent 
about.
Dotty Parker Block
Population: 50,000
Block Assembly: DP Representatives Forum
Allies:  Bruce Springsteen
Enemies: Buzz Aldrin
An old, proud block, resembling a malformed gherkin atop a 
slice of lumpy rye bread, Dotty Parker is the most populous 
of the Sector 13 blocks and every bit as aloof as its enemy, 
Buzz Aldrin. In most respects, Dotty Parker is an average 
city block: over-populated, under-employed, over-zealous 
and ﬁ lled with a sense of its own importance. Its greatest 
claim to fame is that S-13VB Studios are located on the mid-
level of the block and it broadcasts to the whole of Sector 13 
and beyond. Run by the formidable Agnes Lamprey (who is 
also president-for-life of the DP Reps Forum), its vid output 
is staggering: hit shows like ‘Swap My Spouse!’, ‘Never Mind 
the Hitchcocks’, ‘Guthrie’s Comedy Die-In’ and ‘Resyk Blues’ 
are syndicated city wide and it boasts a vault-full of Meggies 
(Mega-City One vid show awards) in all major categories.
Its sworn enemy is Buzz Aldrin block. Dotty Parker hates the 
Buzzers’ arrogance but what sparked the feud was a block-
embargo Buzz Aldrin imposed over a decade ago when 
S-13VB broadcast its infamous ‘Blocks Exposed!’ show (it 
won 24 Meggies, including ‘Best Violence’, ‘Best Editing’ 
and ‘Best Voiceover – Human’ categories) which centred 
completely on Buzz Aldrin block. It did not cast the block in 
a good light and that led to enmity. Buzz Aldrin embargoed 
all SB-13VB broadcasts for an entire year, thereby earning 
the wrath of Agnes Lamprey and consequently the whole of 
Dotty Parker block.
Agnes Lamprey controls Dotty Parker block like a personal 
ﬁ efdom. Prestigious jobs in her studios go to those whom she 
favours – and she does not necessarily favour competence. 
The DP Reps Forum merely acts on her instructions and the 
residents of Dotty Parker have zero say in the block’s affairs. 
Agnes rules the roost from her summit suite penthouse 
(occupying an entire double-letter level) and there she 
entertains the great, the good and the popular from across 
Mega-City One. Her parties are legendary, attended by vid-
stars, music stars, artists, fashion designers, politicos and 
inﬂ uential of ﬁ cials. When Agnes Lamprey announces a 
party, it is hot gossip and selected highlights are broadcast 
in ‘Party Nite with Agnes’, one of her station’s most highly 
rated shows.
Beneath her, the block steadily crumbles. It has come out 
worst in the Block Wars it has fought and so much money is 
diverted to the colossal costs of running the studio that block 
maintenance is thin on the ground. As an older block that 
sustained a couple of missile hits in the Apocalypse War, the 
building’s state of repair is grim and level C-West completely 
collapsed, killing 800 residents, ﬁ ve years ago. The whole 
catastrophe was blamed on Buzz Aldrin insurgents (triggering 
a minor war) and a vide-thon raised MCr. 18 to have the wing 
rebuilt (only MCr. 6 made it into the rebuild coffers; the rest 
went into making the spectacular, 900 episode epic ‘Gone 
Like the Wind’, made by – you guessed it – S-13VB).
But Dotty Parker’s residents are in awe of Agnes Lamprey. 
Mega-City One celebrities are always visiting the block as 
part of their studio conrtacts and Agnes parades them up 
and down the levels on ‘Celeb Sweeps’, allowing the great, 
DP unwashed, to almost shake the hands of their heroes and 
heroines. The block loves Agnes and so its appalling state 
of repair and simmering feud with Buzz Aldrin, is completely 
forgiven – as long as the block continues to make great, 
Meggie-award winning broadcasts and the celebs continue 
to tramp through the block’s corridors.
Naturally, Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni’s Crew has people inside S-
13VB, pulling strings and scams here and there. Repair 
contracts get awarded to Crew construction companies, 
although they do just enough to make it look like an efﬁ cient 
job and not quite enough to ensure that, six months later, 
they need to come back to do more work. Elsewhere, Buzz 
Aldrin has its own people inside Dotty Parker, looking for 
ways to goad an all-out Block War but to no avail thus far.
And Agnes Lamprey and her dirty little secret? Well, she owes 
a great deal of her in ﬂ uence to the demoness Gorgonessa, 
a vile creature brought through the dimensional gate during 
Necropolis that has managed, somehow, to remain, with 
the help of Agnes Lamprey’s wretched psionic niece, 
Letitia. Gorgonessa inhabits an ancient urn that forms part 
of Agnes’s treasure haul and it offers advice and a certain 
amount of psychic and sorcerous help that ensures S-13VB 
continues to win awards and attract the biggest stars. All 
Agnes and Letitia need to do is feed Gorgonessa a fresh, 
human soul every month and the demoness is happy. 
With an over-populated block beneath her and plenty of 
sycophantic fools aching to gain Agnes’s favour, keeping 

===PAGE 264===
 Sector 13
263
Gorgonessa happy is an easy task. And the sacri ﬁ ces are 
always carefully chosen by Letitia to ensure they will never 
be missed…
Zeb Pike Block
Population: 33,000
Block Assembly: Zeb Citizens’ Forum
Allies:  Jon Bon Jovi
Enemies: Bruce Springsteen
A sparkling new block, built on the ruins of the old, Norm 
Schwarzkopf block (tac-nuked during the Apocalypse War), 
Zeb Pike houses the Mega-City One Central Memorial 
Hospital, a state-of-the-art medical facility that acts as the 
main hospital for the Eustace Fargo spaceport. The hospital 
occupies all 102 ﬂ oors of the east wing whilst the residential 
west wing houses the populace.
Because of the hospital, Zeb Pike is careful to stay out of 
Block Wars, although that does not stop it from allying with 
Jon Bon Jovi block against Bruce Springsteen. It is difﬁ cult to 
discern the motive for this alliance but it exists nonetheless. 
It may be down to the fact that when the block was built 
some Jon Bon Jovi overspill was allocated to Zeb Pike and 
old allegiances just die hard.
Being such a recent block, Zeb Pike is designed to be 
maintenance-free and has some of the most advanced 
automated maintenance facilities of any block within a 
hundred sectors. The hospital’s maintenance facilities are 
part of the overall block circuitry and so the residential wing 
beneﬁ ts from ﬁ rst-rate cleaning robotic cleaning and repair 
facilities as well as assured power, with back-up, 24/7. 
Apartment rents are higher in Zeb Pike than the other blocks 
and so it attracts wealthier citizens but for the most part the 
block is as normal as one can get for a Mega-City One city 
block. It has all mod cons, including a Palais-de-Boing® and 
this makes Lloyd H Conover block exceedingly jealous.
The Zeb Citizens’ Forum is a real, honest-to-goodness 
democracy, with free votes and representative rights. 
Elections are held biannually and any resident is eligible to 
stand. Current leader of the forum is Elmer Fudd Gantry, 
leader of the K-Level Party and once a pro-democracy 
supporter and veteran of the Grand Hall of Justice march 
back in 2109. His views have tempered somewhat and 
he now has no issue with Justice Department control of 
the city but for local representation he is a true democrat 
and believer in fair-play/one-citizen, one-vote politics. The 
K-Level party is moderate politically and ensures that the 
enmity with Bruce Springsteen is kept at bay, although the 
E-Level Party, which consists of old Jon Bon Jovi overspill, 
opposes this line vociferously and agitates for action against 
the so-called Boss Block. Thankfully, K-level Party has the 
support of the F, G, M and Q level parties and this keeps 
E-Level on the… level.
The hospital covers all major treatments and has some 
of the ﬁ  nest human surgeons in the world. This includes 
Doctor Hyram Foster-Gloster, a prominent plastic surgeon 
who specialises in respiratory system repair, replacement 
and enhancement – a key skill given the number of aliens 
who come into Sector 13 via the spaceport. Also well 
known amongst the hospital staff is Doctor Doug Klooney, a 
heartthrob obstetrician and part-time vid star who appeared 
in the hit S-13VBC production ‘Meg ER’, playing himself. 
Plenty of women, from all over Mega-City One, have either 
deliberately got themselves pregnant or brought their off-
spring into the hospital, just to be treated by the rugged, 
handsome, vain, wealthy, single, Doctor Doug. Some 
women have even come into the hospital in the hope of 
getting pregnant by him – and on at least six occasions 
Judges have had to break-up ﬁ ghts between rabid Doug-
Fans (or Duggies) who all claim he agreed to marry them or 
impregnate them or even just give them a peck on the cheek. 
As a result, Doctor Doug is under caution by Sector House 
not to encourage fan behaviour or to face a Contributing to 
Anti-Social Behaviour charge. So Doctor Doug keeps a low 
proﬁ le, whilst a growing, makeshift settlement of all-female 
Duggies has established itself in Kildare Plaza, outside Zeb 
Pike block. Doctor Doug is now a prisoner of his own block 
and, such is the fanaticism of the Duggies, he has over ﬁ fty 
unrepentant stalkers.
Bruce Springsteen Block
Population: 29,000
Block Assembly: The E Street Band
Allies:  Dotty Parker
Enemies: Jon Bon Jovi, Zeb Pike
Second of the Rock Blocks, Bruce Springsteen block is a 
parody of a guitar-shape and proudly ﬂ ies, at the very top of 
its Citi-Def emplacement, a massive ﬂ ag emblazoned with the 
words ‘The Boss Lives’, in abject de ﬁ ance of neighbouring 
Jon Bon Jovi block across E Street Plaza.
Like JBJ, Springsteen is named for the rock legend of 
the 20
th Century born in this vicinity of the Old City. It is a 
working-class block (a contradiction in terms; less than 0.5% 
of its citizens have a job) and, like JBJ, the ancient old rocker 
forms the basis of the E Street Cult – those who worship 
Brucie’s music, scathing lyrical attacks on the old culture 
of America and diehard left-leaning patriotism. The cult, of 
which all residents are honorary members whether they 

===PAGE 265===
 Sector 13
264
like it or not, claims to own Springsteen’s bones, which are 
locked away in the block’s central vaults. Indeed, a skeleton 
is there but no one can prove if the bones belonged to the 
Boss or not. The most valued residents are allowed into the 
vault to gaze upon the Great Man’s skeleton, which is formed 
into a famous pose (one arm leaning on his sax-honking 
bodyguard, Clarry Clemons, guitar round his shoulders at 
a rock-god angle) from one of Springsteen’s most famous 
audio recordings. Touching is strictly forbidden: only direct 
descendents of the Boss may go anywhere near the statue 
and, although many in the block claim such heritage, none 
can prove it. Owning old Springsteen CDs is not, in itself, 
proof of lineage.
The block is run on semi-democratic lines by the E Street 
Band, an elected council that is meant to represent each 
level of the block but, in reality, represents only those who are 
true Boss fanatics. Band members are recognisable by their 
ripped jeans, checked shirts and plasteen-capped labourer’s 
boots. In truth, the E Street Band runs the block reasonably 
well and does not, thankfully, insist on Springsteen songs 
being piped through the building morning, noon and night 
– as does JBJ. In fact, any practice JBJ block adopts, 
Springsteen rejects just to be different. The amenities of the 
block are named for the old E Street band (Brucie’s backing 
band, not the council) members: so Stevie Van Zandt 
Shopping Plaza dominates the lower stories and the Clarry 
Clemons Parkarama graces the basement section. The Nils 
Lofgren Zoomtube station is around halfway up the block 
and it curves away over the plaza to join with the Sector 13 
main zoomtube about half a kilometre away.
Hatred between Springsteen and Jon Bon Jovi block is well 
known and the Springsteeners revel in the rivalry every bit 
as much as the Bonjovites across the plaza. Head of the 
E Street Band (the council, not Brucie’s backing band) is 
Waynetta Sconce, the young (thank-you Nu-You face clinics 
and rejuve!), attractive, ﬂ irtatious daughter of the old head, 
Wayne Sconce. Waynetta is waiting for JBJ to provoke all-
out Block War but does not intend making the ﬁ rst move. It 
is not something Brucie would have done, back in the old 
days and so neither will his name-sake block. Instead, she 
is quite content to sanction the E Street juve gang to harass 
the Slippery ‘n’ Wets, provoking them into increasingly bitter 
turf wars, fought when the Judges are not looking.
The E Street Juves are fascinated by cars and inspired by 
the Boss’s ﬁ nest lyrics on his seminal, apocryphal album 
‘Born to Run’. Using Clarry Clemons Parkarama as their 
base, the E Street Juves prep their old, petrol-burning 
suicide machines in their spring-cages. At night, when the 
trafﬁ c ebbs a little, the gang members rev their engines and 
burst out from the cages and onto the Kev Spacey Intersect 
to partake in ‘Last-Chance Power Drives’, at high speed, 
down Meryl Streep skedway, through the Patty McBride 
underzoom and then onto Budd Abbott Megway. As this 
route takes the juves around the edge of E Street Plaza, 
close to where the Slippery ‘n’ Wets hang-out, the E Street 
Juves take the opportunity for a little drive-by shooting and 
hoping that there is no Lawmaster parked on the Justice 
Ramp a little further up the sked. To date, ﬁ fteen innocent 
citizens have been injured or killed by these Power Drives 
and Chief DiMaggio at Sector House has ordered a clean 
up of the gang to prevent more maiming and death. But 
the resources of Justice Department are stretched thinly 
and Judges cannot be everywhere at once: the clean up 
operation (Codename: Nebraska) is taking its time.
Springsteen block enjoys an alliance with Dotty Parker block 
owing to assistance given during the Apocalypse War when 
Sector 13’s blocks turned on Buzz Aldrin. The alliance is 
fragile but still present and Waynetta Sconce enjoys the 
patronage of Agnes Lamprey, having attended several of 
her parties. Waynetta has also sent several of her people 
over to meet with Agnes on various occasions and has 
wondered why they never decided to return. These people 
were promised jobs in S-13VBC studios, so they probably 
have jobs now, as grips or Best Boys – whatever it is Agnes 
had planned for them. It is good that blocks can co-operate, 
Waynetta believes; and it is just a shame that Jon Bon Jovi 
block cannot acknowledge that the real King of Rock was the 
man they called The Boss and not some long-haired proto-
metallist who preferred spandex to good, hard-wearing 
synthi-denim.
Sector House 
13
A ﬁ fty-storey fortiﬁ ed bubble, vaguely resembling a Justice 
Department helmet, Sector House 13 is located to the north 
the sector near the intersection between the Aaron Burr and 
Davey Copperﬁ eld Megways. The original Sector House 
was smaller but it suffered during the Apocalypse War and 
was rebuilt into its current form shortly after. The Sector 
House is well equipped, with Tek, Med and Psi Divisions all 
represented to a reasonable degree. The sub-level garages 
house a Manta Prowl tank and the upper level hangar 
houses three H Wagons that are detailed to cover the whole 
of Sector 13 and provide back up to Sectors 11 and 12, as 
needed. The Med Division medbays are as well-equipped 
as those of the Mega-City One Central Memorial Hospital, 
over at Zeb Pike and provide relief med facilities for Sectors 
11 and 12 also.

===PAGE 266===
 Sector 13
265
In the shadow of Sector House 13 is Iso Block 911, which 
houses perps doing stretches of between eight and ﬁ fteen 
years. Next to it is Psycho Block 101, which holds mid-level 
kooks who can (or should) be curable within two to four 
years of referral. Sector House 13 provides Judges on guard 
detail for both facilities.
Sector Chief Hal DiMaggio has been in charge of the Sector 
House for nine years but was a senior Judge in the sector 
before that and led the resistance during the Apocalypse 
War. He is pushing 60 years old now and the Judges reckon 
he will opt for the Long Walk in the next three to ﬁ ve years. 
Gruff, grey-haired and pock-marked, he is every bit an old 
school Judge who worked with Dredd on several occasions 
and, as a young Street Judge, did not shrink in awe and fear 
of Chief Judge Cal. DiMaggio is highly anti-mutant, believing 
that the relaxation of the Mutant Laws was a huge mistake 
for all concerned. His ofﬁ cers are under instructions to come 
down hard on any muties who ﬂ out the law in his sector. He 
is equally tough on the growing drug problem, exacerbated 
by the sector’s proximity to the Eustace Fargo spaceport 
and an in ﬂ ux of off-world chemicals that, whilst not strictly 
illegal, have contributed to rising crime levels amongst the 
juve gangs of the lower levels of the sector and in some 
of the blocks. Of less concern to him is organised crime. 
There has not been a Mob War since before Max Contralto 
became head of the Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni Crew and even the 
murderous Azbury Park Brotherhood, the Contralto Crew’s 
nearest rivals, have remained low-key in recent years. This 
allows DiMaggio to focus on controlling the drugs and mutie 
problems that he believes are at the heart of Sector 13’s 
crime statistics.
DiMaggio takes a personal interest in the careers of all his 
Judges. Sector 13 has ﬁ ve hundred Street Judges, one 
hundred Tek, eighty Med and seven Psi Judges. He insists 
on monthly ﬁ tness assessments and reports, co-ordinated 
by his Med team and he likes to personally brief all Judges 
at least once a week instead of relying on machines or 
other Senior Judges to do it. His memory is phenomenal: 
he knows the name and arrest record of every Judge in his 
sector and is both quick to praise and admonish as circumstances dictate. Any Judge who screws-up is summoned for one of 
his infamous ‘Helmet Dentings’ where he directs his gruff, thick, old New York accent in a criticism-laden barrage against the 
hapless miscreant in a stream so hard, it can dent a helmet. Conversely, when a Judge performs well and cracks a major or 
difﬁ cult case, he is personally overjoyed to hand out a citation in front of all the other Judges, even if the recipient had a Helmet 
Denting that same morning.
DiMaggio is supported by Deputy Chief Abi Dryden. Dryden heads-up Psi Division in Sector 13 and is one of Justice Department’s 
best empaths. She understands DiMaggio perfectly, having worked with him for almost thirteen years and her approach mirrors 
his: attention to detail and a genuine concern for her Judges. But, as an empath, Dryden is easier to get along with, tailors her 
mood to those she is talking to and manages to place everyone at ease. She still walks rounds at Psyko Block 101, helping to 
treat the inmates there and occasionally gets summoned to Grand Hall to assist with high-pro ﬁ le Psi cases. Unlike DiMaggio, 
Dryden is worried about organised crime. Her best precogs have all reported premonitions of some form of power-struggle 

===PAGE 267===
 Sector 13
266
accommodation in the ‘Saturn Towers’, off the junction 
of Scala and Stieglitz skedways. There are no known 
bodysnatchers or organleggers at work in Sector 13 and the 
disappearances could be coincidence but DiMaggio wants 
them investigated.
Tapping Rise
A possible tapping gang working the lower pedways of 
Lindbergh sub-district. The pedways are a favourite shortcut 
to and from Fargo Spaceport. The tappers use old-fashioned 
bolt-cutters to threaten their marks, which indicates someone 
with access to 20
th Century tools.
Sector 13 Festival
Held every year and centred on Alf Kinsey ped-plaza, 
largest of the open plazas in the sector. The festival attracts 
all manner of craze fanatics, simps, uglies, punks and 
neuromantics who come to listen to the local bands who 
play the temporary festival arena. Psi Division predicts 
trouble between Springsteen and Bonjovites this year – 81% 
chance of it happening – so Judge presence will be larger 
than usual this year.
Mopad Piracy
A sharp rise in mopad piracy on and around Whitney 
Houston Mega-Circular and Norm Mailer Megway Intersect. 
The perps may have a fake Service Hov and be acting in 
response to home-shopping deliveries put out by mopad 
residents. There are no reports of stolen service Hovs in 
the sector but several from Sectors 14, 27, 108 and 224. 
Could be an itinerant gang. Either way, Sector 13 has been 
targeted.
Substance Running
Someone is funding drug runs through Fargo Spaceport. 
More drugs are hitting Sector 13 streets than ever before and 
DiMaggio wants this cracked. Someone at the spaceport is 
smoothing the way, which means someone is taking a cut of 
the proﬁ ts. Obvious suspects are those working for known 
Contralto Crew companies servicing the spaceport but they 
are, by no means, the only culprits.
 Eustace 
Fargo 
Spaceport
Eustace Fargo is located northwest of Sector 13 and borders 
Sector 10. The spaceport has three terminals – two for 
passenger services and one dedicated to commercial and 
cargo services. Each terminal has four take-off and landing 
zones, with each zone having three launch and landing 
between the organised crime groups in the not too distant 
future. Details are exceedingly hazy and the recurring motif 
of the premonitions is a full moon. DiMaggio listens to her 
concerns but dismisses them, noting that a 74.6% chance 
of accuracy is not a basis for going to war with the gangs. 
Dryden is subtly attempting to convince him otherwise.
Head of Tek Division is Tek Chief Hawking. A wizard with 
vehicles, he came to Sector 13 from the Q Branch of Tek 
Central at the Grand Hall of Justice. He worked on several 
Manta versions before transferring and is currently engaged 
in research on the vehicle pro ﬁ les for the highways around 
the sector. He wants to ﬁ nd a way of clamping down on the 
Last Chance Power Rides of the E Street Juves through the 
use of fully automated prowl cars instead of having to rely on 
Street Judge patrols. The scheme needs funding and, if his 
developments meet with GHoJ approval, then he can put his 
plans into production using the Sector House garages and 
armoury.
Head of Med Division is Med Chief Holby. A specialist in 
off-world diseases and chemicals, it is his team that has 
researched and classi ﬁ ed the various off-world narcotics 
coming through Fargo Spaceport. Three in particular are 
of real interest and all originate in the Qux system. One, 
‘Glean’, is a combat stimulant; not illegal, as it is also an 
anaesthetic but it does have certain emotion-suppressing 
properties. The second, ‘Flare’, is mildly hallucinogenic 
but no more so than many legal stimulants; instead, Holby 
believes it is linked to Petty Crime Addiction, relaxing the 
inhibitions of its users and increasing petty crime propensity. 
The third, ‘Cake’, is a little like Umpty Candy but clearly an 
independent concoction. It tastes damned ﬁ ne and has no 
known damaging side effects but is in such short supply 
that the usual drug-runners are selling small amounts of it 
alongside their usual stashes of zziz and ganja.
Sector House 13 has all the standard facilities of any other 
Sector House and plenty more. Street Judges bene ﬁ t from 
excellent resources and a diligent control room that covers 
most of the sector. Current trouble spots are:
Springsteen/Bon Jovi Block 
Relations
A series of Power Drives by E Street Juves left three 
Slipperies on their way to Resyk last month. DiMaggio wants 
the perps caught and slammed inside for 30 ASAP.
Missing Persons
Four unsolved MP reports in the past four months: 
two from Dotty Parker block and two spacers who had 
returned from an inter-system haul and found temporary 

===PAGE 268===
 Sector 13
267
pads. Terminal One supports direct landing and take-off 
spacecraft; small vessels capable of interplanetary and 
hyperdrive travel but which are also capable of atmospheric 
operation. Terminal Two supports shuttle services to the 
orbital docking platforms for the larger ships which cannot 
enter the atmosphere. Terminal Three is the commercial 
and cargo area and mixes both shuttle and direct services. 
Passenger and commercial/cargo are effectively autonomous 
operations although all the ﬂ ight and landing procedures are 
regulated from the Operational Control Services building.
The immense, twin geodesic domes of the main halls 
dominate the skyline for kilometres around, ﬂ anked by the 
huge, cantilevered hangar and maintenance buildings to 
either side. Zoomtube transits provide the main arteries with 
the surrounding sectors, as well as the sixteen-lane Angie 
Dickinson Megway. The sector districts around Fargo offer a 
mixture of the high class, middling-fare and low-rent in terms 
of accommodation, shopping and housing. Many of the 
residents work at the spaceport in blue collar facilities, whilst 
the transitory population is of the business and travelling 
class, en-route to off-world.
Eustace Fargo is one of the oldest non-military, commercial 
spaceports in operation. Upgraded in the early 21
st Century, 
it was once known as Newark International but over the 
next one hundred years was refashioned as space-faring 
technology developed, commercial space ﬂ ight became 
cheaper to sustain and cargo requirements were developed. 
In 2112 it was redesigned by George ‘Capability’ Puce, the 
famed architect behind several domed habitats, including 
New London (Brit-Cit) and New Carthage (Tunisia) in the 
early 2090s. He was responsible for the construction of the 
twin domes and the redesign of the space-side facilities 
and maintenance yards. Fargo retained its old airport-style 
infrastructure and feel but the passenger facilities were now 
in keeping with the dome habitat approach so familiar and 
comfortable for many in Mega-City One.
Terminal Three handles all cargo and trade ﬂ ights into and 
out of Fargo. The Cargo and Commerce Services hangar 
attached to Terminal Three provides full manifest reporting 
and checking facilities, with several teams of docking crews 
(all Contralto-controlled or owned) responsible for monitoring 
the cargo coming through the facility and getting it loaded 
and unloaded. Docking crews have the authority to open and 
check all cargo, irrespective of owner and destination. Most 
checks are performed with the latest scanning and detection 
apparatus but physical checks are conducted randomly or if 
suspicions are aroused. Judges maintain a visible presence 
here although all manifest checking is handled by the 
docking teams.
The terminal does not provide anything other than very 
short-term storage for inbound and outbound cargo; there 
is simply too much of it, so the facility is a constant through-
ﬂ ow of a bewildering array of goods and equipment. Where 
cargo storage is necessary, a short-term storage facility is 
offered to the east of the main services building but all cargo 
is subject to rigorous security checks before being stored and 
as it emerges from storage. There have been a few cases of 
stowaways bribing their way into the storage depot and so 
vital signs scans are conducted on each item as it enters and 
leaves. Stowaways who are caught are taken to the security 
centre for detention and questioning before being placed 
under arrest and handed over to the duty Judges.

===PAGE 269===
 Sector 13
268
Other Areas 
of Interest 
in Sector 13
Majestyk’s Bar and Grill 
(South End, E Street Plaza)
Run by the Majestyk family for over ﬁ fty years, the Bar and 
Grill offers good, simply prepared munce at ridiculously 
inﬂ ated prices – as do most of the food outlets in the E Street 
Plaza area. Pictures of the Majestyk family down the years 
line the walls and the current manager Gil Majestyk tends 
front of house whilst his twin sons Dominic and Dashford 
manage the bar. It specialises in synthi-beers from around the 
Sol system and beyond, boasting a range of ninety different 
types and strengths. ‘We Can Refresh Ya!’ is the tagline 
and it is the Bar and Grill’s sheer variety and the constant 
stream of curious customers that allow it to get away with the 
exorbitant prices it charges. Food is grilled munce and soy-
vegetables served in a variety of styles, although the chef, 
Montalban, has introduced several off-world specialities 
over the years. The atmosphere is friendly and convivial and 
the clientele eclectic. The Bar and Grill is popular with alien 
visitors and consequently the Majestyk’s maintain a healthy 
stock of food and beers to suit their palates.
Gil Majestyk is a front man for the Contraltos of Jimmy 
Gandolﬁ ni block and has contacts at Fargo spaceport 
Terminal Three. This means that he is able to receive and 
move certain contraband stocks on behalf of the Contralto 
Crew and on behalf of others. It takes years to earn Gil’s 
trust and thus make use of his services and contacts but if 
the price is right and discretion assured, Gil can handle it. 
If he is crossed, his friends in Jimmy Gandol ﬁ ni make sure 
retribution is swift, painful and conveying the right message. 
Gil Majestyk and his family are clever and carefully cover 
their trail, with considerable help from Max Contralto (who 
likes to eat at the restaurant). 
Rumsay’s (1004
th Street and 
Acres Pedway, Upper Level)
Considered somewhat passé but still popular, is the Brit-
Cit-based Ramsay food chain, specialising in ﬁ ne cuisine 
at affordable prices. The menu is typically 21
st Century 
specialties: over-presented fast food with ostentatious 
titles served with a scowl and an expletive. Mega-City One 
citizens delight in this collision of style and attitude, enjoying 
the howls and curses emanating from the kitchen area. A 
holograph of the long-dead proprietor, demonstrating the 
preparation of the restaurant’s signature dishes, plays across 
the numerous tri-dee displays but there is no masking the 
pretension of the place. For fast food, the quality is good 
and prices reasonable but there is no question over the un-
ambitious (yet remarkably quaint) approach to presentation.
eezyBed (Various franchises 
across Sector 13)
A self-service hotel, the cubicle rooms are for rent using a 
credit chip either by the hour, day or week. Each cubicle 
has a fold-down bed, table, washbasin, shower and 
communication facilities. Very popular with budget travellers 
going to, or coming from, Fargo Spaceport, it is affordable, 
no-frills comfort. The communication system has a direct 
link into the Geisha Bank, allowing guests to select intimate 
company according to their means and preferences. 
Naturally enough eezyBed (or Sleazybed, as most know 
it) has a seedy reputation but it provides a vital service 
considering the ridiculous prices charged by the likes of the 
Hyatt and other brand hotels.
The Geisha Bank (Illegal Adult 
Services)
The Geisha Bank describes itself as a ‘personal shopping and 
facilitation service for the busy commercial traveller’. For an 
appropriate hourly fee, one of the Bank’s attractive, skilled 
Personal Assistants will act as a personal shopper, a guide 
around the sector, a messenger or, as is most common but 
curiously never advertised, an escort. The Bank has over three 
hundred Personal Assistants of a variety of races on its books, 
all with impeccable security credentials, providing services to 
all classes of traveller (as long as they can pay). The business 
is entirely legal as long as it is conducted with discretion and 
the Geisha Bank’s owners, Jubal Masakara and Diana Wade, 
are never less than discrete. Naturally enough they learn a 
great deal about what is happening within the sector and 
elsewhere and have the good sense to offer their intelligence 
for sale in the right quarters, including the Sector House and 
the ever-curious Jimmy Gandolﬁ ni block.
